.

The Generalized Riemann Hypothesis is used to show that
axiom (
3.2
) above holds for sequences of interest to us
(i.e. smoothed indicator functions on the set of
w
-rough integers in
\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
.)
See Section
6
for details.

Where important, the dependence of implied constants on auxiliary parameters
will be indicated in subscripts i.e.
O_{\varepsilon,\xi,A,\ldots}
,
\ll_{\varepsilon,\xi,A,\ldots}
and
\gg_{\varepsilon,\xi,A,\ldots}
. It will be crucial to give the implied constants
of certain error terms in the proofs of Theorem
1.1

and Theorem
1.3

explicitly in terms of some of the auxiliary parameters. Such terms are clearly indicated.

4.
Poisson summation formula

We will need a minor variant of the number field Poisson summation formula.

Lemma 4.1
.

Let
V:\mathbb{R}^{2}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
be a smooth Schwartz function. By an abuse of notation, set
V(x+iy):=V(x,y)
. Then
we have

(4.1)

\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]}V(m)=\frac{2}{\sqrt{3}}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}V(x,y)\check{e}\Big(\frac{k(x+iy)}{\lambda}\Big)dxdy.

Proof.

Let
\Lambda:=\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
, viewed as a discrete lattice embedded in
\mathbb{C}
.
We identify
x+iy
with
(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
. Poisson summation gives

\sum_{\mathbf{x}\in\Lambda}V(\mathbf{x})=\frac{1}{\text{covol}(\Lambda)}\sum_{\mathbf{x}\in\Lambda^{\star}}\widehat{V}(\mathbf{x}),

where
\Lambda^{\star}=\lambda^{-1}\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
is the dual lattice to
\Lambda
,

\widehat{V}(\mathbf{x}):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}V(u,v)e(2(xu+vy))dudv,

and

\text{covol}(\Lambda)=\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}.

Observe that

\text{Re}[(x+iy)(u-iv)]=xu+yv.

Thus Poisson summation for
\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
is given by

(4.2)

\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]}V(m)=\frac{2}{\sqrt{3}}\sum_{k\in\lambda^{-1}\mathbb{Z}[\omega]}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}V(x,y)e(2\text{Re}(\overline{k}(x+iy)))dxdy.

We can replace
\overline{k}
by
k
in (
4.2
), since
\lambda^{-1}\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
is closed under conjugation.
Thus (
4.1
) holds, as required.
∎

Lemma 4.2
.

Let
0\neq q\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
,

\psi:\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
be a
q
-periodic function, and

V:\mathbb{R}^{2}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
be a smooth Schwartz function.
Then

\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]}\psi(m)V(m)=\frac{2}{\sqrt{3}N(q)}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]}\dot{\psi}(k)\dot{V}\Big(\frac{k}{q}\Big),

where

\dot{\psi}(k):=\sum_{t\pmod{q}}\psi(t)\check{e}\Big({-\frac{tk}{q\lambda}}\Big),

and

\dot{V}(u):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}V(x,y)\check{e}\Big(\frac{u(x+iy)}{\lambda}\Big)dxdy.

Remark 4.1
.

For each
t\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
, note that the additive character

k\mapsto\check{e}\Big({-\frac{tk}{3\lambda q}}\Big)

has minimal period
3q
(not
3\lambda q
).

Proof.

We have

\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]}\psi(m)V(m)=\sum_{t\pmod{q}}\psi(t)\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]}V(mq+t)

Application of Lemma
4.1
to the summation over
m
gives

\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]}V(mq+t)=\frac{2}{\sqrt{3}}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}V((x+iy)q+t)\check{e}\Big(\frac{k(x+iy)}{\lambda}\Big)dxdy.

A linear change of variable then shows that

(4.3)

\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]}V(mq+t)=\check{e}\Big({-\frac{kt}{q\lambda}}\Big)\frac{2}{\sqrt{3}N(q)}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}V(x,y)\check{e}\Big(\frac{k(x+iy)}{q\lambda}\Big)dxdy.

The result follows upon summing both sides of (
4.3
)
over
t\pmod{q}
with the
q
-periodic weights
\psi(t)
.
∎

We will specialise to the case where the test function is radially symmetric.

Lemma 4.3
.

Let
q\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
with
q\equiv 1\pmod{3}
,

\psi:\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
be a
q
-periodic function,
and
V:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
be a smooth Schwartz function.
Then for any
M>0
we have

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
m\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}\psi(m)V\Big(\frac{N(m)}{M}\Big)=\frac{4\pi M}{9\sqrt{3}N(q)}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]}\ddot{\psi}(k)\ddot{V}\Big(\frac{k\sqrt{M}}{q}\Big),

where

\ddot{\psi}(k):=\check{e}\Big({-\frac{k}{3\lambda}}\Big)\sum_{x\pmod{q}}\psi(3\lambda x)\check{e}\Big({-\frac{kx}{q}}\Big),

and

\ddot{V}:\mathbb{C}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
is defined by

(4.4)

\ddot{V}(u):=\int_{0}^{\infty}rV(r^{2})J_{0}\Big(\frac{4\pi r|u|}{3\sqrt{3}}\Big)dr.

Proof.

Application of Lemma
4.2
gives

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
m\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}\psi(m)V\Big(\frac{N(m)}{M}\Big)=\frac{1}{N(3q)}\frac{2}{\sqrt{3}}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]}\dot{\psi}(k)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}V\Big(\frac{x^{2}+y^{2}}{M}\Big)\check{e}\Big(\frac{k(x+iy)}{3\lambda q}\Big)dxdy,

where

(4.5)

\dot{\psi}(k)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}t\pmod{3q}\\
t\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}\psi(t)\check{e}\Big({-\frac{tk}{3\lambda q}}\Big).

We first simplify the integral. A change of variable gives

(4.6)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}V\Big(\frac{x^{2}+y^{2}}{M}\Big)\check{e}\Big(\frac{k(x+iy)}{3\lambda q}\Big)dxdy=M\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}V(x^{2}+y^{2})\check{e}\Big(\frac{k(x+iy)\sqrt{M}}{3\lambda q}\Big)dxdy.

We change
x+iy
to polar coordinates via the substitution
x+iy=re^{i\vartheta}
.
Let
\kappa\in[-\pi,\pi)
be a fixed angle (depending on
k
and
q
) such that

e^{-i\kappa}=\begin{cases}\frac{k}{|k|}\frac{|\lambda q|}{\lambda q}&\text{if}\quad k\neq 0\\
1&\text{if}\quad k=0.\end{cases}

Then (
4.6
) becomes

(4.7)

\displaystyle M\int_{0}^{\infty}rV(r^{2})\int_{0}^{2\pi}\check{e}\Big(\frac{re^{i\vartheta-i\kappa}|k|\sqrt{M}}{3\sqrt{3}|q|}\Big)d\vartheta dr.

We eliminate the translation by
\kappa
by a linear change of variable in
\vartheta
and the fact that the integrand is periodic with period
2\pi
.
Therefore (
4.7
) becomes

M\int_{0}^{\infty}rV(r^{2})\int_{0}^{2\pi}\exp\Big(\frac{4\pi ir\cos(\vartheta)|k|\sqrt{M}}{3\sqrt{3}|q|}\Big)d\vartheta dr.

Observe that by
[
DLMF
, (10.9.2)]
and the fact that
J_{0}(x)
is real-valued we obtain

J_{0}(x)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}e^{ix\cos(\vartheta)}d\vartheta.

Thus (
4.7
) is equal to

2\pi M\int_{0}^{\infty}rV(r^{2})J_{0}\Big(\frac{4\pi r|k|\sqrt{M}}{3\sqrt{3}|q|}\Big)dr.

It remains to compute
\dot{\psi}(k)
.
The Chinese Remainder theorem guarantees that we can write any
t\pmod{3q}
as
t=aq+3b
with
a
a representative of a residue class
\pmod{3}
and
b
a representative of a residue class
\pmod{q}
. Necessarily
a\equiv\overline{q}\pmod{3}
.
Thus (
4.5
) becomes

\displaystyle\dot{\psi}(k)

\displaystyle:=\Big(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\pmod{3}\\
a\equiv\overline{q}\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}\check{e}\Big({-\frac{ak}{3\lambda}}\Big)\Big)\Big(\sum_{b\pmod{q}}\psi(3b)\check{e}\Big({-\frac{bk}{\lambda q}}\Big)\Big)

\displaystyle=\check{e}\Big({-\frac{k}{3\lambda}}\Big)\sum_{b\pmod{q}}\psi(3\lambda b)\check{e}\Big({-\frac{bk}{q}}\Big),

where the displays followed from the fact that
q\equiv 1\pmod{3}
,
\psi
is periodic modulo
q
with
(q,3)=1
,
and Remark
4.1
.
∎

We now state the final version of the Poisson summation formula needed for this paper.

Corollary 4.1
.

Let
n_{1},n_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
be squarefree and satisfy
n_{1}\equiv n_{2}\equiv 1\pmod{3}
.
Let
d:=(n_{1},n_{2})
and
V:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
be a smooth Schwartz function.
Then for any
M>0
we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
m\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}\Big(\frac{m}{n_{1}}\Big)_{3}\overline{\Big(\frac{m}{n_{2}}\Big)_{3}}V\Big(\frac{N(m)}{M}\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{4\pi\big(\frac{d}{n_{1}/d}\big)_{3}\overline{\big(\frac{d}{n_{2}/d}\big)_{3}}Mg(n_{1}/d)\overline{g(n_{2}/d)}}{9\sqrt{3}N\big(n_{1}n_{2}/d\big)}

\displaystyle\times\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]}\widetilde{c}_{d}(k)\overline{\Big(\frac{k}{n_{1}/d}\Big)_{3}}\Big(\frac{k}{n_{2}/d}\Big)_{3}\ddot{V}\Big(\frac{kd\sqrt{M}}{n_{1}n_{2}}\Big),

where

(4.8)

\widetilde{c}_{d}(k):=\check{e}\Big({-\frac{k}{3\lambda}}\Big)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\pmod{d}\\
(x,d)=1\end{subarray}}\check{e}\Big({-\frac{kx}{d}}\Big),

and
\ddot{V}:[0,\infty)\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
is given by

\ddot{V}(u):=\int_{0}^{\infty}rV(r^{2})J_{0}\Big(\frac{4\pi r|u|}{3\sqrt{3}}\Big)dr.

Proof.

We apply Lemma
4.3
to

\psi_{n_{1},n_{2}}(m):=\left(\frac{m}{n_{1}}\right)_{3}\overline{\left(\frac{m}{n_{2}}\right)_{3}}=\left(\frac{m}{n_{1}/d}\right)_{3}\overline{\left(\frac{m}{n_{2}/d}\right)_{3}}\mathbf{1}_{d}(m),\quad m\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega],

where
\textbf{1}_{d}
denotes the principal character modulo
d
. Observe that

\psi_{n_{1},n_{2}}
is
n_{1}n_{2}/d
periodic. All that remains to do is to
compute
\ddot{\psi}_{n_{1},n_{2}}
. We have

(4.9)

\ddot{\psi}_{n_{1},n_{2}}(k)=\check{e}\Big({-\frac{k}{3\lambda}}\Big)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\pmod{n_{1}n_{2}/d}\\
(x,d)=1\end{subarray}}\Big(\frac{3\lambda x}{n_{1}/d}\Big)_{3}\overline{\Big(\frac{3\lambda x}{n_{2}/d}\Big)_{3}}\check{e}\Big({-\frac{kx}{(n_{1}n_{2}/d)}}\Big).

Observe that
3\lambda=(-\lambda)^{3}
and
(n_{1}n_{2}/d,3)=1
,
so (
4.9
) becomes

\ddot{\psi}_{n_{1},n_{2}}(k)=\check{e}\Big({-\frac{k}{3\lambda}}\Big)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\pmod{n_{1}n_{2}/d}\\
(x,d)=1\end{subarray}}\Big(\frac{x}{n_{1}/d}\Big)_{3}\overline{\Big(\frac{x}{n_{2}/d}\Big)_{3}}\check{e}\Big({-\frac{kx}{(n_{1}n_{2}/d)}}\Big).

Since
n_{1}
and
n_{2}
are squarefree we have
(n_{1}n_{2}/d^{2},d)=1
.
We use the Chinese remainder theorem to write
x=a(n_{1}n_{2}/d^{2})+bd
. We find that

\displaystyle\ddot{\psi}_{n_{1},n_{2}}(k)

\displaystyle=\check{e}\Big({-\frac{k}{3\lambda}}\Big)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\pmod{n_{1}n_{2}/d}\\
(x,d)=1\end{subarray}}\Big(\frac{x}{n_{1}/d}\Big)_{3}\overline{\Big(\frac{x}{n_{2}/d}\Big)_{3}}\check{e}\Big({-\frac{kx}{(n_{1}n_{2}/d)}}\Big)

\displaystyle=\Big(\check{e}\Big({-\frac{k}{3\lambda}}\Big)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\pmod{d}\\
(a,d)=1\end{subarray}}\check{e}\Big({-\frac{ak}{d}}\Big)\Big)\Big(\sum_{b\pmod{n_{1}n_{2}/d^{2}}}\Big(\frac{bd}{n_{1}/d}\Big)_{3}\overline{\Big(\frac{bd}{n_{2}/d}\Big)_{3}}\check{e}\Big({-\frac{kb}{(n_{1}n_{2}/d^{2})}}\Big)\Big).

Observe that
(n_{1}/d,n_{2}/d)=1
.
To evaluate the sum over
b
we use the
Chinese remainder theorem again. Writing
b=t(n_{1}/d)+u(n_{2}/d)

gives

\displaystyle\ddot{\psi}_{n_{1},n_{2}}(k)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{c}_{d}(k)\Big(\sum_{t\pmod{n_{2}/d}}\overline{\Big(\frac{tn_{1}}{n_{2}/d}\Big)_{3}}\check{e}\Big({-\frac{tk}{n_{2}/d}}\Big)\Big)\Big(\sum_{u\pmod{n_{1}/d}}\Big(\frac{un_{2}}{n_{1}/d}\Big)_{3}\check{e}\Big({-\frac{uk}{n_{1}/d}}\Big)\Big)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{c}_{d}(k)\Big(\frac{k}{n_{2}/d}\Big)_{3}\overline{\Big(\frac{k}{n_{1}/d}\Big)_{3}}\Big(\frac{n_{2}}{n_{1}/d}\Big)_{3}\overline{\Big(\frac{n_{1}}{n_{2}/d}\Big)_{3}}\cdot g(n_{1}/d)\overline{g(n_{2}/d)},

where the last display follows from the primitivity of characters
\left(\frac{\cdot}{n_{1}/d}\right)_{3}

and
\overline{\left(\frac{\cdot}{n_{2}/d}\right)_{3}}
.
Finally,

\Big(\frac{n_{2}}{n_{1}/d}\Big)_{3}\overline{\Big(\frac{n_{1}}{n_{2}/d}\Big)_{3}}=\left(\frac{n_{2}/d}{n_{1}/d}\right)_{3}\left(\frac{d}{n_{1}/d}\right)_{3}\overline{\left(\frac{n_{1}/d}{n_{2}/d}\right)_{3}\left(\frac{d}{n_{2}/d}\right)_{3}}=\Big(\frac{d}{n_{1}/d}\Big)_{3}\overline{\Big(\frac{d}{n_{2}/d}\Big)_{3}},

where the last equality follows from cubic reciprocity. This completes the proof.
∎

We close this section with standard estimate for
\ddot{V}
.

Lemma 4.4
.

Let
V:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
be a smooth compactly supported function. Then for any integer
k\geq 0
,

(4.10)

|\ddot{V}(u)|\ll_{k,V}(1+|u|)^{-k},\quad u\in\mathbb{C}.

Proof.

Integrating (
4.4
) by parts
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
times using
[
DLMF
, (10.22.1)]

gives

(4.11)

\ddot{V}(u)=(-1)^{k}\Big(\frac{3\sqrt{3}}{2\pi}\Big)^{k}\frac{1}{|u|^{k}}\int_{0}^{\infty}V^{(k)}(r^{2})\cdot r^{k+1}J_{k}\Big(\frac{4\pi r|u|}{3\sqrt{3}}\Big)dr.

The claim immediately follows.
∎

5.
Voronoi summation in the level aspect

The Fourier coefficients of the cubic theta function essentially sample cubic Gauss sums.
Naturally, automorphy of the theta function is a key input in the proof of our
level aspect Voronoi summation formula.

5.1.
Geometry, groups, and the cubic theta function at cusps

Let
\mathbb{H}^{3}
denote the hyperbolic 3-space
\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}
.
We embed
\mathbb{H}^{3}
in the Hamilton quaternions by identifying

i=\sqrt{-1}
with
\hat{i}
and

w=(z,v)=(x+iy,v)\in\mathbb{H}^{3}
with
x+y\hat{i}+v\hat{k}
, where

1,\hat{i},\hat{j},\hat{k}
denote the unit quaternions. In terms of
quaternion arithmetic, the group action
of
\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
on
\mathbb{H}^{3}

is given by

\gamma w=(aw+b)(cw+d)^{-1},\quad\quad\gamma=\left(\begin{matrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{matrix}\right)\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{C})\quad\text{and}\quad w\in\mathbb{H}^{3}.

In terms of coordinates,

(5.1)

\gamma w=\bigg(\frac{(az+b)\overline{(cz+d)}+a\overline{c}v^{2}}{|cz+d|^{2}+|c|^{2}v^{2}},\frac{v}{|cz+d|^{2}+|c|^{2}v^{2}}\bigg),\quad w=(z,v)\in\mathbb{H}^{3}.

Let
\Gamma:=\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z}[\omega])
.
It is a standard fact that
\Gamma
is generated by the elements

P:=\left(\begin{matrix}\omega&0\\
0&\omega^{2}\end{matrix}\right),\quad T:=\left(\begin{matrix}1&1\\
0&1\end{matrix}\right),\quad\text{and}\quad E:=\left(\begin{matrix}0&-1\\
1&0\end{matrix}\right).

Let
A\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]

satisfy
A\equiv 0\pmod{3}
, and

\Gamma_{1}(A):=\{\gamma\in\Gamma:\gamma\equiv I\pmod{A}\}.

Observe that
\Gamma_{1}(A)
is a normal subgroup of
\Gamma
since it is the kernel of the
reduction modulo
A
map. Let

(5.2)

\Gamma_{2}:=\langle\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z}),\Gamma_{1}(3)\rangle=\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})\Gamma_{1}(3)=\Gamma_{1}(3)\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z}),

where the last two equalities follow because

\Gamma_{1}(3)
is normal in
\Gamma
.
We also have
[\Gamma:\Gamma_{2}]=27
(see
[
Pat77
, §2]
for the calculation).
We also observe the trivial (but useful) fact that for any
g,g^{\prime}\in\Gamma

we have that

\Gamma_{1}(3)g=\Gamma_{1}(3)g^{\prime}\iff g\equiv g^{\prime}\pmod{3}.

Furthermore, for any
g,g^{\prime}\in\Gamma
we have that

(5.3)

g\equiv g^{\prime}\pmod{3}\implies\Gamma_{2}g=\Gamma_{2}g^{\prime}.

More properties of these groups can be found in
[
Pat77
, §2]
.

Let

\chi:\Gamma_{1}(3)\rightarrow~\{1,\omega,\omega^{2}\}

be the famous cubic Kubota character
[
Kub66
,
Kub69
]
, given by

(5.4)

\chi(\gamma):=\begin{cases}\left(\frac{c}{a}\right)_{3}&\text{if}\quad\gamma=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in\Gamma_{1}(3)\quad\text{and}\quad c\neq 0,\\
1&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

It was shown by Patterson
[
Pat77
, §2]
that
\chi
extends
to a well-defined homomorphism

\chi:\Gamma_{2}\rightarrow\{1,\omega,\omega^{2}\},

when one defines

\chi\lvert_{\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})}\equiv 1
.

Remark 5.1
.

There is a useful alternative expression for
\chi(\gamma)
to the one given in (
5.4
). For
\gamma=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in\Gamma_{1}(3)
, we have the determinant equation

ad-bc=1
with
1+bc\equiv 1\pmod{9}
. Given
0\neq c\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]

we write
c=\pm\lambda^{k}\omega^{j}c^{\prime}
where
k\geq 2
,
0\leq j\leq 2
are integers, and

c^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
is such that
c^{\prime}\equiv 1\pmod{3}
.
Thus

\Big(\frac{c}{ad}\Big)_{3}=\Big(\frac{c}{1+bc}\Big)_{3}=\Big(\frac{\lambda}{1+bc}\Big)^{k}_{3}\Big(\frac{\omega}{1+bc}\Big)^{j}_{3}\Big(\frac{c^{\prime}}{1+bc}\Big)_{3}=1,

where the last equality follows from
1+bc\equiv 1\pmod{9}
, (
1.4
), and (
1.5
).
Hence

\Big(\frac{c}{a}\Big)_{3}=\overline{\Big(\frac{c}{d}\Big)_{3}}=\Big(\frac{b}{d}\Big)_{3},

and we obtain

(5.5)

\chi(\gamma)=\begin{cases}\left(\frac{b}{d}\right)_{3}&\text{if}\quad\gamma=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in\Gamma_{1}(3)\quad\text{and}\quad c\neq 0,\\
1&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Let
\theta(w)
denote the cubic metaplectic theta function of Kubota on
\mathbb{H}^{3}
.
It is automorphic on
\Gamma_{2}

with multiplier
\chi
. It has Fourier expansion (at
\infty
) given by

\theta(w)=\sigma v^{2/3}+\sum_{\mu\in\lambda^{-3}\mathbb{Z}[\omega]}\tau(\mu)vK_{\frac{1}{3}}(4\pi|\mu|v)\check{e}(\mu z),\quad w\in\mathbb{H}^{3},

where

(5.6)

\sigma:=3^{5/2}/2,

and the other Fourier coefficients were
computed by Patterson
[
Pat77
, Theorem 8.1]
. They are

(5.7)

\tau(\mu)=\begin{cases}\overline{g(\lambda^{2},c)}\big|\frac{d}{c}\big|3^{n/2+2}&\quad\text{if}\quad\mu=\pm\lambda^{3n-4}cd^{3},\quad n\geq 1\\
e^{-\frac{2\pi i}{9}}\overline{g(\omega\lambda^{2},c)}\big|\frac{d}{c}\big|3^{n/2+2}&\quad\text{if}\quad\mu=\pm\omega\lambda^{3n-4}cd^{3},\quad n\geq 1\\
e^{\frac{2\pi i}{9}}\overline{g(\omega^{2}\lambda^{2},c)}\big|\frac{d}{c}\big|3^{n/2+2}&\quad\text{if}\quad\mu=\pm\omega^{2}\lambda^{3n-4}cd^{3},\quad n\geq 1\\
\overline{g(1,c)}\big|\frac{d}{c}\big|3^{n/2+5/2}&\quad\text{if}\quad\mu=\pm\lambda^{3n-3}cd^{3},\quad n\geq 0\\
0&\quad\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

where

(5.8)

c,d\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega],\quad c,d\equiv 1\pmod{3},\quad\text{and}\quad\mu^{2}(c)=1.

It follows from (
5.7
) that
\tau(\cdot)
is an even function.

Implicit in
[
Pat77
, §7 and §8]
is a careful study of
\theta(w)
at
various cusps of
\Gamma_{2}
. We extract the information that will be of use to us.
Let
\{\gamma_{j}:j=1,\ldots 27\}
be the complete set of inequivalent representatives
for
\Gamma_{2}\backslash\Gamma
given in
[
Pat77
, Table II pg. 129]
.
Particular coset representatives
\gamma_{j}
of
\Gamma_{2}\backslash\Gamma

of importance to us are

\gamma_{1}=I,\gamma_{2}=\left(\begin{matrix}1&\omega\\
0&1\end{matrix}\right),\gamma_{3}=\left(\begin{matrix}1&-\omega\\
0&1\end{matrix}\right),\gamma_{10}=\left(\begin{matrix}1&0\\
\omega&1\end{matrix}\right),\text{ and }\gamma_{19}=\left(\begin{matrix}1&0\\
\omega^{2}&1\end{matrix}\right).

For each
j=1,2,\ldots,27
, let

(5.9)

F_{j}(w):=\theta(\gamma_{j}(w)),\quad w\in\mathbb{H}^{3}.

If
g\in\Gamma
, then

(5.10)

\gamma_{j}g=g_{j}(g)\gamma_{k_{j}(g)},\quad\text{for some}\quad g_{j}(g)\in\Gamma_{2}\quad\text{and}\quad 1\leq k_{j}(g)\leq 27.

Thus

(5.11)

F_{j}(g(w))=\chi(g_{j}(g))F_{k_{j}(g)}(w)\quad\text{for all}\quad w\in\mathbb{H}^{3}.

Each
F_{j}
is automorphic on
\Gamma_{1}(9)
with multiplier
\chi

by
[
Pat78a
, Lemma 2.1]
. Following Patterson, we define

(5.12)

F_{j}^{\star}(w):=\sum_{\mu}d_{j}(\mu)vK_{1/3}(4\pi|\mu|v)\check{e}(\mu z),\quad w\in\mathbb{H}^{3},

where the

d_{j}(\mu)
have support contained in
\lambda^{-4}\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\setminus\{0\}
,
and have expressions in terms of
\tau(\mu)
,
\tau_{1}(\mu)

[
Pat77
, (8.8)]

and
\tau_{2}(\mu)

[
Pat77
, (8.9)]
, see Appendix
A
. For the reader’s convenience we state formulae for

\tau_{1}(\mu)
and
\tau_{2}(\mu)
here. They are given by

(5.13)

\tau_{1}(\mu)=\begin{cases}9\omega\overline{g(\lambda^{2},c)}\big|\frac{d}{c}\big|&\quad\text{if}\quad\mu=\lambda^{-4}cd^{3}\\
9e^{-\frac{2\pi i}{9}}\omega^{2}\overline{g(\omega\lambda^{2},c)}\big|\frac{d}{c}\big|&\quad\text{if}\quad\mu=\omega\lambda^{-4}cd^{3}\\
9e^{\frac{2\pi i}{9}}\overline{g(\omega^{2}\lambda^{2},c)}\big|\frac{d}{c}\big|&\quad\text{if}\quad\mu=\omega^{2}\lambda^{-4}cd^{3}\\
0&\quad\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

and

(5.14)

\tau_{2}(\mu)=\begin{cases}9\omega^{2}\overline{g(\lambda^{2},c)}\big|\frac{d}{c}\big|&\quad\text{if}\quad\mu=-\lambda^{-4}cd^{3}\\
9e^{-\frac{2\pi i}{9}}\overline{g(\omega\lambda^{2},c)}\big|\frac{d}{c}\big|&\quad\text{if}\quad\mu=-\lambda^{-4}\omega cd^{3}\\
9\omega e^{\frac{2\pi i}{9}}\overline{g(\omega^{2}\lambda^{2},c)}\big|\frac{d}{c}\big|&\quad\text{if}\quad\mu=-\lambda^{-4}\omega^{2}cd^{3}\\
0&\quad\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

where
c
and
d
are as in (
5.8
). The formulas for the
d_{j}(\mu)

are given in
[
Pat77
, Table III pg. 151]
. We have also included
them in Appendix
A
.
We have the Fourier expansions (at
\infty
)

[
Pat77
, pg. 148]
,

(5.15)

F_{j}(w)=\begin{cases}\sigma v(w)^{2/3}+F_{j}^{\star}(w)&\text{if}\quad 1\leq j\leq 9,\\
F_{j}^{\star}(w)&\text{if}\quad 10\leq j\leq 27\end{cases},\quad w\in\mathbb{H}^{3}.

To understand the maps

j\mapsto g_{j}(\cdot)
,
j\mapsto k_{j}(\cdot)

and
j\mapsto\chi(g_{j}(\cdot))

occurring in (
5.10
)
and (
5.11
),
it suffices to compute them on
the generators of
\Gamma=\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z}[\omega])
:
P,T,
and
E
.
The values of
k_{j}(E)
appear in

[
Pat77
, Table III]
. We have included
the
k_{j}
values on all three generators in
Appendix
A
.

5.2.
Conjugation and coefficient sieving

It is more convenient for us to work with
the
\overline{F_{j}}(w)
.
It follows from (
5.15
) that they each have Fourier expansion (at
\infty
) given by

(5.16)

\overline{F_{j}}(w)=\begin{cases}\sigma v^{2/3}+\sum_{\mu}\overline{d_{j}(-\mu)}vK_{\frac{1}{3}}(4\pi|\mu|v)\check{e}(\mu z)&\text{if}\quad 1\leq j\leq 9\\
\sum_{\mu}\overline{d_{j}(-\mu)}vK_{\frac{1}{3}}(4\pi|\mu|v)\check{e}(\mu z)&\text{if}\quad 10\leq j\leq 27\end{cases},\quad w\in\mathbb{H}^{3},

since
K_{1/3}(x)\in\mathbb{R}
for
x>0
.

The Fourier coefficients of
\overline{F_{1}}(w)
are given by

\overline{d_{1}(-\mu)}=\overline{\tau(-\mu)}=\overline{\tau(\mu)},

where the last equality follows from the fact that
\tau(\cdot)
is even. Let

(5.17)

S:=\{\lambda^{-3}cd^{3}\in\mathbb{Q}(\omega):c,d\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega],\quad c,d\equiv 1\hskip-5.69046pt\pmod{3}\quad\text{and}\quad\mu^{2}(c)=1\},

and

\overline{F_{1}}(w)_{S}:=\sum_{\mu\in S}\overline{\tau(\mu)}vK_{\frac{1}{3}}(4\pi|\mu|v)\check{e}(\mu z),\quad w\in\mathbb{H}^{3}.

Remark 5.2
.

We study the function
\overline{F_{1}}(w)_{S}
because its Fourier coefficients are supported on
S

and the
\mu=\lambda^{-3}cd^{3}
th coefficient is

\widetilde{g}(c)|d|
up to an absolute constant (cf. (
5.7
)).
These are the coefficients (up to twisting)
we want to appear in our Voronoi formula (cf. Proposition
5.3
).

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
\overline{F_{1}}(w)_{S}
be as above. Then

\overline{F_{1}}(w)_{S}=\frac{1}{3}\Big(\overline{F_{1}}(w)+\omega\overline{F_{2}}(w)+\omega^{2}\overline{F_{3}}(w)\Big),

and
\overline{F_{1}}(w)_{S}
is automorphic under
\Gamma_{1}(9)
with multiplier
\overline{\chi}
.

Proof.

Following
[
Pat78a
, Theorem 5.2]
, we detect
\mu\in S
additively.
From (
5.7
), we have

\{\mu\in\lambda^{-3}\mathbb{Z}[\omega]:\check{e}(\omega\mu)=\omega^{2}\quad\text{and}\quad\tau(\mu)\neq 0\}=S.

Thus

\displaystyle\overline{F_{1}}(w)_{S}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3}\sigma v^{2/3}(1+\omega+\omega^{2})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{3}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mu\end{subarray}\in\lambda^{-3}\mathbb{Z}[\omega]}\overline{\tau(\mu)}vK_{\frac{1}{3}}(4\pi|\mu|v)\check{e}(\mu z)\big(1+\omega\check{e}(\omega\mu)+\omega^{2}\check{e}(2\omega\mu)\big)

(5.18)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3}\Big(\overline{F_{1}}(w)+\omega\overline{F_{2}}(w)+\omega^{2}\overline{F_{3}}(w)\Big),

where the last term in (
5.18
) was obtained by writing

\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&2\omega\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&3\omega\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&-\omega\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)

and using automorphy of
\overline{F_{1}}(w)=\overline{\theta}(w)

on
\Gamma_{2}
with multiplier
\overline{\chi}
. This proves the first claim.
Each
\overline{F_{j}}
is automorphic on
\Gamma_{1}(9)

with multiplier
\overline{\chi}
, and so the second claim follows.
∎

5.3.
Twists

Let
r\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
with
r\equiv 1\pmod{3}
, and
\psi

be a function on
\mathbb{Z}[\omega]

that is periodic modulo
r
.
In view of (
5.12
), the
\psi
-twist of
\overline{F^{\star}_{j}}(w)
is
defined by

(5.19)

\overline{F^{\star}_{j}}(w;\psi):=\sum_{\mu}\overline{d_{j}(-\mu)}\psi(\lambda^{4}\mu)vK_{\frac{1}{3}}(4\pi|\mu|v)\check{e}(\mu z),\quad w\in\mathbb{H}^{3}.

In view of (
5.16
), the
\psi
-twist of
\overline{F_{j}}(w)

is

(5.20)

\overline{F_{j}}(w;\psi):=\begin{cases}\sigma\psi(0)v^{2/3}+\overline{F^{\star}_{j}}(w;\psi)&1\leq j\leq 9\\
\overline{F^{\star}_{j}}(w;\psi)&10\leq j\leq 27\end{cases},\quad w\in\mathbb{H}^{3}.

Remark 5.3
.

The Fourier coefficients of all the
\overline{F_{j}}

have support contained in
\lambda^{-4}\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
. This explains why we define a general twist by

\psi(\lambda^{4}(\cdot))
in (
5.20
). In the special case
\overline{F_{1}}=\overline{\theta}
, (
5.7
)
tells us that the Fourier coefficients have support contained in
\lambda^{-3}\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
. Thus
our twisting definition produces an extraneous
\psi(\lambda)
factor in this case. This will be immaterial
in our final results.

Define the Fourier transform

(5.21)

\widehat{\psi}(u):=\sum_{x\pmod{r}}\psi(x)\check{e}\Big(\frac{ux}{r}\Big),\quad u\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega].

Fourier inversion tells us that

\psi(x)=\frac{1}{N(r)}\sum_{u\pmod{r}}\widehat{\psi}(u)\check{e}\Big({-\frac{ux}{r}}\Big),\quad x\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega].

We also define the following non-Archimedean
analogue of a Bessel
K_{1/3}
–transform,

(5.22)

\widetilde{\psi}(u):=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\pmod{r}\end{subarray}}\psi(x)S_{1/3}(x,u;r),\quad u\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega],

where

(5.23)

S_{1/3}(x,u;r):=\left(\frac{\lambda}{r}\right)_{3}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d\pmod{r}\\
(d,r)=1\\
(\lambda^{4}d)(\lambda^{4}a)\equiv 1\pmod{r}\end{subarray}}\Big(\frac{a}{r}\Big)_{3}\check{e}\Big(\frac{xd+ua}{r}\Big),

is the cubic Kloosterman sum. Note that it is convenient for us to have the
\lambda^{4}=9
factors built into the congruence
in the cubic Kloosterman sum. These factors naturally appear when we use the automorphy of the
\overline{F}_{j}
(with multiplier
\overline{\chi}
)
on the group
\Gamma_{1}(\lambda^{4})
in the proof of the following result.

To isolate twists of the cubic Gauss sums, we need to analyse

(5.24)

\overline{F_{1}}(w;\psi)_{S}:=\sum_{\mu\in S}\overline{\tau(\mu)}\psi(\lambda^{4}\mu)vK_{\frac{1}{3}}(4\pi|\mu|v)\check{e}(\mu z),\quad w\in\mathbb{H}^{3}.

Lemma 5.2
.

Suppose
r\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
with
r\equiv 1\pmod{3}
,
and
\psi
is a sequence on
\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
that is periodic modulo
r
.
Suppose that
\widehat{\psi}
is supported only on residue classes coprime to
r
.
For
w=(z,v)\in\mathbb{H}^{3}
,
we have

(5.25)

\displaystyle\overline{F_{1}}(w;\psi)_{S}=\frac{1}{3N(r)}\Big\{\overline{F_{1}}+\omega\overline{F^{\star}_{19}}+\omega^{2}\overline{F^{\star}_{10}}\Big\}\Big(\Big(-\frac{\overline{z}}{r^{2}(|z|^{2}+v^{2})},\frac{v}{|r|^{2}(|z|^{2}+v^{2})}\Big);\widetilde{\psi}\Big).

Proof.

Fourier inversion and Lemma
5.1
imply that

\displaystyle\overline{F_{1}}(w;\psi)_{S}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N(r)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d\pmod{r}\\
(d,r)=1\end{subarray}}\widehat{\psi}(d)\overline{F_{1}}\Big(z-\frac{\lambda^{4}d}{r},v\Big)_{S}

(5.26)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3N(r)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d\pmod{r}\\
(d,r)=1\end{subarray}}\widehat{\psi}(d)\Big(\overline{F_{1}}\Big(z-\frac{\lambda^{4}d}{r},v\Big)+\omega\overline{F_{2}}\Big(z-\frac{\lambda^{4}d}{r},v\Big)+\omega^{2}\overline{F_{3}}\Big(z-\frac{\lambda^{4}d}{r},v\Big)\Big).

Given our
r\equiv 1\pmod{3}
, and each
d
in (
5.3
), we have

(r,\lambda^{4}d)=1
. Thus
there exists

\left(\begin{matrix}r&-\lambda^{4}a\\
\lambda^{4}d&b\end{matrix}\right)\in\Gamma_{1}(3),

and hence there exists

(5.27)

\gamma:=\left(\begin{matrix}0&1\\
-1&0\end{matrix}\right)\left(\begin{matrix}r&-\lambda^{4}a\\
\lambda^{4}d&b\end{matrix}\right)=\left(\begin{matrix}\lambda^{4}d&b\\
-r&\lambda^{4}a\end{matrix}\right)\in\Gamma_{2}.

Note that we also used (
5.2
) implicitly in the above display.

A direct computation shows that

(5.28)

\gamma\Big(\frac{\lambda^{4}a}{r}-\frac{\overline{z}}{r^{2}(|z|^{2}+v^{2})},\frac{v}{|r|^{2}(|z|^{2}+v^{2})}\Big)=\Big(z-\frac{\lambda^{4}d}{r},v\Big).

We now carefully factorise the
\gamma
in (
5.27
) as a word in

P
,
E
and
T
so that (
5.28
) and automorphy can be used in
(
5.3
).
For each
\kappa=m+n\omega\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
,
m,n\in\mathbb{Z}
,
let

A(\kappa):=PT^{-m}PT^{-m+n}P=\left(\begin{matrix}1&\kappa\\
0&1\end{matrix}\right).

For each
r,b\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
occurring in (
5.27
), let

W(r,b):=E^{3}A(r)EA(b)EA(r)=\left(\begin{matrix}b&-1+br\\
1-br&2r-br^{2}\end{matrix}\right).

Then

(5.29)

W(r,b)E\gamma=\left(\begin{matrix}-9d+br+9bdr&-b-9ab+b^{2}r\\
r+18dr-br^{2}-9bdr^{2}&-9a+2br+9abr-b^{2}r^{2}\end{matrix}\right)\\
=:\widetilde{\gamma}\in\Gamma_{1}(9).

Equivalently,

(5.30)

\gamma=E^{3}W(r,b)^{-1}\widetilde{\gamma}.

To obtain (
5.3
)
immediately below we use (
5.28
), (
5.30
), (
5.10
), (
5.11
),
and the fact that each
F_{j}
is automorphic on
\Gamma_{1}(9)

with multiplier
\chi
. For each
j=1,2,3
,

\displaystyle\overline{F_{j}}\Big(z-\frac{\lambda^{4}d}{r},v\Big)

\displaystyle=\overline{\chi}(g_{j}(E^{3}W(r,b)^{-1}))\cdot\overline{\chi}(\widetilde{\gamma})

(5.31)

\displaystyle\times\overline{F_{k_{j}(E^{3}W(r,b)^{-1})}}\Big(\frac{\lambda^{4}a}{r}-\frac{\overline{z}}{r^{2}(|z|^{2}+v^{2})},\frac{v}{|r|^{2}(|z|^{2}+v^{2})}\Big).

We claim that

(5.32)

\displaystyle k_{1}\big(E^{3}W(r,b)^{-1}\big)

\displaystyle=1;

(5.33)

\displaystyle k_{2}\big(E^{3}W(r,b)^{-1}\big)

\displaystyle=19;

(5.34)

\displaystyle k_{3}\big(E^{3}W(r,b)^{-1}\big)

\displaystyle=10.

We will use (
5.3
) and the computations below to establish (
5.32
)–(
5.34
).
Observe that

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}E^{3}W(r,b)^{-1}\gamma_{1}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{matrix}-1+br&b\\
-2r+br^{2}&-1+br\end{matrix}\right)

(5.35)

\displaystyle\equiv E^{3}\pmod{3}.

Thus
\gamma_{1}E^{3}W(r,b)^{-1}\gamma_{1}^{-1}\in\Gamma_{2}
, and (
5.32
) follows.
Note that (
5.33
) (resp. (
5.34
)) follow similarly from

\displaystyle\gamma_{2}E^{3}W(r,b)^{-1}\gamma_{19}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{matrix}(\omega+1)b-2\omega r+\omega br^{2}&-\omega+b+\omega br\\
-(\omega+1)-2r+(\omega+1)br+br^{2}&-1+br\end{matrix}\right)

(5.36)

\displaystyle\equiv T^{-1}E^{3}\pmod{3},

(resp.)

\displaystyle\gamma_{3}E^{3}W(r,b)^{-1}\gamma_{10}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{matrix}\omega-\omega b+2\omega r-\omega br-\omega br^{2}&\omega+b-\omega br\\
\omega-2r-\omega br+br^{2}&-1+br\end{matrix}\right)

(5.37)

\displaystyle\equiv E^{3}\pmod{3}.

We now compute the automorphy factor
\overline{\chi}(g_{j}(E^{3}W(r,b)^{-1}))\cdot\overline{\chi}(\widetilde{\gamma})

for each
j=1,2,3
in (
5.3
).
The displays (
5.3
)–(
5.3
) prove that

\displaystyle g_{1}(E^{3}W(r,b)^{-1})

\displaystyle=E^{3}W(r,b)^{-1};

\displaystyle g_{2}(E^{3}W(r,b)^{-1})

\displaystyle=\gamma_{2}E^{3}W(r,b)^{-1}\gamma_{19}^{-1};

\displaystyle g_{3}(E^{3}W(r,b)^{-1})

\displaystyle=\gamma_{3}E^{3}W(r,b)^{-1}\gamma_{10}^{-1},

respectively. In order to use either (
5.4
) or (
5.5
) to compute

\overline{\chi}(g_{j}(E^{3}W(r,b)^{-1}))

we note that
\chi|_{\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})}=1

and
Eg_{1}(E^{3}W(r,b)^{-1}),ETg_{2}(E^{3}W(r,b)^{-1}),Eg_{3}(E^{3}W(r,b)^{-1})\in\Gamma_{1}(3)
(cf. (
5.3
)–(
5.3
)).
We also repeatedly use the determinant equation
81ad+br=1
from
(
5.27
). Now,

\displaystyle\overline{\chi}(g_{1}(E^{3}W(r,b)^{-1}))

\displaystyle=\overline{\chi}(Eg_{1}(E^{3}W(r,b)^{-1}))

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\overline{\left(\frac{1-br}{b}\right)_{3}}&\text{if}\quad-1+br\neq 0\\
1&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

(5.38)

\displaystyle=1.

Similar computations yield

\displaystyle\overline{\chi}(g_{3}(E^{3}W(r,b)^{-1}))

\displaystyle=\overline{\chi}(Eg_{3}(E^{3}W(r,b)^{-1}))

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\overline{\left(\frac{1-br}{\omega+b-\omega br}\right)_{3}}&\text{if}\quad\omega(1-b+2r-br-br^{2})\neq 0\\
1&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

(5.39)

\displaystyle=1,

and

\displaystyle\overline{\chi}

\displaystyle(g_{2}(E^{3}W(r,b)^{-1}))

\displaystyle=\overline{\chi}(ETg_{2}(E^{3}W(r,b)^{-1}))

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\overline{\left(\frac{1-br}{-\omega+b+\omega br+br-1}\right)_{3}}&\text{if}\quad(\omega+1)b-2\omega r+\omega br^{2}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad-(\omega+1)-2r+(\omega+1)br+br^{2}\neq 0\\
1&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

(5.40)

\displaystyle=1.

We also have

\displaystyle\overline{\chi}(\widetilde{\gamma})

\displaystyle=\overline{\left(\frac{r+18dr-br^{2}-9bdr^{2}}{-9d+br+9bdr}\right)_{3}}=\overline{\left(\frac{81adr+18dr-9bdr^{2}}{-9d+br+9bdr}\right)_{3}}

\displaystyle=\overline{\left(\frac{9d}{-9d+br+9bdr}\right)_{3}}\cdot\overline{\left(\frac{9ar+2r-br^{2}}{-9d+br+9bdr}\right)_{3}}

\displaystyle=\overline{\left(\frac{9d}{1}\right)_{3}}\cdot\overline{\left(\frac{-9d+br+9bdr}{9ar+2r-br^{2}}\right)_{3}}\quad\quad\text{(by cubic reciprocity)}

\displaystyle=\overline{\left(\frac{-9d+br+9bdr}{r}\right)}\cdot\overline{\left(\frac{-9d+br+9bdr}{9a+2-br}\right)_{3}}

\displaystyle=\overline{\left(\frac{9d}{r}\right)_{3}}\cdot\overline{\left(\frac{1-81ad-729ad^{2}}{9a+1+81ad}\right)_{3}}=\left(\frac{9a}{r}\right)_{3}\cdot\overline{\left(\frac{9d+1}{9a+1+81ad}\right)_{3}}

(5.41)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{9a}{r}\right)_{3}\cdot\overline{\left(\frac{9d+1}{1}\right)_{3}}=\left(\frac{\lambda^{4}a}{r}\right)_{3}.

We combine (
5.3
)–(
5.3
)
in (
5.3
). Note that
\overline{F_{19}}=\overline{F^{\star}_{19}}

and
\overline{F_{10}}=\overline{F^{\star}_{10}}
by (
5.15
).
We then use the Fourier expansions
(
5.16
) to open
\overline{F_{1}}
,
\overline{F^{\star}_{19}}
and
\overline{F^{\star}_{10}}
,
and assembling the sum over
d
(equivalently
a
) shows that

\displaystyle\overline{F}_{1}(w;\psi)_{S}=\frac{1}{3N(r)}\Big(\overline{F_{1}}+\omega\overline{F^{\star}_{19}}+\omega^{2}\overline{F^{\star}_{10}}\Big)\Big(-\frac{\overline{z}}{r^{2}(|z|^{2}+v^{2})},\frac{v}{|r|^{2}(|z|^{2}+v^{2})};\Psi\Big),

where

(5.42)

\Psi(u):=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d\pmod{r}\\
(d,r)=1\\
(\lambda^{4}d)(\lambda^{4}a)\equiv 1\pmod{r}\end{subarray}}\widehat{\psi}(d)\Big(\frac{\lambda^{4}a}{r}\Big)_{3}\check{e}\Big({\frac{au}{r}}\Big),\quad u\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega].

After opening
\widehat{\psi}(d)
using the definition (
5.21
),
and interchanging the order of summation,
we readily see that
\Psi(u)=\widetilde{\psi}(u)
for all
u\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
.
∎

For the coming lemma it will be instructive to open the definition of
\check{e}(\cdot)
,

\check{e}(\mu z)=e(\mu z+\overline{\mu z}),\quad z\in\mathbb{C}.

For
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}
and
1\leq j\leq 27
, let

\overline{F_{j}}(w;\psi,\ell)=\overline{F_{j}^{\star}}(w;\psi,\ell):=\begin{cases}\sum_{\mu}\overline{d_{j}(-\mu)}\psi(\lambda^{4}\mu)\mu^{\ell}vK_{\tfrac{1}{3}}(4\pi|\mu|v)e(\mu z+\overline{\mu z}),&\text{ if }\ell>0\\
\sum_{\mu}\overline{d_{j}(-\mu)}\psi(\lambda^{4}\mu)\overline{\mu}^{|\ell|}vK_{\tfrac{1}{3}}(4\pi|\mu|v)e(\mu z+\overline{\mu z}),&\text{ if }\ell<0\end{cases}.

For
\ell=0
,
\overline{F_{j}}(w;\psi,0):=\overline{F_{j}}(w;\psi)
from before.
We have

(5.43)

\overline{F_{j}}(w;\psi,\ell)=\frac{1}{(2\pi i)^{|\ell|}}\begin{cases}\big(\frac{\partial}{\partial z}\big)^{\ell}\overline{F_{j}}(w;\psi),&\text{if}\quad\ell>0\\
\big(\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{z}}\big)^{|\ell|}\overline{F_{j}}(w;\psi),&\text{if}\quad\ell<0,\end{cases}\quad w=(z,v)\in\mathbb{H}^{3}.

We apply differential operators in the proof of the next lemma. Thus we remind the reader that

\overline{F_{j}}(w;\psi,\ell)=\overline{G_{j}}(z,\overline{z},v;\psi,\ell)
is a function of
z,\overline{z}
and
v
,
although the
\overline{F_{j}}
notation suppresses this.

With these observations in mind we deduce the following Corollary.

Corollary 5.1
.

Suppose
r\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
with
r\equiv 1\pmod{3}
,
and
\psi
is a sequence on
\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
that is periodic modulo
r
.
Suppose that
\widehat{\psi}
is supported only on residue classes coprime to
r
.
By abuse of notation, write

F_{j}((0,v);\psi,\ell)
as
F_{j}(v;\psi,\ell)
for all
v>0
,
1\leq j\leq 27
and
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
.
Then we have

\overline{F_{1}}(v;\psi,\ell)_{S}=\frac{(-1)^{\ell}}{3N(r)^{1+|\ell|}v^{2|\ell|}}\Big(\frac{\overline{r}}{r}\Big)^{-\ell}\Big(\delta_{\ell\neq 0}\overline{F_{1}^{\star}}+\delta_{\ell=0}\overline{F_{1}}+\omega\overline{F^{\star}_{19}}+\omega^{2}\overline{F^{\star}_{10}}\Big)\Big(\frac{1}{|r|^{2}v};\widetilde{\psi},-\ell\Big),

or equivalently,

\Big(\overline{F_{1}^{\star}}\delta_{\ell\neq 0}+\overline{F_{1}}\delta_{\ell=0}+\omega\overline{F^{\star}_{19}}+\omega^{2}\overline{F^{\star}_{10}}\Big)(v;\widetilde{\psi},-\ell)=\frac{3\cdot(-1)^{\ell}}{N(r)^{|\ell|-1}v^{2|\ell|}}\Big(\frac{\overline{r}}{r}\Big)^{\ell}\overline{F_{1}}\Big(\frac{1}{|r|^{2}v};\psi,\ell\Big).

Proof.

Setting
z=0
in (
5.25
) gives the claim when
\ell=0
.
If
\ell>0
, we write
|z|^{2}=z\overline{z}
and
apply the operator
\frac{1}{(2\pi i)^{\ell}}\big(\frac{\partial}{\partial z}\big)^{\ell}\big|_{z=0}

to both sides of (
5.25
).
A computation with the chain rule yields

\displaystyle\overline{F_{1}}((0,v);\psi,\ell)_{S}=\frac{(-1)^{\ell}}{3N(r)^{1+\ell}v^{2\ell}}\Big(\frac{\overline{r}}{r}\Big)^{-\ell}\cdot\Big(\overline{F_{1}^{\star}}+\omega\overline{F^{\star}_{19}}+\omega^{2}\overline{F^{\star}_{10}}\Big)\Big(\Big(0,\frac{1}{|r|^{2}v}\Big);\widetilde{\psi},-\ell\Big).

When
\ell<0
a similar argument with the operator
\frac{1}{(2\pi i)^{|\ell|}}\big(\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{z}}\big)^{|\ell|}\Big|_{z=0}

yields the analogous result.
∎

5.4.
Poles and Dirichlet series

Let
\psi
be as in Corollary
5.1
.
For
\operatorname{Re}(s)>1
,
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
and
1\leq j\leq 27
, consider the family of Dirichlet series

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}(s,\overline{F^{\star}_{j}};\psi,\ell)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\mu}\frac{\overline{d_{j}(-\mu)}\psi(\lambda^{4}\mu)\big(\frac{\mu}{|\mu|}\big)^{\ell}}{N(\mu)^{s}};

(5.44)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}(s,{\overline{F_{1}}};\psi,\ell)_{S}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\mu\in S}\frac{\overline{\tau(\mu)}\psi(\lambda^{4}\mu)\big(\frac{\mu}{|\mu|}\big)^{\ell}}{N(\mu)^{s}}.

For
\operatorname{Re}(s)>1
, we introduce the integral transforms

\displaystyle\Lambda(s,\overline{F^{\star}_{j}};\psi,\ell)

\displaystyle:=\int_{0}^{\infty}\overline{F^{\star}_{j}}(v;\psi,\ell)v^{2s+|\ell|-2}dv;

\displaystyle\Lambda(s,{\overline{F_{1}}};\psi,\ell)_{S}

\displaystyle:=\int_{0}^{\infty}{\overline{F}_{1}}(v;\psi,\ell)_{S}v^{2s+|\ell|-2}dv,

where by abuse of notation we wrote
F_{i}(v;\psi,\ell)=F_{i}((0,v);\psi,\ell)
.
In the case
\ell=0
we will omit the index
\ell
from the notation.

Lemma 5.3
.

For
\operatorname{Re}(s)>1
we have

\displaystyle\Lambda(s,\overline{F^{\star}_{j}};\psi,\ell)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}(2\pi)^{-2s-|\ell|}\Gamma\Big(s+\frac{|\ell|}{2}-\frac{1}{6}\Big)\Gamma\Big(s+\frac{|\ell|}{2}+\frac{1}{6}\Big)\mathcal{D}(s,\overline{F^{\star}_{j}};\psi,\ell);

\displaystyle\Lambda(s,\overline{F_{1}};\psi,\ell)_{S}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}(2\pi)^{-2s-|\ell|}\Gamma\Big(s+\frac{|\ell|}{2}-\frac{1}{6}\Big)\Gamma\Big(s+\frac{|\ell|}{2}+\frac{1}{6}\Big)\mathcal{D}(s,\overline{F_{1}};\psi,\ell)_{S}.

Proof.

The proofs of both identities are virtually identical, so we prove the latter, with
\ell<0
.
For
\operatorname{Re}(s)>1
we have

\displaystyle\Lambda(s,\overline{F}_{1};\psi,\ell)_{S}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\sum_{\mu\in S}\overline{\tau(\mu)}\psi(\lambda^{4}\mu)\overline{\mu}^{|\ell|}K_{\frac{1}{3}}(4\pi|\mu|v)v^{2s+|\ell|-1}dv

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(4\pi)^{2s+|\ell|}}\sum_{\mu\in S}\frac{\overline{\tau(\mu)}\psi(\lambda^{4}\mu)\big(\frac{\overline{\mu}}{|\mu|}\big)^{|\ell|}}{N(\mu)^{s}}\int_{0}^{\infty}K_{\frac{1}{3}}(T)T^{2s+|\ell|-1}dT

(5.45)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}(2\pi)^{-2s-|\ell|}\Gamma\Big(s+\frac{|\ell|}{2}-\frac{1}{6}\Big)\Gamma\Big(s+\frac{|\ell|}{2}+\frac{1}{6}\Big)\sum_{\mu\in S}\frac{\overline{\tau(\mu)}\psi(\lambda^{4}\mu)\big(\frac{\mu}{|\mu|}\big)^{\ell}}{N(\mu)^{s}}.

The interchange of summation and integration above for
\operatorname{Re}(s)>1
is justified by absolute convergence
(cf.
[
DLMF
, (10.25.3),(10.30.2)]
). Furthermore,
(
5.45
) follows from
[
DLMF
, (10.43.19)]
.
∎

Proposition 5.1
.

The completed Dirichlet series
\Lambda(\overline{F_{1}},s;\psi)_{S}
admits a meromorphic continuation to the whole complex plane
\mathbb{C}
. It has a unique pole
(that is simple) at
s=5/6
, with residue

(5.46)

\underset{s=\tfrac{5}{6}}{\text{Res }}\Lambda(s,\overline{F_{1}};\psi)_{S}=\frac{\sigma\widetilde{\psi}(0)}{6N(r)^{5/3}},

where
\sigma=3^{5/2}/2
is as in (
5.6
).
In particular,

(5.47)

\underset{s=\tfrac{5}{6}}{\text{Res }}\mathcal{D}(s,\overline{F_{1}};\psi)_{S}=\frac{2(2\pi)^{5/3}\sigma\widetilde{\psi}(0)}{3\Gamma(\tfrac{2}{3})N(r)^{5/3}}.

For
\ell\neq 0
the Dirichlet series
\Lambda(\overline{F_{1}},s;\psi,\ell)_{S}
is entire.
Moreover, for all
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
we have the functional equation

(5.48)

3(-1)^{\ell}N(r)^{2s}\Big(\frac{\overline{r}}{r}\Big)^{\ell}\Lambda(s,\overline{F_{1}};\psi,\ell)_{S}=\Lambda(1-s,\overline{F^{\star}_{1}}+\omega\overline{F^{\star}_{19}}+\omega^{2}\overline{F^{\star}_{10}};\widetilde{\psi},-\ell).

This functional equation also determines the poles of
\Lambda(s,\overline{F^{\star}_{1}}+\omega\overline{F^{\star}_{19}}+\omega^{2}\overline{F^{\star}_{10}};\widetilde{\psi})
.

Proof.

For
\operatorname{Re}s>1
we have

(5.49)

\Lambda(s,\overline{F_{1}};\psi,\ell)_{S}=\int_{0}^{N(r)^{-1}}\overline{F_{1}}(v;\psi,\ell)_{S}v^{2s+|\ell|-2}dv+\int_{N(r)^{-1}}^{\infty}\overline{F_{1}}(v;\psi,\ell)_{S}v^{2s+|\ell|-2}dv.

When
\ell=0
observe that
\overline{F_{1}}(v;\psi,\ell)_{S}
has exponential decay at
\infty
by (
5.17
)
and (
5.24
). The same claim holds when
\ell\neq 0

using (
5.43
) (termwise differentiation of the Fourier series) and the same reasoning as before.
Thus the second integral in (
5.49
) has analytic continuation
to an entire function.

Let

(5.50)

\overline{G}(w;\widetilde{\psi},\ell):=\begin{cases}(\overline{F_{1}}+\omega\overline{F^{\star}_{19}}+\omega^{2}\overline{F^{\star}_{10}})(w;\tilde{\psi},\ell)&\text{ if }\ell=0\\
(\overline{F_{1}^{\star}}+\omega\overline{F^{\star}_{19}}+\omega^{2}\overline{F^{\star}_{10}})(w;\tilde{\psi},\ell)&\text{ if }\ell\neq 0\end{cases},\quad w\in\mathbb{H}^{3}.

Using (
5.50
) and Corollary
5.1
gives

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{N(r)^{-1}}

\displaystyle\overline{F_{1}}(v;\psi,\ell)_{S}v^{2s+|\ell|-2}dv

(5.51)

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{\ell}}{3N(r)^{1+|\ell|}}\Big(\frac{\overline{r}}{r}\Big)^{-\ell}\int_{0}^{N(r)^{-1}}\overline{G}\Big(\frac{1}{v|r|^{2}};\tilde{\psi},-\ell\Big)v^{2s-|\ell|-2}dv

\displaystyle=\frac{\sigma\widetilde{\psi}(0)N(r)^{-2s}}{6}\cdot\frac{\delta_{\ell=0}}{s-\tfrac{5}{6}}

(5.52)

\displaystyle+\frac{(-1)^{\ell}N(r)^{-2s}}{3}\Big(\frac{\overline{r}}{r}\Big)^{-\ell}\int_{1}^{\infty}\Big(\overline{F_{1}^{\star}}+\omega\overline{F_{19}^{\star}}+\omega^{2}\overline{F_{10}^{\star}}\Big)(v;\widetilde{\psi},-\ell)v^{|\ell|-2s}dv.

When
\ell=0
observe that
(\overline{F_{1}^{\star}}+\omega\overline{F^{\star}_{19}}+\omega^{2}\overline{F^{\star}_{10}})(\cdot,\widetilde{\psi},-\ell)
has exponential decay at
\infty
by (
5.19
), Appendix
A
(the expressions for
d_{j}(\mu)
), (
5.7
), (
5.13
), and (
5.14
). The same claim holds when
\ell\neq 0

using (
5.43
) (termwise differentiation of the Fourier series)
and the same reasoning as before. Thus the integral in (
5.52
) has analytic continuation to an entire function. This gives the meromorphicity and entirety claims in the Proposition, as well as (
5.46
). Observe that (
5.47
) follows from (
5.46
) and Lemma
5.3
.

We now prove the functional equation (
5.48
).
From (
5.49
) and (
5.51
) we found that

(5.53)

\displaystyle\Lambda(s,\overline{F_{1}};\psi,\ell)_{S}=\frac{(-1)^{\ell}N(r)^{-2s}}{3}\Big(\frac{\overline{r}}{r}\Big)^{-\ell}\int_{1}^{\infty}\overline{G}(v;\widetilde{\psi},-\ell)v^{|\ell|-2s}dv+\int_{N(r)^{-1}}^{\infty}\overline{F_{1}}(v;\psi,\ell)_{S}v^{2s+|\ell|-2}dv.

We now repeat a similar argument, but instead start with

(5.54)

\overline{G^{\star}}(w;\widetilde{\psi},\ell)=(\overline{F_{1}^{\star}}+\omega\overline{F_{19}^{\star}}+\omega^{2}\overline{F_{10}^{\star}})(w;\widetilde{\psi},\ell),\quad\text{for all}\quad w\in\mathbb{H}^{3}\quad\text{and}\quad\ell\in\mathbb{Z}.

For
\operatorname{Re}s>1
we have

(5.55)

\Lambda(s,\overline{G^{\star}};\widetilde{\psi},-\ell)=\int_{0}^{1}\overline{G^{\star}}(v;\widetilde{\psi},-\ell)v^{2s+|\ell|-2}dv+\int_{1}^{\infty}\overline{G^{\star}}(v;\widetilde{\psi},-\ell)v^{2s+|\ell|-2}dv.

For
\operatorname{Re}s>1
we have

(5.56)

\int_{0}^{1}\overline{G^{\star}}(v;\widetilde{\psi},-\ell)v^{2s+|\ell|-2}dv=\int_{0}^{1}\overline{G}(v;\widetilde{\psi},-\ell)v^{2s+|\ell|-2}dv-\delta_{\ell=0}\cdot\frac{3\sigma\widetilde{\psi}(0)}{6s-1}.

Then (
5.56
) holds for all
s\in\mathbb{C}
by meromorphic continuation. Similarly,
for
\operatorname{Re}s<-1
, we have

(5.57)

\int_{1}^{\infty}\overline{G^{\star}}(v;\widetilde{\psi},-\ell)v^{2s+|\ell|-2}dv=\int_{1}^{\infty}\overline{G}(v;\widetilde{\psi},-\ell)v^{2s+|\ell|-2}dv+\delta_{\ell=0}\frac{3\sigma\widetilde{\psi}(0)}{6s-1}.

Then (
5.57
) holds for all
s\in\mathbb{C}
by meromorphic continuation.
Insertion of (
5.56
) and (
5.57
) into (
5.55
) gives

(5.58)

\Lambda(s,\overline{G^{\star}};\widetilde{\psi},-\ell)=\int_{0}^{1}\overline{G}(v;\widetilde{\psi},-\ell)v^{2s+|\ell|-2}dv+\int_{1}^{\infty}\overline{G}(v;\widetilde{\psi},-\ell)v^{2s+|\ell|-2}dv,

where both integrals are to be interpreted as the meromorphic continuations of the original integrals.
Using Corollary
5.1
we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\overline{G}(v;\widetilde{\psi},-\ell)v^{2s+|\ell|-2}dv

\displaystyle=3(-1)^{\ell}N(r)^{1-|\ell|}\Big(\frac{\overline{r}}{r}\Big)^{\ell}\int_{0}^{1}\overline{F_{1}}\Big(\frac{1}{|r|^{2}v};\psi,\ell\Big)_{S}v^{2s-|\ell|-2}dv

(5.59)

\displaystyle=3(-1)^{\ell}N(r)^{2-2s}\Big(\frac{\overline{r}}{r}\Big)^{\ell}\int_{N(r)^{-1}}^{\infty}\overline{F_{1}}(v;\psi,\ell)_{S}v^{|\ell|-2s}dv.

Substitution of (
5.4
) into (
5.58
) gives

\Lambda(s,\overline{G^{\star}};\widetilde{\psi},-\ell)=3(-1)^{\ell}N(r)^{2-2s}\Big(\frac{\overline{r}}{r}\Big)^{\ell}\int_{N(r)^{-1}}^{\infty}\overline{F_{1}}(v;\psi,\ell)_{S}v^{|\ell|-2s}dv+\int_{1}^{\infty}\overline{G}(v;\widetilde{\psi},-\ell)v^{2s+|\ell|-2}dv.

Equivalently,

(5.60)

\Lambda(1-s,\overline{G^{\star}};\widetilde{\psi},-\ell)=3(-1)^{\ell}N(r)^{2s}\Big(\frac{\overline{r}}{r}\Big)^{\ell}\int_{N(r)^{-1}}^{\infty}\overline{F_{1}}(v;\psi,\ell)_{S}v^{2s+|\ell|-2}dv+\int_{1}^{\infty}\overline{G}(v;\widetilde{\psi},-\ell)v^{|\ell|-2s}dv.

After combining (
5.53
) and (
5.60
) we obtain

3(-1)^{\ell}N(r)^{2s}\Big(\frac{\overline{r}}{r}\Big)^{\ell}\Lambda(s,\overline{F_{1}};\psi,\ell)_{S}=\Lambda(1-s,\overline{G^{\star}};\widetilde{\psi},-\ell),

as required.
∎

5.5.
Sieving for
\tilde{g}(c)

Let
r\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
satisfy
r\equiv 1\pmod{3}
,
\psi
be a primitive character to modulus
r
,
and
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
. Then let

\zeta_{\mathbb{Q}(\omega)}(s;\psi,\ell):=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}\frac{\psi(d)\big(\frac{d}{|d|}\big)^{\ell}}{N(d)^{s}},\quad\operatorname{Re}(s)>1.

In the case
\ell=0
we omit
\ell
from the notation and write
\zeta_{\mathbb{Q}(\omega)}(s;\psi)
.
We denote the principal character modulo
r
by
\bm{1}_{r}
.
Any
J\unlhd\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
with
(J,3)=1

has a unique generator
d\equiv 1\pmod{3}
.
Thus when
\ell=0
and
\psi=\mathbf{1}_{r}
, the above
L
-function coincides with the Dedekind
\zeta
-function of
\mathbb{Q}(\omega)
,
except at the local factors of primes dividing
(\lambda r)
. Note that

\zeta_{\mathbb{Q}(\omega)}(s;\psi,\ell)
has standard meromorphic continuation
to all of
\mathbb{C}
; the only case when this function is not holomorphic is when
\psi=\mathbf{1}_{r}
is the principal character and
\ell=0
, in that case there is a unique simple pole at
s=1
. Standard functional equations for these
L
-functions can be found in
[
Miy89
, §3.3]
.

Lemma 5.4
.

Let
r\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
with
r\equiv 1\pmod{3}
, and

\psi
be a primitive cubic Dirichlet character on
\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
to modulus
r
.
For
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
and

\operatorname{Re}s>1
we have

(5.61)

(-1)^{\ell}i^{-\ell}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}c\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}\frac{\tilde{g}(c)\psi(\lambda c)\big(\frac{c}{|c|}\big)^{\ell}}{N(c)^{s}}=\frac{3^{-5/2-3s}\cdot\mathcal{D}(s,\overline{F_{1}};\psi,\ell)_{S}}{\zeta_{\mathbb{Q}(\omega)}(3s-\tfrac{1}{2};\mathbf{1}_{r},3\ell)},

where
\mathcal{D}(s,\overline{F_{1}};\psi,\ell)_{S}
is as in (
5.4
).

Remark 5.4
.

Note that we have abused notation in the results and proofs that follow:
\mu\in\mathbb{Q}(\omega)

is used to index Fourier coefficients of various automorphic forms, and
\mu(\cdot)

denotes the Möbius function on
\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
. Meanings should be clear from context.

Proof.

The Dirichlet coefficients on
the right side on (
5.61
) have support contained
in
\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\setminus\{0\}
since
3^{-3s}=N(\lambda^{3})^{-s}
.
The
\nu
th Dirichlet coefficient on the right side of (
5.61
) is given by

(5.62)

\frac{1}{3^{5/2}}\Big(\frac{\nu/\lambda^{3}}{|\nu/\lambda^{3}|}\Big)^{\ell}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mu\in S\\
d\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
(d,r)=1\\
\nu=\lambda^{3}\mu d^{3}\end{subarray}}\overline{\tau(\mu)}\psi(\lambda^{4}\mu)\mu(d)|d|.

Recall the definition of
S
in (
5.17
).
If
\nu\notin\lambda^{3}S
, then (
5.62
) is zero.
Therefore we can assume that
\nu\in\lambda^{3}S
.
If
\mu\in S
and
\tau(\mu)\neq 0
, then by (
5.7
)
we must have

\overline{\tau(\mu)}=3^{5/2}\tilde{g}(e)|f|,

where

\mu=\lambda^{-3}ef^{3}\quad\text{for some}\quad e,f\equiv 1\pmod{3}\quad\text{and}\quad\mu^{2}(e)=1.

Thus (
5.62
) is equal to

\displaystyle(-1)^{\ell}i^{-\ell}\Big(\frac{\nu}{|\nu|}\Big)^{\ell}

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\begin{subarray}{c}e,f,d\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
(d,r)=1\\
\nu=e(df)^{3}\end{subarray}\end{subarray}}\tilde{g}(e)\psi(\lambda ef^{3})\mu(d)|d||f|

(5.63)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\ell}i^{-\ell}\Big(\frac{\nu}{|\nu|}\Big)^{\ell}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\begin{subarray}{c}e,f,d\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
(df,r)=1\\
\nu=e(df)^{3}\end{subarray}\end{subarray}}\tilde{g}(e)\psi(\lambda e)\mu(d)|d||f|,

where the last display follows from the assumption that
\psi
is a primitive cubic
character to modulus
r
. Note that it is redundant to have
\mu^{2}(e)=1

in (
5.5
) because this condition is automatically captured
by (
2.2
). Möbius inversion then tells us that
the right side of (
5.5
) is equal to

\displaystyle(-1)^{\ell}i^{-\ell}\Big(\frac{\nu}{|\nu|}\Big)^{\ell}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}e,u\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
(u,r)=1\\
\nu=eu^{3}\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\tilde{g}(e)\psi(\lambda e)|u|\Big(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d\mid u\\
d\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}\mu(d)\Big)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\ell}i^{-\ell}\Big(\frac{e}{|e|}\Big)^{\ell}\tilde{g}(e)\psi(\lambda e),

as required.
∎

The following lemma records the standard evaluation of Ramanujan sums over
\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
.
The lemma follows from the evaluation

\displaystyle c_{\varpi}(u)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\varphi(\varpi)&\quad\text{if}\quad u\equiv 0\pmod{\varpi}\\
-1&\quad\text{otherwise}\end{cases},

\displaystyle\text{for}\quad\varpi\equiv 1\pmod{3}\quad\text{prime}\quad\text{and}\quad u\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega],

and the multiplicativity of Ramanujan sums i.e.

\displaystyle c_{mn}(u)=c_{m}(u)c_{n}(u)\quad

\displaystyle\text{for}\quad u,m,n\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\quad\text{with}\quad m,n\equiv 1\pmod{3}

\displaystyle\text{and}\quad(m,n)=1.

Lemma 5.5
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
be squarefree and satisfy
m\equiv 1\pmod{3}
. Then for
u\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
,

c_{m}(u):=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\pmod{m}\\
(x,m)=1\end{subarray}}\check{e}\Big(\frac{ux}{m}\Big)=\mu\Big(\frac{m}{(m,u)}\Big)\frac{\varphi(m)}{\varphi\big(\frac{m}{(m,u)}\big)},

where
\varphi(\cdot)
is the Euler phi function on
\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
.

Let
r\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
be squarefree and satisfy
r\equiv 1\pmod{3}
.
We are now able to study the
the analytic properties of the Dirichlet series

(5.64)

\sum_{c\equiv 1\pmod{3}}\frac{\tilde{g}(c)\overline{\big(\frac{c}{r}\big)_{3}}\big(\frac{c}{|c|}\big)^{\ell}}{N(c)^{s}},\quad\operatorname{Re}(s)>1.

The following result records a level aspect (i.e. in the conductor of the cubic twist in (
5.64
)) functional equation for
(
5.64
) that generalises

[
Pat77
, Theorem 6.1]
. It explicates the root number and level, and is crucial
to our paper.
Yoshimoto
[
Yos87
]
established level aspect analogues
of
[
Pat77
, Theorem 6.1]
for twists of Gauss sums
by arbitrary non-cubic Dirichlet characters.
Clearly, Yoshimoto’s results do not cover the case
we need.

Remark 5.5
.

The functional equation in
[
Pat77
, Theorem 6.1]
could be potentially used to obtain a functional equation for (
5.64
) similar to the one presented below
in Proposition
5.2
(at least when
(r,c)=1
).
Patterson exploited the fact that (
5.64
) (and its variants) are the Fourier coefficients
of cubic metaplectic Eisenstein series attached to the essential cusps of
\Gamma_{1}(3)
with respect to
\chi
.
The drawback of the functional equation in
[
Pat77
, Theorem 6.1]
is that the dual side of is a linear combination of variants
of Dirichlet series of twisted cubic Gauss sums that involve the ramified prime, and this obscures the root number.
We found it advantageous to work directly with the automorphy of the cubic theta function (this is a more advanced starting point since
the purpose of
[
Pat77
]
was to compute the Fourier coefficients of the cubic theta function).

Proposition 5.2
.

Let
r\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
be squarefree and satisfy
r\equiv 1\pmod{3}
, and

\psi:=\overline{\big(\frac{\cdot}{r}\big)_{3}}
. Let
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
.
Then the Dirichlet series

(5.65)

\mathcal{R}(s;\psi,\ell):=\sum_{c\equiv 1\pmod{3}}\frac{\tilde{g}(c)\overline{\big(\frac{c}{r}\big)_{3}}\big(\frac{c}{|c|}\big)^{\ell}}{N(c)^{s}},\quad\operatorname{Re}(s)>1,

admits meromorphic continuation to all of
\mathbb{C}
. If
\ell=0
, the Dirichlet series

(5.66)

\zeta_{\mathbb{Q}(\omega)}(3s-\tfrac{1}{2};\mathbf{1}_{r},3\ell)\mathcal{R}(s;\psi,\ell)

has a unique pole located at
s=\tfrac{5}{6}
, and it is simple. If
\ell\neq 0
the Dirichlet series
(
5.66
) defines an entire function. We have

\text{Res}_{s=\frac{5}{6}}\big(\zeta_{\mathbb{Q}(\omega)}(3s-\tfrac{1}{2};\mathbf{1}_{r})\mathcal{R}(s;\psi)\big)=\frac{(2\pi)^{5/3}\overline{g(r)}\varphi(r)}{\Gamma(\tfrac{2}{3})3^{7/2}N(r)^{5/3}},

and for
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
we have the functional equation

\displaystyle\zeta_{\mathbb{Q}(\omega)}

\displaystyle(3s-\tfrac{1}{2};\mathbf{1}_{r},3\ell)\mathcal{R}(s;\psi,\ell)

\displaystyle=\overline{g(r)}\Big(\frac{\overline{r}}{r}\Big)^{-\ell}\cdot\frac{i^{\ell}(2\pi)^{4s-2}}{3^{7/2}N(r)^{2s}}\frac{\Gamma(\tfrac{5}{6}+\tfrac{|\ell|}{2}-s)\Gamma(\tfrac{7}{6}+\tfrac{|\ell|}{2}-s)}{\Gamma(s+\tfrac{|\ell|}{2}-\tfrac{1}{6})\Gamma(s+\tfrac{|\ell|}{2}+\tfrac{1}{6})}

\displaystyle\times\zeta_{\mathbb{Q}(\omega)}(\tfrac{5}{2}-3s;\mathbf{1}_{r},-3\ell)\mathcal{R}_{r}^{\dagger}(1-s;-\ell),

where

\mathcal{R}_{r}^{\dagger}(s;\ell):=(-1)^{\ell}i^{-\ell}\sum_{\nu\in\lambda^{-1}\mathbb{Z}[\omega]}\frac{a^{\dagger}(\nu)b_{r}^{\dagger}(\nu)\big(\frac{\nu}{|\nu|}\big)^{\ell}}{N(\nu)^{s}},\quad\operatorname{Re}(s)>1,\quad\ell\in\mathbb{Z},

for some Dirichlet coefficients
a^{\dagger}(\nu)
. The coefficients
a^{\dagger}(\nu)
have support contained
in the set

\displaystyle Q^{\dagger}:=\big\{\nu=\lambda^{L}\zeta

\displaystyle hw{h^{\prime}}^{3}:L\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq-1},\quad\zeta\in\{\pm 1,\pm\omega,\pm\omega^{2}\},

\displaystyle h,h^{\prime},w\equiv 1\pmod{3},\quad h,h^{\prime}\mid r^{\infty},\quad(w,r)=1\quad\text{and}\quad\mu^{2}(hw)=1\big\},

and for
\nu\in Q^{\dagger}
,

(5.67)

a^{\dagger}(\nu)=\begin{cases}\overline{\tau(-\lambda^{L-3}\nu)}&\text{if}\quad L\geq 0\\
\omega^{2}\overline{\tau_{1}(-\lambda^{L-3}\omega^{2}\nu)}\check{e}(\lambda^{L-3}\nu)&\text{if}\quad L=-1,\quad\zeta\in\{-1,-\omega,-\omega^{2}\}\\
\omega\overline{\tau_{2}(-\lambda^{L-3}\omega\nu)}\check{e}(\lambda^{L-3}\nu)&\text{if}\quad L=-1,\quad\zeta\in\{1,\omega,\omega^{2}\}\end{cases},

and

(5.68)

b_{r}^{\dagger}(\nu)=\mu\Big(\frac{r}{(\lambda\nu,r)}\Big)\frac{\varphi(r)}{\varphi\big(\frac{r}{(\lambda\nu,r)}\big)}.

Remark 5.6
.

Recall that
\tau(\cdot),\tau_{1}(\cdot)
and
\tau_{2}(\cdot)
are given in (
5.7
), (
5.13
) and
(
5.14
) respectively.

Proof.

Meromorphic continuation of

\zeta_{\mathbb{Q}(\omega)}(3s-\tfrac{1}{2};\mathbf{1}_{r},3\ell)\mathcal{R}(s;\psi,\ell)

to all of
\mathbb{C}
follows from Lemma
5.3
,
Proposition
5.1
and Lemma
5.4
.
If
\ell\neq 0
, then it is entire. If
\ell=0
,
then it has a unique simple pole at
s=5/6
with residue

\text{Res}_{s=\frac{5}{6}}\big(\zeta_{\mathbb{Q}(\omega)}(3s-\tfrac{1}{2};\mathbf{1}_{r})\mathcal{R}(s;\psi)\big)=\left(\frac{\lambda}{r}\right)_{3}\frac{(2\pi)^{5/3}\widetilde{\psi}(0)}{3^{7/2}\Gamma(\tfrac{2}{3})N(r)^{5/3}}.

We now evaluate
\widetilde{\psi}(u)
(defined in (
5.22
)). Recall from (
5.42
) and the argument following it that we have

(5.69)

\widetilde{\psi}(u)=\left(\frac{\lambda}{r}\right)_{3}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d\pmod{r}\\
(d,r)=1\\
(\lambda^{4}d)(\lambda^{4}a)\equiv 1\pmod{r}\end{subarray}}\widehat{\psi}(d)\Big(\frac{a}{r}\Big)_{3}\check{e}\Big(\frac{au}{r}\Big),\quad u\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega].

Moreover, using the definition (
5.21
) and the fact that
\psi
is primitive gives us

\widehat{\psi}(d)=\Big(\frac{d}{r}\Big)_{3}\overline{g(r)}.

We have
ad\equiv\overline{\lambda^{8}}\pmod{r}
in (
5.69
).
Therefore

(5.70)

\widetilde{\psi}(u)=\Big(\frac{\lambda^{2}}{r}\Big)_{3}\overline{g(r)}c_{r}(u),

where
c_{r}(\cdot)
denotes the usual Ramanujan sum.
In particular,

\widetilde{\psi}(0)=\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}}{r}\right)_{3}\overline{g(r)}\varphi(r).

Lemma
5.4
tells us that

\zeta_{\mathbb{Q}(\omega)}(3s-\tfrac{1}{2};\mathbf{1}_{r},3\ell)\mathcal{R}(s;\psi,\ell)=(-1)^{\ell}i^{\ell}\left(\frac{\lambda}{r}\right)_{3}3^{-5/2-3s}\mathcal{D}(s,\overline{F_{1}};\psi,\ell)_{S}.

Thus Lemma
5.3
and Proposition
5.1
imply that

\displaystyle\zeta_{\mathbb{Q}(\omega)}

\displaystyle(3s-\tfrac{1}{2};\mathbf{1}_{r},3\ell)\mathcal{R}(s;\psi,\ell)

\displaystyle=\Big(\frac{\overline{r}}{r}\Big)^{-\ell}\frac{i^{\ell}(2\pi)^{4s-2}}{3^{7/2}N(r)^{2s}}\frac{\Gamma(\tfrac{5}{6}+\tfrac{|\ell|}{2}-s)\Gamma(\tfrac{7}{6}+\tfrac{|\ell|}{2}-s)}{\Gamma(s+\tfrac{|\ell|}{2}-\tfrac{1}{6})\Gamma(s+\tfrac{|\ell|}{2}+\tfrac{1}{6})}\zeta_{\mathbb{Q}(\omega)}\big(\tfrac{5}{2}-3s;\mathbf{1}_{r},-3\ell\big)

(5.71)

\displaystyle\times\left(\frac{\lambda}{r}\right)_{3}3^{-3s}\frac{\mathcal{D}(1-s,\overline{G^{\star}};\widetilde{\psi},-\ell)}{\zeta_{\mathbb{Q}(\omega)}(\frac{5}{2}-3s;\mathbf{1}_{r},-3\ell)},

where
\overline{G^{\star}}(w;\widetilde{\psi},\ell)
is as in (
5.54
).
Observe that (
5.70
) gives

\left(\frac{\lambda}{r}\right)_{3}3^{-3s}\frac{\mathcal{D}(s,\overline{G^{\star}};\widetilde{\psi},-\ell)}{\zeta_{\mathbb{Q}(\omega)}(3s-\tfrac{1}{2};\mathbf{1}_{r},-3\ell)}=\overline{g(r)}\mathcal{R}_{r}^{\dagger}(s,-\ell),

where

(5.72)

\mathcal{R}_{r}^{\dagger}(s;\ell):=3^{-3s}\frac{\mathcal{D}(s,\overline{G^{\star}};c_{r}(\cdot),\ell)}{\zeta_{\mathbb{Q}(\omega)}(3s-\tfrac{1}{2};\mathbf{1}_{r},3\ell)},\quad\ell\in\mathbb{Z}.

We now analyse the Dirichlet coefficients of
\mathcal{R}_{r}^{\dagger}(s;-\ell)
.
Let
a_{-\ell}^{\dagger}(\nu)
and
a_{-\ell}^{\star}(\nu)
be the Fourier coefficients of

\mathcal{R}_{r}^{\dagger}(s;-\ell)

and
\mathcal{D}(s,\overline{G^{\star}};-\ell)
respectively.
Using the definition of
G^{\star}(w;\widetilde{\psi},\ell)
in (
5.54
) and Appendix
A
for the closed form expressions for
d_{1}(\mu),d_{19}(\mu)
, and
d_{10}(\mu)
gives

\displaystyle a_{-\ell}^{\star}(\mu)

\displaystyle=\Big(\frac{\mu}{|\mu|}\Big)^{-\ell}\big(\overline{d_{1}(-\mu)}+\omega\overline{d_{19}(-\mu)}+\omega^{2}\overline{d_{10}(-\mu)}\big)

(5.73)

\displaystyle=\Big(\frac{\mu}{|\mu|}\Big)^{-\ell}\Big(\overline{\tau(-\mu)}+\omega^{2}\overline{\tau_{1}(-\omega^{2}\mu)}\check{e}(\mu)+\omega\overline{\tau_{2}(-\omega\mu)}\check{e}(\mu)\Big).

Consultation with (
5.7
), (
5.13
) and (
5.14
)
shows that the
a^{\star}(\mu)
have support contained in the set

U:=\big\{\mu=\lambda^{k}\zeta cj^{3}:k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq-4},\quad\zeta\in\{\pm 1,\pm\omega,\pm\omega^{2}\},\quad c,j\equiv 1\pmod{3}\quad\text{and}\quad\mu^{2}(c)=1\big\}.

Each of the three terms in (
5.5
) have disjoint support.
In particular,

\displaystyle a_{-\ell}^{\star}(\mu)

\displaystyle=\Big(\frac{\mu}{|\mu|}\Big)^{-\ell}\cdot\begin{cases}\overline{\tau(-\mu)}&\text{if}\quad k\geq-3\\
\omega^{2}\overline{\tau_{1}(-\omega^{2}\mu)}\check{e}(\mu)&\text{if}\quad k=-4,\quad\zeta\in\{-1,-\omega,-\omega^{2}\}\\
\omega\overline{\tau_{2}(-\omega\mu)}\check{e}(\mu)&\text{if}\quad k=-4,\quad\zeta\in\{1,\omega,\omega^{2}\}\end{cases}

(5.74)

\displaystyle=:\Big(\frac{\mu}{|\mu|}\Big)^{-\ell}a^{\star}(\mu).

Observe that (
5.72
) and (
5.5
)
imply that the coefficients
a^{\dagger}(\cdot)
have support contained in
\lambda^{3}U\subset\lambda^{-1}\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\setminus\{0\}
.
Then

\displaystyle a_{-\ell}^{\dagger}(\nu)

\displaystyle=\Big(\frac{\nu/\lambda^{3}}{|\nu/\lambda^{3}|}\Big)^{-\ell}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu=\lambda^{3}\mu d^{3}\\
\mu\in U\\
(d,r)=1\\
d\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}a^{\star}(\mu)c_{r}(\lambda^{4}\mu)\mu(d)|d|

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\ell}i^{\ell}\Big(\frac{\nu}{|\nu|}\Big)^{-\ell}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu=\lambda^{3}\mu d^{3}\\
\mu\in U\\
(d,r)=1\\
d\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}a^{\star}(\mu)c_{r}(\lambda^{4}\mu)\mu(d)|d|.

Evaluation of the Ramanujan sum using
Lemma
5.5
gives

(5.75)

a_{-\ell}^{\dagger}(\nu)=(-1)^{\ell}i^{\ell}\Big(\frac{\nu}{|\nu|}\Big)^{-\ell}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu=\lambda^{3}\mu d^{3}\\
\mu\in U\\
(d,r)=1\\
d\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}a^{\star}(\mu)\mu\Big(\frac{r}{(\lambda^{4}\mu,r)}\Big)\frac{\varphi(r)}{\varphi\big(\frac{r}{(\lambda^{4}\mu,r)}\big)}\mu(d)|d|.

To continue the evaluation of
a^{\dagger}(\nu)
in (
5.75
),
we write each
\mu\in U
occurring on the right side
uniquely as

\displaystyle\lambda^{4}\mu

\displaystyle=\lambda^{k+4}\zeta hw(h^{\prime}w^{\prime})^{3}\quad\text{with}\quad\zeta\in\{\pm 1,\pm\omega,\pm\omega^{2}\},

\displaystyle\quad h,h^{\prime}\mid r^{\infty},\quad(ww^{\prime},r)=1,\quad h,h^{\prime},w,w^{\prime}\equiv 1\pmod{3}\quad\text{and}\quad\mu^{2}(hw)=1.

Then

(5.76)

a_{-\ell}^{\dagger}(\nu)=(-1)^{\ell}i^{\ell}\Big(\frac{\nu}{|\nu|}\Big)^{-\ell}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu=\lambda^{k+3}\zeta hw(h^{\prime}w^{\prime}d)^{3}\\
h,h^{\prime},w,w^{\prime},d\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
\mu^{2}(hw)=1\\
h,h^{\prime}\mid r^{\infty}\\
(dww^{\prime},r)=1\end{subarray}}a^{\star}\big(\lambda^{k}\zeta hw(h^{\prime}w^{\prime})^{3}\big)\mu\Big(\frac{r}{(h{h^{\prime}}^{3},r)}\Big)\frac{\varphi(r)}{\varphi\big(\frac{r}{(h{h^{\prime}}^{3},r)}\big)}\mu(d)|d|.

Furthermore, (
5.5
) tells us that

\displaystyle a^{\star}\big(

\displaystyle\lambda^{k}\zeta hw(h^{\prime}w^{\prime})^{3}\big)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\overline{\tau\big({-\lambda^{k}\zeta hw(h^{\prime}w^{\prime})^{3}}\big)}&\text{if}\quad k\geq-3\\
\omega^{2}\overline{\tau_{1}\big({-\lambda^{k}\omega^{2}\zeta hw(h^{\prime}w^{\prime}})^{3}\big)}\check{e}\big(\lambda^{k}\zeta hw(h^{\prime}w^{\prime})^{3}\big)&\text{if}\quad k=-4,\quad\zeta\in\{-1,-\omega,-\omega^{2}\}\\
\omega\overline{\tau_{2}\big({-\lambda^{k}\omega\zeta hw(h^{\prime}w^{\prime})^{3}\big)}}\check{e}\big(\lambda^{k}\zeta hw(h^{\prime}w^{\prime})^{3}\big)&\text{if}\quad k=-4,\quad\zeta\in\{1,\omega,\omega^{2}\}\end{cases}.

Further consultation with (
5.7
), (
5.13
) and (
5.14
) shows that

(5.77)

a^{\star}\big(\lambda^{k}\zeta hw(h^{\prime}w^{\prime})^{3}\big)=b_{k,\zeta}^{\star}(hw)\frac{|h^{\prime}w^{\prime}|}{|hw|},

for some sequence of coefficients
b^{\star}_{k,\zeta}(\cdot)
on squarefree elements of

\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
that are congruent to
1
(the sequence depends only on
k
and
\zeta
).
Using (
5.77
) in (
5.76
), we obtain

(5.78)

\displaystyle a_{-\ell}^{\dagger}(\nu)=(-1)^{\ell}i^{\ell}\Big(\frac{\nu}{|\nu|}\Big)^{-\ell}

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu=\lambda^{k+3}\zeta hw(h^{\prime}u)^{3}\\
h,h^{\prime},w,u\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
\mu^{2}(hw)=1\\
h,h^{\prime}\mid r^{\infty}\\
(uw,r)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{b^{\star}_{k,\zeta}(hw)}{|hw|}|h^{\prime}u|\mu\Big(\frac{r}{(h{h^{\prime}}^{3},r)}\Big)\frac{\varphi(r)}{\varphi\big(\frac{r}{(h{h^{\prime}}^{3},r)}\big)}

\displaystyle\times\Big(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d\mid u\\
d\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}\mu(d)\Big).

Möbius inversion tells us that
u=1
in (
5.78
).
Subsequent use of (
5.77
) (in reverse)
gives

(5.79)

a_{-\ell}^{\dagger}(\nu)=(-1)^{\ell}i^{\ell}\Big(\frac{\nu}{|\nu|}\Big)^{-\ell}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu=\lambda^{k+3}\zeta hw{h^{\prime}}^{3}\\
h,h^{\prime},w\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
\mu^{2}(hw)=1\\
h,h^{\prime}\mid r^{\infty}\\
(w,r)=1\end{subarray}}a^{\star}(\lambda^{k}\zeta hw{h^{\prime}}^{3})\mu\Big(\frac{r}{(h{h^{\prime}}^{3},r)}\Big)\frac{\varphi(r)}{\varphi\big(\frac{r}{(h{h^{\prime}}^{3},r)}\big)}.

For a given
\nu
, there is at most one summand on the right side on (
5.79
).
This completes the proof.
∎

5.6.
Voronoi formula

We are finally able to prove a variant of the Voronoi summation formula.

Proposition 5.3
.

Let
W
be a smooth Schwartz function, compactly supported in
(0,\infty)
. Let
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
.
Then for
X>0
we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}c,d\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
(d,r)=1\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle|d|\tilde{g}(c)\Big(\frac{cd^{3}}{|cd^{3}|}\Big)^{\ell}\cdot\overline{\Big(\frac{c}{r}\Big)_{3}}W\Big(\frac{N(cd^{3})}{X}\Big)

\displaystyle=\delta_{\ell=0}\cdot X^{5/6}\widetilde{W}\Big(\frac{5}{6}\Big)\frac{(2\pi)^{5/3}\varphi(r)\overline{g(r)}}{3^{7/2}\Gamma(\tfrac{2}{3})N(r)^{5/3}}

(5.80)

\displaystyle+\frac{\overline{g(r)}}{3^{7/2}(2\pi)^{2}}\Big(\frac{\overline{r}}{r}\Big)^{-\ell}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu\in\lambda^{-1}\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
d\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
(d,r)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{a^{\dagger}(\nu)b_{r}^{\dagger}(\nu)}{N(\nu)N(d)^{5/2}}\Big(\frac{d^{3}\nu}{|d^{3}\nu|}\Big)^{-\ell}\cdot\widecheck{W_{\ell}}\Big(\frac{(2\pi)^{4}N(d^{3}\nu)X}{N(r)^{2}}\Big),

where the
a^{\dagger}(\cdot)
and
b_{r}^{\dagger}(\cdot)
are as in (
5.67
) and (
5.68
) respectively, and

\widecheck{W_{\ell}}:\mathbb{R}_{>0}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
is defined by

(5.81)

\widecheck{W_{\ell}}(u):=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{-\varepsilon-i\infty}^{-\varepsilon+i\infty}u^{s}\frac{\Gamma(\tfrac{5}{6}+\tfrac{|\ell|}{2}-s)\Gamma(\tfrac{7}{6}+\tfrac{|\ell|}{2}-s)}{\Gamma(s+\tfrac{|\ell|}{2}-\tfrac{1}{6})\Gamma(s+\tfrac{|\ell|}{2}+\tfrac{1}{6})}\widetilde{W}(s)ds,

for
\varepsilon\in(0,\frac{1}{10000})
.
For any
A>0
we have

(5.82)

\widecheck{W_{\ell}}(u)\ll_{W,A}(1+|\ell|)^{2}\cdot\begin{cases}(u/(1+\ell^{4}))^{5/6}&\text{ if }|u|\leq(1+\ell^{4})\\
(u/(1+\ell^{4}))^{-A}&\text{ if }|u|>(1+\ell^{4}).\end{cases}

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d,c\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
(d,r)=1\end{subarray}}|d|\tilde{g}(c)

\displaystyle\overline{\Big(\frac{c}{r}\Big)_{3}}\Big(\frac{cd^{3}}{|cd^{3}|}\Big)^{\ell}W\Big(\frac{N(cd^{3})}{X}\Big)

(5.83)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{2-i\infty}^{2+i\infty}\zeta_{\mathbb{Q}(\omega)}(3s-\tfrac{1}{2};\mathbf{1}_{r},3\ell)\mathcal{R}(s;\psi,\ell)X^{s}\widetilde{W}(s)ds,

where
R(s;\psi,\ell)
is given in (
5.65
).
We shift the contour to
\operatorname{Re}s=-\varepsilon
. Proposition
5.2
tells us that we collect a pole at
s=\tfrac{5}{6}
when
\ell=0
.
Thus (
5.83
) is equal to

(5.84)

\delta_{\ell=0}\cdot X^{5/6}\widetilde{W}\Big(\frac{5}{6}\Big)\frac{(2\pi)^{5/3}\overline{g(r)}\varphi(r)}{3^{7/2}\Gamma(\tfrac{2}{3})N(r)^{5/3}}+\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{-\varepsilon-i\infty}^{-\varepsilon+i\infty}\zeta_{\mathbb{Q}(\omega)}(3s-\tfrac{1}{2};\mathbf{1}_{r},3\ell)\mathcal{R}(s;\psi,\ell)X^{s}\widetilde{W}(s)ds.

We evaluate the integral in (
5.84
) by applying the functional equation in Proposition
5.2
.
We obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{-\varepsilon-i\infty}^{-\varepsilon+i\infty}\zeta_{\mathbb{Q}(\omega)}(3s-\tfrac{1}{2};\mathbf{1}_{r},3\ell)\mathcal{R}(s;\psi,\ell)X^{s}\widetilde{W}(s)ds

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\cdot\frac{i^{\ell}\overline{g(r)}}{3^{7/2}}\Big(\frac{\overline{r}}{r}\Big)^{-\ell}\int_{-\varepsilon-i\infty}^{-\varepsilon+i\infty}\frac{(2\pi)^{4s-2}}{N(r)^{2s}}\frac{\Gamma(\tfrac{5}{6}+\tfrac{|\ell|}{2}-s)\Gamma(\tfrac{7}{6}+\tfrac{|\ell|}{2}-s)}{\Gamma(s+\tfrac{|\ell|}{2}-\tfrac{1}{6})\Gamma(s+\tfrac{|\ell|}{2}+\tfrac{1}{6})}

(5.85)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\zeta_{\mathbb{Q}(\omega)}(\tfrac{5}{2}-3s;\mathbf{1}_{r},-3\ell)\mathcal{R}^{\dagger}_{r}(1-s,-\ell)X^{s}\widetilde{W}(s)ds.

Expanding the absolutely convergent series we see that (
5.6
) is equal to

\displaystyle\frac{\overline{g(r)}}{3^{7/2}(2\pi)^{2}}\Big(\frac{\overline{r}}{r}\Big)^{-\ell}

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu\in\lambda^{-1}\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
d\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
(d,r)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{a^{\dagger}(\nu)b_{r}^{\dagger}(\nu)\big(\frac{d^{3}\nu}{|d^{3}\nu|}\big)^{-\ell}}{N(\nu)N(d)^{5/2}}

\displaystyle\times\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{-\varepsilon-i\infty}^{-\varepsilon+i\infty}\Big(\frac{(2\pi)^{4}N(d^{3}\nu)X}{N(r)^{2}}\Big)^{s}\frac{\Gamma(\tfrac{5}{6}+\tfrac{|\ell|}{2}-s)\Gamma(\tfrac{7}{6}+\tfrac{|\ell|}{2}-s)}{\Gamma(s+\tfrac{|\ell|}{2}-\tfrac{1}{6})\Gamma(s+\tfrac{|\ell|}{2}+\tfrac{1}{6})}\cdot\widetilde{W}(s)ds.

The above display can be expressed as

\frac{\overline{g(r)}}{3^{7/2}(2\pi)^{2}}\Big(\frac{\overline{r}}{r}\Big)^{-\ell}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu\in\lambda^{-1}\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
d\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
(d,r)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{a^{\dagger}(\nu)b_{r}^{\dagger}(\nu)\big(\frac{d^{3}\nu}{|d^{3}\nu|}\big)^{-\ell}}{N(\nu)N(d)^{5/2}}\cdot\widecheck{W}_{\ell}\Big(\frac{(2\pi)^{4}N(d^{3}\nu)X}{N(r)^{2}}\Big).

This establishes (
5.3
). The decay bound (
5.82
) follows from a direct computation with Stirling’s asymptotic

[
DLMF
, (5.11.1)]
.

∎

6.
Cancellations in sequences over primes

We begin with a remark about ordering integral ideals in number fields.

Remark 6.1
.

Let
K
be an algebraic number field of degree
n
, and let
\mathfrak{a}
and
\mathfrak{b}
denote integral ideals of
K
.
The order on integral ideals of
K
,
<_{K}
, will essentially be given by the norm
N_{K/\mathbb{Q}}
. That is,
N_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(\mathfrak{a})<N_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(\mathfrak{b})
implies
\mathfrak{a}<_{K}\mathfrak{b}
. For ideals with equal norm the ordering can be arbitrary. For instance, if

N_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(\mathfrak{a})=N_{K/\mathbb{Q}}(\mathfrak{b})
and
\tilde{a}
and
\tilde{b}
are some fixed choice of
ideal numbers of

\mathfrak{a}
and
\mathfrak{b}
respectively, then one could declare that

\mathfrak{a}<_{K}\mathfrak{b}
if and only if
\arg\tilde{a}<\arg\tilde{b}
.
We will abuse notation and denote
<_{\mathbb{Q}(\omega)}
by
<
when the meaning is clear from context

Lemma 6.1
.

Assume the Generalized Riemann Hypothesis for the Dedekind zeta function attached to
\mathbb{Q}(\omega)
twisted by
Größencharaktern.
Let
W
be a smooth test function with compact support in
(0,\infty)
and
m_{W}:=\max\{1,\max_{x\in(0,\infty)}|W(x)|\}
.
Let
B\geq 10
,
10\leq w\leq M\leq N\ll B
,
and
\pi\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
satisfy
\pi\equiv 1\pmod{3}

be a prime or
1
.
If
R\leq\frac{\log B}{K\log\log B}
with
K>1000
, then the sequence

\beta_{b}=\frac{1}{m_{W}}W\Big(\frac{N(b)}{B}\Big)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b=\prod_{j=1}^{R}\varpi_{j}\\
\varpi_{j}\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
\varpi_{1}<\varpi_{2}<\ldots<\varpi_{R}\\
M\leq N(\varpi_{j})\leq N\\
(b,\pi)=1\end{subarray}}1

belongs to
\mathcal{C}_{\eta}(B,w)
(given in Definition
3.1
) for all
\eta>\frac{100}{K}
.

Remark 6.2
.

Note that scaling
m_{W}
is a minor technicality introduced so that the given
\bm{\beta}
satisfies
the first axiom of Definition
3.1
.

Proof.

It is clear the first three properties in Definition
3.1
follow from definition
of
\bm{\beta}
.
After Mellin inversion of
W
,

\beta_{b}:=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\frac{1}{m_{W}}\int_{2-i\infty}^{2+i\infty}\widetilde{W}(v)B^{v}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b=\prod_{j=1}^{R}\varpi_{j}\\
M\leq\varpi_{1}<\varpi_{2}<\ldots<\varpi_{R}\leq N\\
\varpi_{j}\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
\varpi_{j}\neq\pi\end{subarray}}\prod_{j=1}^{R}\frac{1}{N(\varpi_{j})^{v}}dv,

it suffices to check the fourth property of Definition
3.1
for all
\eta\gg 1/K
.
For
t\in\mathbb{R}
,
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
and
k,u\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
with
k,u\equiv 1\pmod{3}
,
it suffices to estimate

(6.1)

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
u|b\end{subarray}}\beta_{b}\Big(\frac{b}{|b|}\Big)^{\ell}\Big(\frac{k}{b}\Big)_{3}N(b)^{it},

provided that
\ell\neq 0
, or if
\ell=0
, then provided that
k\neq\hbox to8.91pt{\vbox to8.91pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

\lxSVG@closescope }}}

\lxSVG@closescope {{}}{}{{}}{}{{}}
