\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}
.
Without loss of generality we can take
u=1
since the case
u\neq 1
reduces to this case after combinatorial manipulations.
Thus (
6.1
) (with
u=1
) is equal to

(6.2)

\frac{1}{2\pi i}\frac{1}{m_{W}}\int_{2-i\infty}^{2+i\infty}\widetilde{W}(v)B^{v}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}M\leq\varpi_{1}<\varpi_{2}<\ldots<\varpi_{R}\leq N\\
\varpi_{j}\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
\varpi_{j}\neq\pi\end{subarray}}\prod_{j=1}^{R}\frac{\big(\frac{k}{\varpi_{j}}\big)_{3}\big(\frac{\varpi_{j}}{|\varpi_{j}|}\big)^{\ell}}{N(\varpi_{j})^{v-it}}dv.

The Newton-Girard identity
[
Mac95
,
(2.14^{\prime})
]
implies that

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}M\leq\varpi_{1}<\varpi_{2}<\ldots<\varpi_{R}\leq N\\
\varpi_{j}\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
\varpi_{j}\neq\pi\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\prod_{j=1}^{R}\frac{\big(\frac{k}{\varpi_{j}}\big)_{3}\big(\frac{\varpi_{j}}{|\varpi_{j}|}\big)^{\ell}}{N(\varpi_{j})^{v-it}}

(6.3)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{R}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1},\ldots,m_{R}\geq 0\\
m_{1}+2m_{2}+\cdots+Rm_{R}=R\end{subarray}}\prod_{j=1}^{R}\frac{(-1)^{m_{j}}}{m_{j}!j^{m_{j}}}\Big(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}M\leq N(\varpi)\leq N\\
\varpi\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
\varpi\neq\pi\end{subarray}}\frac{\big(\frac{k}{\varpi}\big)^{j}_{3}\big(\frac{\varpi}{|\varpi|}\big)^{j\ell}}{N(\varpi)^{j(v-it)}}\Big)^{m_{j}}.

We can assume without loss of generality that
M
and
N
are half-integers. Using
[
Dav00
, pg. 105]
each sharp cut-off can be written as

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}M\leq N(\varpi)\leq N\\
\varpi\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
\varpi\neq\pi\end{subarray}}\frac{\big(\frac{k}{\varpi}\big)^{j}_{3}\big(\frac{\varpi}{|\varpi|}\big)^{j\ell}}{N(\varpi)^{j(v-it)}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(2\pi i)^{2}}\iint_{1/\log B-iB^{100}}^{1/\log B+iB^{100}}D\big(j(v-it)+s-w;\Big(\frac{k}{\cdot}\Big)^{j}_{3}\bm{1}_{\pi},j\ell\big)N^{s}M^{-w}\frac{dsdw}{sw}

(6.4)

\displaystyle+O(B^{-50})\quad\text{say},\quad\operatorname{Re}(v)=2,

where (for
\operatorname{Re}(s)>1/2
) we have

D\Big(s;\Big(\frac{k}{\cdot}\Big)^{j}_{3}\bm{1}_{\pi},j\ell\Big):=\log\zeta_{\mathbb{Q}(\omega)}(s;\Big(\frac{k}{\cdot}\Big)_{3}^{j}\bm{1}_{\pi},j\ell)-\sum_{m\geq 2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\varpi\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
\varpi\neq\pi\end{subarray}}\frac{\big(\frac{k}{\varpi}\big)^{mj}_{3}\big(\frac{\varpi}{|\varpi|}\big)^{mj\ell}}{mN(\varpi)^{ms}}.

We shift the
v
-contour in (
6.2
) to
\operatorname{Re}(v)=1/2+1/\log B
.
From (
6
), the Riemann hypothesis, and
[
IK04
, Theorem 5.19]
,
we deduce that (uniformly in
j\geq 1
)

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}M\leq N(\varpi)\leq N\\
\varpi\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
\varpi\neq\pi\end{subarray}}\frac{\big(\frac{k}{\varpi}\big)^{j}_{3}\big(\frac{\varpi}{|\varpi|}\big)^{j\ell}}{N(\varpi)^{j(v-it)}}\ll(\log^{2}B)\cdot\log^{2}\Big(2+(1+|v|+|t|)(1+|\ell|)N(k)B\Big),\operatorname{Re}v\geq\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{\log B}.

Substitution of this bound into (
6
) shows that for
\operatorname{Re}(v)\geq 1/2+1/\log B
we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}M\leq\varpi_{1}<\varpi_{2}<\ldots<\varpi_{R}\leq N\\
\varpi_{j}\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
\varpi_{j}\neq\pi\end{subarray}}\prod_{j=1}^{R}\frac{\big(\frac{k}{\varpi_{j}}\big)_{3}\big(\frac{\varpi_{j}}{|\varpi_{j}|}\big)^{\ell}}{N(\varpi_{j})^{v-it}}

\displaystyle\ll C^{R}(\log^{2R}B)\cdot\log^{2R}\Big(2+(1+|v|+|t|)(1+|\ell|)N(k)B\Big)

\displaystyle\ll(C/\varepsilon^{4})^{R}(\log^{2R}B^{\varepsilon})\cdot\log^{2R}\Big(2+\big((1+|v|+|t|)(1+|\ell|)N(k)B\big)^{\varepsilon}\Big),

for some absolute constant
C>1
and any fixed
\varepsilon>0
.
Returning to (
6.2
) (and recalling that we shifted the contour to
\operatorname{Re}(v)=1/2+1/\log B
)
we see that (
6.1
) is

(6.5)

\ll(C/\varepsilon^{4})^{R}B^{1/2+1/\log B}(\log^{2R}B^{\varepsilon})\cdot\log^{2R}\big(2+\big((1+|t|)(1+|\ell|)N(k)B\big)^{\varepsilon}\big).

We use the hypothesis
1\leq R\leq\log B/(K\log\log B)
, the inequality

(\log x)^{L}\leq L!x,\quad x\geq 1,\quad L\geq 1,

and Stirling’s asymptotic formula
[
DLMF
, (5.11.1)]

to conclude that (
6.5
) is

\ll_{\varepsilon}B^{1/2+100/K+3\varepsilon}(1+|t|)^{\varepsilon}(1+|\ell|)^{\varepsilon}N(k)^{\varepsilon},

say. This concludes the proof.
∎

A minor variation of the above proof gives a smoothed version of the Lemma.

Lemma 6.2
.

Assume the Generalized Riemann Hypothesis for the Dedekind zeta function attached to
\mathbb{Q}(\omega)
twisted by
Größencharaktern.
Let
V
and
W
be a smooth test functions with compact support in
(0,\infty)
, and let
m_{V}
and
m_{W}
be as in
Lemma
6.1
. Let
R\in\mathbb{N}
,
B\geq 10
,
10\leq w\leq M\leq N\ll B
,
and
P_{1},\ldots,P_{R}>1
be such that
P_{1}\cdots P_{R}\asymp B
.
If
R\leq\frac{\log B}{K\log\log B}
with
K>1000
, then the sequence

\beta_{b}=\frac{1}{m_{W}}W\Big(\frac{N(b)}{B}\Big)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b=\prod_{j=1}^{R}\varpi_{j}\\
\varpi_{j}\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
\varpi_{1}<\varpi_{2}<\ldots<\varpi_{R}\\
M\leq N(\varpi_{j})\leq N\end{subarray}}\prod_{j=1}^{R}\Big(\frac{1}{m_{V}}V\Big(\frac{N(\varpi_{j})}{P_{j}}\Big)\Big),

belongs to
\mathcal{C}_{\eta}(B,w)
(given in Definition
3.1
) for all
\eta>\frac{100}{K}
.

Remark 6.3
.

A sum over
R
running through any subset of
[1,\log B/(K\log\log B)]

can be introduced in the definition of
\bm{\beta}
occurring
in both Lemma
6.1
and Lemma
6.2
with no change to the conclusions.

Lemma 6.3
.

Suppose
A,B\geq 10
,
X:=AB
,
0<\eta_{1},\eta_{2}\leq 1/4
and
w_{1}>w_{2}\geq 10
.
Let
\bm{\alpha}=(\alpha_{a})\in\mathcal{C}_{\eta_{1}}(A,w_{1})
, and

\bm{\beta}=(\beta_{b})\in\mathcal{C}_{\eta_{2}}(B,w_{2})
be
such that

(6.6)

\beta_{b}\neq 0\implies(\varpi\mid b\implies w_{2}<N(\varpi)\leq w_{1}).

Let
10\leq M\leq N

and
\bm{\gamma}:=(\gamma_{c})
be given by

\gamma_{c}:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}M\leq N(c)\leq N\\
a,b\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
a,b\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
ab=c\end{subarray}}\alpha_{a}\beta_{b}.

Then
\bm{\gamma}=(\gamma_{c})\in\mathcal{C}_{\max\{\eta_{1},\eta_{2}\}}(X,w_{2})
.

Remark 6.4
.

It will be helpful to recall the definition of
\mathscr{P}(z)
in (
3.1
).

Proof.

Observe that the hypotheses (
6.6
) and
w_{2}<w_{1}
imply that

\bm{\gamma}=(\gamma_{c})
is supported on squarefree
w_{2}
-rough integers. We also
have
\gamma_{c}\neq 0\implies N(c)\asymp X
from the supports of
\bm{\alpha}=(\alpha_{a})
and

\bm{\beta}=(\beta_{b})
. Each
c\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
with
c\equiv 1\pmod{3}
has a unique factorisation
c=ab

with
(a,\mathscr{P}(w_{1}))=1
and
b\mid\mathscr{P}(w_{1})
. Thus hypothesis (
6.6
) implies that

|\gamma_{c}|=|\alpha_{a}\beta_{b}|\leq 1
.

It only remains to prove inequality (
3.2
) in
Definition
3.1
for
\bm{\gamma}=(\gamma_{c})
.
Without loss of generality we can assume that
M
and
N
are half-integers and that
M\asymp X
(resp.
N\asymp X
), otherwise
\delta_{M\leq N(c)}
(resp.
\delta_{N(c)\leq N}
) is a redundant condition. Using
[
Dav00
, pg. 105]
, the sharp cut-off can be written as

\delta_{M\leq N(c)\leq N}=\frac{1}{(2\pi i)^{2}}\iint_{1/\log X-iX^{100}}^{1/\log X+iX^{100}}\frac{N^{s}M^{-w}}{N(c)^{s-w}}\frac{dsdw}{sw}+O(X^{-50}),

say. The integrals incur an acceptable loss of
O((\log X)^{2})
. Thus it suffices to show that

\tilde{\gamma}_{c}:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a,b\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
a,b\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
ab=c\end{subarray}}\alpha_{a}\beta_{b}

satisfies (
3.2
).
In other words, for
t\in\mathbb{R}
,
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
and
k,u\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
with
k,u\equiv 1\pmod{3}
,
we need to estimate

(6.7)

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}c\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
u|c\end{subarray}}\tilde{\gamma}_{c}\Big(\frac{c}{|c|}\Big)^{\ell}\Big(\frac{k}{c}\Big)_{3}N(c)^{it},

provided that
\ell\neq 0
, or if
\ell=0
, then provided that
k\neq\hbox to8.91pt{\vbox to8.91pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}

.
Observe
u
has a unique factorisation
u=u_{1}u_{2}
such that

(u_{1},\mathscr{P}(w_{1}))=1
and
u_{2}\mid\mathscr{P}(w_{1})
.
Hypothesis (
6.6
) implies that that (
6.7
) is equal to

(6.8)

\Big(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\\
u_{1}\mid a\end{subarray}}\alpha_{a}\Big(\frac{a}{|a|}\Big)^{\ell}\Big(\frac{k}{a}\Big)_{3}N(a)^{it}\Big)\Big(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\\
u_{2}\mid b\end{subarray}}\beta_{b}\Big(\frac{b}{|b|}\Big)^{\ell}\Big(\frac{k}{b}\Big)_{3}N(b)^{it}\Big).

Since
\bm{\alpha}=(\alpha_{a})\in\mathcal{C}_{\eta_{1}}(A,w_{1})
and

\bm{\beta}=(\beta_{b})\in\mathcal{C}_{\eta_{2}}(B,w_{2})
, we see that (
6.8
)
is

\displaystyle\ll_{\varepsilon}(1+|\ell|)^{\varepsilon}N(k)^{\varepsilon}(1+|t|)^{\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{A}{N(u_{1})}\Big)^{1/2+\eta_{1}+\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{B}{N(u_{2})}\Big)^{1/2+\eta_{2}+\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\ll_{\varepsilon}(1+|\ell|)^{\varepsilon}N(k)^{\varepsilon}(1+|t|)^{\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{X}{N(u)}\Big)^{1/2+\max\{\eta_{1},\eta_{2}\}+\varepsilon},

as required.

∎

7.
Narrow Type II/III estimates

We establish estimates for type-II/III sums that are useful
in narrow ranges
corresponding to two or three variables of equal size respectively.
In the three variable case, two variables are clumped together
to reduce to a type-II analysis.
These estimates will be
in ranges where sharp bounds are required (but not asymptotics).

7.1.
Sieve weights

We will need to use auxiliary sieve weights in the proof of our narrow range bounds.

Lemma 7.1
.

Given
w\geq y^{2}>1
, there exists coefficients
(\lambda_{d})_{d\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]}
such that

(1)

\lambda_{1}=1
and
|\lambda_{d}|\ll_{\varepsilon}N(d)^{\varepsilon}
for all
d\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
and all
\varepsilon>0
;

(2)

\lambda_{d}=0
if
N(d)>y^{2}
or
d\not\equiv 1\pmod{3}
;

(3)

For all
n\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
we have

(7.1)

\delta_{(n,\mathscr{P}(w))=1}\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d\mid n\\
d\mid\mathscr{P}(w)\\
N(d)\leq y^{2}\end{subarray}}\lambda_{d};

(4)

They satisfy

(7.2)

\sum_{d\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]}\frac{\lambda_{d}}{N(d)}\ll\frac{1}{\log y}.

Proof.

Given
d\equiv 1\pmod{3}
, define

\lambda_{d}:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}N(e),N(f)\leq y\\
e,f\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
d=[e,f]\end{subarray}}\mu(e)\mu(f)\Big(1-\frac{\log N(e)}{\log y}\Big)\Big(1-\frac{\log N(f)}{\log y}\Big).

Properties (
1
) and (
2
) are immediate from the definition.
Property (
3
) follows from

(7.3)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d\mid n\\
d\mid\mathscr{P}(w)\\
N(d)\leq y^{2}\end{subarray}}\lambda_{d}=\Big(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}N(e)\leq y\\
e\mid(n,\mathscr{P}(w))\\
e\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}\mu(e)\Big(1-\frac{\log N(e)}{\log y}\Big)\Big)^{2}.

It remains to check property (
4
). Observe that

\displaystyle\sum_{d\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]}\frac{\lambda_{d}}{N(d)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}N(e),N(f)\leq y\\
e,f\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(e)\mu(f)}{N([e,f])}\Big(1-\frac{\log N(e)}{\log y}\Big)\Big(1-\frac{\log N(f)}{\log y}\Big)

(7.4)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(2\pi i)^{2}}\iint_{1/\log y-i\infty}^{1/\log y+i\infty}\frac{H(s,w)\zeta_{\mathbb{Q}(\omega)}(1+s+w)}{\zeta_{\mathbb{Q}(\omega)}(1+s)\zeta_{\mathbb{Q}(\omega)}(1+w)}\cdot\frac{y^{s+w}}{s^{2}w^{2}}\cdot\frac{dsdw}{(\log y)^{2}},

where
H(s,w)
is an analytic and absolutely convergent Euler product for
\operatorname{Re}s,\operatorname{Re}w>-1/4
.
One can conclude that the display in (
7.1
) is
\ll 1/\log y

by shifting contours or by carefully bounding the integral using a Taylor expansion around the pole.
∎

7.2.
Narrow Type-II/III bound

We are now ready to state the main result of this section.

Proposition 7.1
.

Let
\varepsilon\in(0,\tfrac{1}{10000})
,
A,B\geq 10
,
X:=AB
,
10\leq w<X^{\varepsilon}
, and
\eta>0
.
Suppose that
\bm{\alpha}=(\alpha_{a})
is a sequence supported on squarefree
w
-rough
a\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
with

a\equiv 1\pmod{3}
and
N(a)\in[A/10,10A]
. Suppose that
\bm{\beta}=(\beta_{b})\in\mathcal{C}_{\eta}(B,w)
.
Then

(7.5)

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a,b\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\end{subarray}}\alpha_{a}\beta_{b}\tilde{g}(ab)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathcal{K}}{\sqrt{\log w}}\|\bm{\alpha}\|_{2}\Big(A^{1/2}\|\bm{\beta}\|_{2}+A^{1/3}\Big(\sum_{b\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]}\frac{|\beta_{b}|}{N(b)^{1/6}}\Big)\Big)

\displaystyle+O_{\varepsilon}\Big(B^{\eta}\cdot X^{1+\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{1}{A^{1/2}}+\frac{1}{B}\Big)\Big)+O_{\varepsilon}(X^{1+\varepsilon}\cdot(A/w)^{-1000}).

with
\mathcal{K}>1
an absolute constant.

Proof.

Without loss of generality we can include the condition
(a,b)=1
on the left side (
7.5
) by (
2.2
).
Application of (
2.3
) and Cauchy-Schwarz gives

(7.6)

\Big|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a,b\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\end{subarray}}\alpha_{a}\beta_{b}\tilde{g}(ab)\Big|\leq\|\bm{\alpha}\|_{2}\Big(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
(a,\mathscr{P}(w))=1\\
a\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}\Big|\sum_{b}\beta_{b}\tilde{g}(b)\overline{\Big(\frac{a}{b}\Big)_{3}}\Big|^{2}\Big)^{1/2}.

Let
V:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be a fixed smooth positive function
with compact support in
[1/100,100]
. We also stipulate that it
satisfies
V\geq\delta_{[1/10,10]}
. By positivity of the right side
of (
7.6
), we introduce both the smooth function
V

and the sieve weight (
7.1
) on the
a
-sum. Thus the right
side of (
7.6
) is

(7.7)

\leq\|\bm{\alpha}\|_{2}\Big(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
a\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}V\Big(\frac{N(a)}{A}\Big)\Big|\sum_{b}\beta_{b}\tilde{g}(b)\overline{\Big(\frac{a}{b}\Big)_{3}}\Big|^{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d\mid a\\
d\mid\mathscr{P}(w)\\
N(d)\leq y^{2}\end{subarray}}\lambda_{d}\Big)^{1/2},

where
y^{2}:=w
.
Expansion of the bracketed sum in (
7.7
) gives

(7.8)

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d\mid\mathscr{P}(w)\\
N(d)\leq y^{2}\end{subarray}}\lambda_{d}\sum_{b_{1},b_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]}\beta_{b_{1}}\overline{\beta_{b_{2}}}\tilde{g}(b_{1})\overline{\tilde{g}(b_{2})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\ ,\ d\mid a\\
a\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}V\Big(\frac{N(a)}{A}\Big)\overline{\Big(\frac{a}{b_{1}}\Big)_{3}}\Big(\frac{a}{b_{2}}\Big)_{3}.

Diagonal contribution to (
7.8
)

The diagonal contribution
b_{1}=b_{2}=:b
to (
7.8
) is

(7.9)

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
a\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}V\Big(\frac{N(a)}{A}\Big)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
(a,b)=1\end{subarray}}|\beta_{b}|^{2}\Big(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d\mid a\\
d\mid\mathscr{P}(w)\\
N(d)\leq y^{2}\end{subarray}}\lambda_{d}\Big).

We can drop the condition
(a,b)=1
by non-negativity of (
7.9
)
(the bracketed sieve weight divisor sum is non-negative by (
7.1
)). Thus

(
7.9
)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d\mid\mathscr{P}(w)\\
N(d)\leq y^{2}\end{subarray}}\lambda_{d}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\end{subarray}}|\beta_{b}|^{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
a\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}V\Big(\frac{N(a)N(d)}{A}\Big)

(7.10)

\displaystyle=\frac{4\pi A}{9\sqrt{3}}\|\bm{\beta}\|^{2}_{2}\Big(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d\mid\mathscr{P}(w)\\
N(d)\leq y^{2}\end{subarray}}\frac{\lambda_{d}}{N(d)}\Big)\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]}\check{e}\Big({-\frac{k}{3\lambda}}\Big)\ddot{V}\big(k\sqrt{A/N(d)}\big)=:\mathscr{D},

where (
7.10
) follows from Poisson summation (in the form of Lemma
4.3
).

Non-diagonal contribution to (
7.8
)

The non-diagonal
b_{1}\neq b_{2}
contribution to (
7.8
) is

(7.11)

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d\mid\mathscr{P}(w)\\
N(d)\leq y^{2}\end{subarray}}\lambda_{d}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b_{1},b_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
b_{1}\neq b_{2}\end{subarray}}\beta_{b_{1}}\overline{\beta_{b_{2}}}\tilde{g}(b_{1})\overline{\tilde{g}(b_{2})}\overline{\Big(\frac{d}{b_{1}}\Big)_{3}}\Big(\frac{d}{b_{2}}\Big)_{3}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
a\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}V\Big(\frac{N(a)N(d)}{A}\Big)\overline{\Big(\frac{a}{b_{1}}\Big)_{3}}\Big(\frac{a}{b_{2}}\Big)_{3}.

For each fixed
b_{1},b_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
occurring in
(
7.11
), let
e:=(b_{1},b_{2})
.
Poisson summation (in the form of Corollary
4.1
)
tells us that

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
a\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle V\Big(\frac{N(a)N(d)}{A}\Big)\overline{\Big(\frac{a}{b_{1}}\Big)_{3}}\Big(\frac{a}{b_{2}}\Big)_{3}

(7.12)

\displaystyle=\frac{4\pi\overline{\big(\frac{e}{b_{1}/e}\big)_{3}}\big(\frac{e}{b_{2}/e}\big)_{3}A\overline{g(b_{1}/e)}g(b_{2}/e)}{9\sqrt{3}N(d)N(b_{1}b_{2}/e)}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]}\widetilde{c}_{e}(k)\Big(\frac{k}{b_{1}/e}\Big)_{3}\overline{\Big(\frac{k}{b_{2}/e}\Big)_{3}}\ddot{V}\Big(\frac{ke\sqrt{A}}{\sqrt{N(d)}b_{1}b_{2}}\Big).

Remark 7.1
.

Since
b_{1}\neq b_{2}
, the character
\big(\frac{\cdot}{b_{1}/e}\big)_{3}\overline{\big(\frac{\cdot}{b_{2}/e}}\big)_{3}
in (
7.2
)
has conductor with norm
>1
. Hence the dual frequency
k=0
contributes zero to (
7.2
).

Observe that (
2.3
) and the squarefree property of
b_{1}
and
b_{2}

imply that

\displaystyle\tilde{g}(b_{1})\overline{\tilde{g}(b_{2})}

\displaystyle=\tilde{g}(b_{1}/e)\tilde{g}(e)\overline{\Big(\frac{e}{b_{1}/e}\Big)_{3}}\overline{\tilde{g}(b_{2}/e)}\cdot\overline{\tilde{g}(e)}\Big(\frac{e}{b_{2}/e}\Big)_{3}

(7.13)

\displaystyle=\tilde{g}(b_{1}/e)\overline{\tilde{g}(b_{2}/e)}\overline{\Big(\frac{e}{b_{1}/e}\Big)_{3}}\Big(\frac{e}{b_{2}/e}\Big)_{3}.

Upon insertion of (
7.2
) and (
7.2
) into (
7.11
),
we see that (
7.11
) becomes

\displaystyle\mathscr{N}

\displaystyle:=\frac{4\pi A}{9\sqrt{3}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d\mid\mathscr{P}(w)\\
N(d)\leq y^{2}\end{subarray}}\frac{\lambda_{d}}{N(d)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}e\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
e\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b_{1}\neq b_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
(b_{1},b_{2})=e\end{subarray}}\frac{\beta_{b_{1}}\overline{\beta_{b_{2}}}}{\sqrt{N(b_{1}b_{2})}}

(7.14)

\displaystyle\times\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]}\widetilde{c}_{e}(k)\Big(\frac{d^{2}ek}{b_{1}/e}\Big)_{3}\overline{\Big(\frac{d^{2}ek}{b_{2}/e}\Big)_{3}}\ddot{V}\Big(\frac{ke\sqrt{A}}{\sqrt{N(d)}b_{1}b_{2}}\Big).

Remark 7.2
.

Note that Poisson summation constitutes a key step in the proof - the dual side (
7.2
) has no Gauss sum weights.

Estimates for
\mathscr{D}
and
\mathscr{N}

We estimate
\mathscr{D}
and
\mathscr{N}
displayed
in (
7.10
) and (
7.2
) respectively.

Consider
\mathscr{D}
. Lemma
4.4
tells us
that

\mathscr{D}=\frac{4\pi A}{9\sqrt{3}}\|\bm{\beta}\|^{2}_{2}\Big(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d\mid\mathscr{P}(w)\\
N(d)\leq y^{2}\end{subarray}}\frac{\lambda_{d}}{N(d)}\Big)\ddot{V}(0)+O_{\varepsilon}(X^{1+\varepsilon}\cdot(A/w)^{-2000}).

Application of (
7.2
) gives

(7.15)

\mathscr{D}\ll\frac{A}{\log w}\|\bm{\beta}\|^{2}_{2}+O_{\varepsilon}(X^{1+\varepsilon}\cdot(A/w)^{-2000}).

imply that

\widetilde{c}_{e}(de^{2}h^{3})=\check{e}\Big({-\frac{de^{2}h^{3}}{3\lambda}}\Big)\varphi(e)=\check{e}\Big({-\frac{h^{3}}{3\lambda}}\Big)\varphi(e),

d\equiv e\equiv 1\pmod{3}
and Remark
4.1
.
Thus

\displaystyle\mathscr{N}_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{4\pi A}{3^{3}\sqrt{3}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}e\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\

(b_{1},b_{2})=e\end{subarray}}\frac{\beta_{b_{1}}\overline{\beta_{b_{2}}}}{\sqrt{N(b_{1}b_{2})}}

(7.16)

\displaystyle\times\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
(h,b_{1}b_{2}/e^{2})=1\end{subarray}}\check{e}\Big({-\frac{h^{3}}{3\lambda}}\Big)\ddot{V}\Big(\frac{e^{3}h^{3}\sqrt{A}}{b_{1}b_{2}}\Big)\Big(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d\mid\mathscr{P}(w)\\

in the above display for

accounts

for

and
0\neq h\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
.

4.4
and recalling (
7.2

(7.17)

\mathscr{N}_{1}\ll\frac{1}{\log w}\Big(A^{2/3}\Big(\sum_{b\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]}\frac{|\beta_{b}|}{N(b)^{1/6}}\Big)^{2}+A\|\bm{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}\Big).

The term

is small because the characters

and

are both non-principal. Using Lemma
4.4
and Lemma
5.5
, we re-install the diagonal

in

\mathscr{N}_{2}
with acceptable error
O(X^{\varepsilon}(A+B))
.
After rescaling the variables

and

and using Lemma

, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathscr{N}_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{4\pi A}{9\sqrt{3}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d\mid\mathscr{P}(w)\\

\displaystyle\times\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\

\displaystyle+O(X^{\varepsilon}(A+B)).

After combining (
4.11
) and the Mellin–Barnes integral representation
[

, (10.9.22)]

for the

(7.18)

\ddot{V}(u)=\frac{(-1)^{L}}{2\pi i}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{-\varepsilon-i\infty}^{-\varepsilon+\infty}V^{(L)}(r^{2})r^{2L+1}\frac{\Gamma(-s)}{\Gamma(L+s+1)}\Big(\frac{2\pi r|u|}{3\sqrt{3}}\Big)^{2s}dsdr,

for
u\neq 0,L\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
. For
L\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}

sufficiently large and fixed depending on
\varepsilon>0
, Stirling’s asymptotic
formula
[
DLMF
, (5.11.9)]
implies that

(7.19)

\displaystyle\ddot{V}(u)=\frac{(-1)^{L}}{2\pi i}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{-\varepsilon-iX^{\varepsilon}}^{-\varepsilon+iX^{\varepsilon}}V^{(L)}(r^{2})r^{2L+1}\frac{\Gamma(-s)}{\Gamma(L+s+1)}\Big(\frac{2\pi r|u|}{3\sqrt{3}}\Big)^{2s}dsdr+O_{\varepsilon}(X^{-2000}),

for
u\neq 0
.

We Möbius invert the condition
(b_{1},b_{2})=1
in the expression for
\mathscr{N}_{2}
above, and
then separate variables using (
7.19
). A subsequent interchange of the
absolutely convergent finite (recall that
V^{(L)}
is compactly supported) sums and integrations by Fubini’s Theorem gives

\displaystyle\mathscr{N}_{2}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{L}\cdot\frac{2A}{9\sqrt{3}i}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{-\varepsilon-iX^{\varepsilon}}^{-\varepsilon+iX^{\varepsilon}}V^{(L)}(r^{2})r^{2L+1}\frac{\Gamma(-s)}{\Gamma(L+s+1)}\Big(\frac{2\pi r\sqrt{A}}{3\sqrt{3}}\Big)^{2s}

\displaystyle\times\Big(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d\mid\mathscr{P}(w)\\
N(d)\leq y^{2}\end{subarray}}\frac{\lambda_{d}}{N(d)^{1+s}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
f\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(f)}{N(f)^{1+2s}}

\displaystyle\times\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}e\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
e\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
d^{2}ek\neq\hbox to5.21pt{\vbox to5.21pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}

{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}

N(k)\ll X^{\varepsilon}(1+N(d)B^{2}/(N(e)A))\end{subarray}}\frac{\widetilde{c}_{e}(k)N(k)^{s}}{N(e)^{1+s}}\Big(\frac{d^{2}ek}{f}\Big)_{3}\overline{\Big(\frac{d^{2}ek}{f}\Big)_{3}}

\displaystyle\times\Big(\sum_{b_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]}\frac{\beta_{efb_{1}}}{\sqrt{N(b_{1})}}\Big(\frac{d^{2}ek}{b_{1}}\Big)_{3}N(b_{1})^{-s}\Big)\Big(\sum_{b_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]}\frac{\overline{\beta_{efb_{2}}}}{\sqrt{N(b_{2})}}\overline{\Big(\frac{d^{2}ek}{b_{2}}}\Big)_{3}N(b_{2})^{-s}\Big)\Big)dsdr

\displaystyle+O(X^{\varepsilon}(A+B)).

We use Axiom 4 of Definition
3.1
to estimate the sum over
b_{1}
and
b_{2}
(square root cancellation), and then estimate the
remaining sums trivially using Lemma
5.5
. We obtain

\displaystyle\mathscr{N}_{2}

\displaystyle\ll AB^{2\eta}X^{\varepsilon}\Big(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d\mid\mathscr{P}(w)\\
N(d)\leq y^{2}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{N(d)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
1\leq N(f)\ll B\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu^{2}(f)}{N(f)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}e\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
1\leq N(e)\ll B\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu^{2}(e)}{N(e)}

\displaystyle\times\sum_{g\mid e}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
(k,e)=g\\
N(k)\ll X^{\varepsilon}(1+N(d)B^{2}/(N(e)A))\end{subarray}}\frac{\varphi(e)}{\varphi(e/g)}\Big)+X^{\varepsilon}(A+B)

(7.20)

\displaystyle\ll X^{\varepsilon}B^{2\eta}(A+B^{2}).

7.2.1.
Conclusion

Combining (
7.15
), (
7.17
) and (
7.2
) tells us that

\eqref{eq:expanded}\leq\frac{\mathcal{K}}{\log w}\Big(A^{2/3}\Big(\sum_{b\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]}\frac{|\beta_{b}|}{N(b)^{1/6}}\Big)^{2}+A\|\bm{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}\Big)+O_{\varepsilon}(B^{2\eta}(A+B^{2})X^{\varepsilon})+O_{\varepsilon}(X^{1+\varepsilon}\cdot(A/w)^{-2000}),

for some absolute constant
\mathcal{K}>1
.
Chasing this bound through (
7.7
) and (
7.6
)
gives the result.

∎

8.
Type I estimates

We now establish Type-I estimates. In the Proposition below we use the Riemann Hypothesis for the
Dedekind zeta function attached to
\mathbb{Q}(\omega)
in order to restrict the sum to squarefree numbers.

Proposition 8.1
.

Assume the Riemann Hypothesis for the family of Dedekind zeta functions attached to
\mathbb{Q}(\omega)
twisted by
Größencharaktern. Let
r\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
be squarefree and satisfy
r\equiv 1\pmod{3}
.
Let
\varepsilon\in(0,\tfrac{1}{10000})
,
and
W:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be a smooth function with compact support contained in
[1,2]
. Then there exists

\rho(\varepsilon)\in(0,\frac{1}{10000})
such that

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
u\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\tilde{g}(ur)\Big(\frac{u}{|u|}\Big)^{\ell}\cdot W\Big(\frac{N(u)}{U}\Big)=\delta_{\ell=0}\cdot\widetilde{W}\Big(\frac{5}{6}\Big)\cdot\frac{(2\pi)^{5/3}U^{5/6}}{3^{7/2}\Gamma(\tfrac{2}{3})\zeta_{\mathbb{Q}(\omega)}(2;\mathbf{1}_{r})}\frac{\varphi(r)}{N(r)^{7/6}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+O_{\varepsilon}\Big((1+|\ell|)^{\varepsilon}\cdot\Big(\frac{U^{5/6-\rho(\varepsilon)}}{N(r)^{1/6+\rho(\varepsilon)}}+U^{1/12+\varepsilon}N(r)^{7/12+\varepsilon}\cdot(1+\ell^{6})\Big)\Big),

for all
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
and
U\geq 1
.

Remark 8.1
.

The function
\rho(\varepsilon)
is somewhat arbitrary. For instance, it follows from
(
8.6
) that
\rho(\varepsilon)=\frac{11}{12}\varepsilon-\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon^{2}
is an acceptable choice.

Remark 8.2
.

Mellin inversion of the smooth function, the Class number formula
[
Lan94
, Chapter VIII, §2, Theorem 5]
, and a contour shift together imply that

(8.1)

\displaystyle\frac{(2\pi)^{2/3}}{3\Gamma(\tfrac{2}{3})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
(u,r)=1\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\frac{\mu^{2}(u)}{N(u)^{1/6}}W\Big(\frac{N(u)}{U}\Big)\cdot\frac{1}{N(r)^{1/6}}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{W}\Big(\frac{5}{6}\Big)\frac{(2\pi)^{5/3}U^{5/6}}{3^{7/2}\Gamma(\tfrac{2}{3})\zeta_{\mathbb{Q}(\omega)}(2;\mathbf{1}_{r})}\frac{\varphi(r)}{N(r)^{7/6}}+O_{\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{U^{1/3+\varepsilon}}{N(r)^{1/6}}\Big).

Thus when
\ell=0
, we can use the main term in (
8.1
) in Proposition
8.1
at negligible cost.

Proof.

Möbius inversion implies that

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
u\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\tilde{g}(ur)\Big(\frac{u}{|u|}\Big)^{\ell}W\Big(\frac{N(u)}{U}\Big)

(8.2)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}u,e\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
u,e\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
(e,r)=1\end{subarray}}\tilde{g}(ur)|e|\Big(\frac{e^{3}u}{|e^{3}u|}\Big)^{\ell}W\Big(\frac{N(e)^{3}N(u)}{X}\Big)\Big(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}e=cd\\
c,d\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}\mu(c)\Big).

Remark 8.3
.

Note that the factor of
|e|
in (
8
) reflects the periodicity property possessed by the coefficients of the cubic theta function on cubes. See
Remark
5.2
.

On the right side of (
8
) we introduce a smooth partition of unity in the
c
variable.
Let
V:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be a fixed smooth function with compact support
contained in
[1,2]
such that

(8.3)

\sum_{C\text{ dyadic}}V\Big(\frac{N(c)}{C}\Big)=1\quad\text{for all}\quad 0\neq c\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega].

Insertion of (
8.3
) into (
8
) yields

(8.4)

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
u\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}\tilde{g}(ur)\Big(\frac{u}{|u|}\Big)^{\ell}W\Big(\frac{N(u)}{U}\Big)=\sum_{C\text{ dyadic}}\mathscr{M}(C,U),

where

\displaystyle\mathscr{M}(C,U)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}u,d\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
u,d\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
(d,r)=1\end{subarray}}\tilde{g}(ur)|d|\Big(\frac{u}{|u|}\Big)^{\ell}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}c\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
c\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
(c,r)=1\end{subarray}}\mu(c)|c|

(8.5)

\displaystyle\times\Big(\frac{cd}{|cd|}\Big)^{3\ell}V\Big(\frac{N(c)}{C}\Big)W\Big(\frac{N(cd)^{3}N(u)}{U}\Big).

We have suppressed the dependence of
\mathscr{M}(C,U)

on the smooth functions
V
and
W
in the notation.

Large dyadic
C

We estimate the contribution to the right side of (
8.4
)
from all dyadic values of
C
satisfying

C\geq(UN(r))^{1/12+\varepsilon/2}.

We Mellin invert the smooth functions
V
and
W
in (
8
). We then use
the rapid decay of their holomorphic Mellin transforms

\widetilde{W}
and
\widetilde{V}
in vertical strips to truncate the integrations appropriately. A subsequent
interchange of the order of absolutely convergent
finite sums and integrations by Fubini’s Theorem gives

\displaystyle\mathscr{M}(C,U)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{(2\pi i)^{2}}\int_{-i(C(1+|\ell|))^{\varepsilon/1000}}^{i(C(1+|\ell|))^{\varepsilon/1000}}\int_{-i(C(1+|\ell|))^{\varepsilon/1000}}^{i(C(1+|\ell|))^{\varepsilon/1000}}\widetilde{V}(s)\widetilde{W}(w)C^{s}U^{w}

\displaystyle\times\Big(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}u,d\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
U/(100C^{3})\leq N(ud^{3})\leq 100U/C^{3}\\
u,d\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
(d,r)=1\end{subarray}}\tilde{g}(ur)N(d)^{1/2-3w}\Big(\frac{ud^{3}}{|ud^{3}|}\Big)^{\ell}N(u)^{-w}

\displaystyle\times\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}c\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
C\leq N(c)\leq 2C\\
c\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
(c,r)=1\end{subarray}}\mu(c)\Big(\frac{c}{|c|}\Big)^{3\ell}N(c)^{1/2-s-3w}\Big)dsdw+O_{\varepsilon}\big((C(1+|\ell|))^{-2000}\big).

To bound the sum over
c
we appeal to the Riemann Hypothesis for the Dedekind zeta function attached to
\mathbb{Q}(\omega)
twisted by a Größencharakter.
Estimating the other summations trivially, we obtain

(8.6)

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}C\text{ dyadic}\\
C\geq(UN(r))^{1/12+\varepsilon/2}\end{subarray}}\mathscr{M}(C,U)\ll_{\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{U}{C^{3}}\Big)C^{1+\varepsilon}(1+|\ell|)^{\varepsilon}\ll_{\varepsilon}(1+|\ell|)^{\varepsilon}\frac{U^{5/6-\rho(\varepsilon)}}{N(r)^{1/6+\rho(\varepsilon)}},

for some
\rho(\varepsilon)\in(0,\frac{1}{10000})
. See also Remark
8.1
.

Small dyadic
C

It remains to estimate

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}C\text{ dyadic}\\
1/2\leq C\leq(UN(r))^{1/12+\varepsilon/2}\end{subarray}}\mathscr{M}(C,U).

Rearranging the sums in (
8
), and then using (
2.3
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathscr{M}(C,U)

\displaystyle=\tilde{g}(r)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}c\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
c\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
(c,r)=1\end{subarray}}|c|\Big(\frac{c}{|c|}\Big)^{3\ell}\mu(c)V\Big(\frac{N(c)}{C}\Big)

(8.7)

\displaystyle\times\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d,u\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
d,u\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
(d,r)=1\end{subarray}}|d|\tilde{g}(u)\overline{\Big(\frac{u}{r}\Big)_{3}}\Big(\frac{ud^{3}}{|ud^{3}|}\Big)^{\ell}W\Big(\frac{N(ud^{3})}{U/N(c)^{3}}\Big).

Application of Voronoi summation (in the form of Proposition
5.3
) gives

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d,u\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
d,u\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
(d,r)=1\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle|d|\tilde{g}(u)\overline{\Big(\frac{u}{r}\Big)_{3}}\Big(\frac{ud^{3}}{|ud^{3}|}\Big)^{\ell}W\Big(\frac{N(ud^{3})}{U/N(c)^{3}}\Big)

\displaystyle=\delta_{\ell=0}\frac{U^{5/6}}{N(c)^{5/2}}\widetilde{W}\Big(\frac{5}{6}\Big)\frac{(2\pi)^{5/3}\varphi(r)\overline{g(r)}}{3^{7/2}\Gamma(\frac{2}{3})N(r)^{5/3}}

\displaystyle+\frac{\overline{g(r)}}{3^{7/2}(2\pi)^{2}}\Big(\frac{\overline{r}}{r}\Big)^{-\ell}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu\in\lambda^{-1}\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
d\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
(d,r)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{a^{\dagger}(\nu)b_{r}^{\dagger}(\nu)\big(\frac{\nu d^{3}}{|\nu d^{3}|}\big)^{-\ell}}{N(\nu)N(d)^{5/2}}\widecheck{W_{\ell}}\Big(\frac{(2\pi)^{4}N(d^{3}\nu)U}{N(c^{3}r^{2})}\Big),

where the
a^{\dagger}(\cdot)
and
b_{r}^{\dagger}(\cdot)
are given by (
5.67
) and (
5.68
) respectively.
Insertion of the above display into (
8
) gives

(8.8)

\mathscr{M}(C,U)=\mathscr{T}(C,U)+\mathscr{E}(C,U),

where

\mathscr{T}(C,U):=\delta_{\ell=0}\frac{(2\pi)^{5/3}}{3^{7/2}\Gamma(\frac{2}{3})}\widetilde{W}\Big(\frac{5}{6}\Big)\frac{\varphi(r)}{N(r)^{7/6}}U^{5/6}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}c\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
c\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
(c,r)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(c)}{N(c)^{2}}V\Big(\frac{N(c)}{C}\Big),

and

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}(C,U)

\displaystyle:=\frac{N(r)^{1/2}}{3^{7/2}(2\pi)^{2}}\Big(\frac{\overline{r}}{r}\Big)^{-\ell}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu\in\lambda^{-1}\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
d\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
(d,r)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{a^{\dagger}(\nu)b_{r}^{\dagger}(\nu)}{N(\nu)N(d)^{5/2}}\Big(\frac{d^{3}\nu}{|d^{3}\nu|}\Big)^{-\ell}

\displaystyle\times\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}c\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
c\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
(c,r)=1\end{subarray}}|c|\mu(c)\Big(\frac{c}{|c|}\Big)^{3\ell}V\Big(\frac{N(c)}{C}\Big)\widecheck{W_{\ell}}\Big(\frac{(2\pi)^{4}N(d^{3}\nu)U}{N(c^{3}r^{2})}\Big).

We now collect the main term from the various
\mathscr{T}(C,U)
. We have

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}C\text{ dyadic}\\
1/2\leq C\leq(UN(r))^{1/12+\varepsilon}\end{subarray}}\mathscr{T}(C,U)

\displaystyle=\sum_{C\text{ dyadic}}\mathscr{T}(C,U)+O_{\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{U^{3/4}}{N(r)^{1/4}}\Big)

(8.9)

\displaystyle=\delta_{\ell=0}\widetilde{W}\Big(\frac{5}{6}\Big)\frac{(2\pi)^{5/3}U^{5/6}}{3^{7/2}\Gamma(\tfrac{2}{3})\zeta_{\mathbb{Q}(\omega)}(2;\mathbf{1}_{r})}\frac{\varphi(r)}{N(r)^{7/6}}+O_{\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{U^{3/4}}{N(r)^{1/4}}\Big),

where the error term follows from a trivial estimation of the tail of
\zeta_{\mathbb{Q}(\omega)}
.

The various
\mathscr{E}(C,U)

contribute the error term in the statement of the result.
By the rapid decay of
\widecheck{W}
in (
5.82
),
we truncate the
d
and
\nu
sums in
\mathscr{E}(C,U)
to

N(d^{3}\nu)\ll(UN(r)(1+|\ell|))^{\varepsilon/1000}(1+\ell^{4})\cdot\Big(1+\frac{C^{3}N(r)^{2}}{U}\Big)=:\mathcal{Z},

with negligible error. To separate variables, we subsequently use the definition (
5.81
)
of
\widecheck{W}
and Mellin inversion on
V
. We truncate the resulting integrations appropriately using
the rapid decay of
\widetilde{V}
and
\widetilde{W}
. A subsequent
interchange of the order of absolutely convergent
finite sums and integrations by Fubini’s Theorem gives

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}(C,U)

\displaystyle=\frac{N(r)^{1/2}}{3^{7/2}(2\pi)^{4}}\Big(\frac{\overline{r}}{r}\Big)^{-\ell}\int_{-i(UN(r)(1+|\ell|))^{\varepsilon/1000}}^{i(UN(r)(1+|\ell|))^{\varepsilon/1000}}\int_{-i(UN(r)(1+|\ell|))^{\varepsilon/1000}}^{i(UN(r)(1+|\ell|))^{\varepsilon/1000}}\frac{\Gamma(\frac{5}{6}+\frac{|\ell|}{2}-w)\Gamma(\frac{7}{6}+\frac{|\ell|}{2}-w)}{\Gamma(w+\frac{|\ell|}{2}-\frac{1}{6})\Gamma(w+\frac{|\ell|}{2}+\frac{1}{6})}

\displaystyle\times\Big(\frac{(2\pi)^{4}U}{N(r)^{2}}\Big)^{w}C^{s}\widetilde{V}(s)\widetilde{W}(w)

\displaystyle\times\Big(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu\in\lambda^{-1}\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
d\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
(d,r)=1\\
N(d^{3}\nu)\ll\mathcal{Z}\end{subarray}}\frac{a^{\dagger}(\nu)b_{r}^{\dagger}(\nu)}{N(\nu)^{1-w}N(d)^{5/2-3w}}\Big(\frac{d^{3}\nu}{|d^{3}\nu|}\Big)^{-\ell}\hskip-11.38092pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}c\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
c\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
(c,r)=1\\
C\leq N(c)\leq 2C\end{subarray}}N(c)^{1/2-s-3w}\mu(c)\Big(\frac{c}{|c|}\Big)^{3\ell}\Big)dsdw

\displaystyle+O_{\varepsilon}\big((UN(r)(1+|\ell|))^{-1000}\big).

We estimate the sum over
c
using the Riemann hypothesis for the Dedekind zeta function attached to
\mathbb{Q}(\omega)
,
and the quotient of Gamma factors using Stirling’s asymptotic
[
DLMF
, (5.11.1)]

The other sums are estimated trivially using (
5.67
), (
5.68
), (
5.7
), (
5.13
) and (
5.14
).
We obtain

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}(C,U)

\displaystyle\ll_{\varepsilon}N(r)^{1/2+\varepsilon/10}\cdot(1+\ell^{2})

\displaystyle\times\sum_{e\mid r}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu\in\lambda^{-1}\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
d\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
(d,r)=1\\
(\lambda\nu,r)=e\\
N(d^{3}\nu)\ll\mathcal{Z}\end{subarray}}\frac{|a^{\dagger}(\nu)|}{N(\nu)N(d)^{5/2}}\frac{\varphi(r)}{\varphi(r/e)}C^{1+\varepsilon/10}(1+|\ell|)^{\varepsilon/10},

\displaystyle\ll N(r)^{1/2+\varepsilon/4}U^{\varepsilon/4}(1+\ell^{6})C^{1+\varepsilon/4}(1+|\ell|)^{\varepsilon/4},

and so

(8.10)

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}C\text{ dyadic}\\
1/2\leq C\leq(UN(r))^{1/12+\varepsilon/2}\end{subarray}}\mathscr{E}(C,U)\ll_{\varepsilon}U^{1/12+\varepsilon}N(r)^{7/12+\varepsilon}\cdot(1+\ell^{6})(1+|\ell|)^{\varepsilon}.

After combining (
8.6
), (
8
) and (
8.10
), we obtain the result.

∎

We also record the following nearly immediate Corollary.

Corollary 8.1
.

Assume the Riemann Hypothesis for the family of Dedekind zeta functions attached to
\mathbb{Q}(\omega)
twisted by Größencharaktern.
Let
r\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
be squarefree and satisfy
r\equiv 1\pmod{3}
. Let

\varepsilon\in(0,\tfrac{1}{10000})
,
and
V,W:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be smooth functions with compact support contained in
[\tfrac{1}{4},4]
.
Then there exists
\rho(\varepsilon)\in(0,\frac{1}{10000})
such that

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
u\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}\tilde{g}(ur)\Big(\frac{u}{|u|}\Big)^{\ell}\cdot V\Big(\frac{N(u)}{U}\Big)W\Big(\frac{N(ur)}{X}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\delta_{\ell=0}\cdot\frac{(2\pi)^{2/3}}{3\Gamma(\tfrac{2}{3})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
(u,r)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu^{2}(u)}{N(u)^{1/6}}V\Big(\frac{N(u)}{U}\Big)W\Big(\frac{N(ur)}{X}\Big)\cdot\frac{1}{N(r)^{1/6}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+O_{\varepsilon}\Big((1+|\ell|)^{\varepsilon}\cdot\Big(\frac{U^{5/6-\rho(\varepsilon)}}{N(r)^{1/6+\rho(\varepsilon)}}+U^{1/12+\varepsilon}N(r)^{7/12+\varepsilon}(1+\ell^{6})\Big)+\frac{U^{1/3+\varepsilon}}{N(r)^{1/6}}\Big),

for all
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
and
X\geq U\geq 1
.

Proof.

If
UN(r)\asymp X
then we simply apply the previous result with a different weight function and use the Remark
8.2
.
If
UN(r)
is not of the order of magnitude of
X
then both main terms are zero.
∎

Remark 8.4
.

The main term in Corollary
8.1
is larger than the error term when
U\geq(N(r)(1+\ell^{6}))^{1+o(1)}
.

9.
Improved cubic large sieve

The cubic large sieve of Heath–Brown is as follows.

Theorem 9.1
.

[
HB00
, Theorem 2]

Let
A,B\geq 1
,
\varepsilon>0
, and
(\beta_{b})_{b\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]}
be an arbitrary sequence of complex numbers
with support contained in the set of squarefree elements of
\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
.
Then

(9.1)

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}N(a)\leq A\\
a\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}\mu^{2}(a)\Big|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}N(b)\leq B\\
b\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}\beta_{b}\Big(\frac{b}{a}\Big)_{3}\Big|^{2}\ll_{\varepsilon}\big(A+B+(AB)^{2/3}\big)(AB)^{\varepsilon}\sum_{b\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]}|\beta_{b}|^{2}.

Recall the operator norm
\mathcal{B}(A,B)
defined in (
1.12
).
The Duality Principle
[
IK04
, (7.9)–(7.11)]
and cubic reciprocity imply that

(9.2)

\mathcal{B}(A,B)=\mathcal{B}(B,A).

See also
[
HB00
, Lemma 4]
. We also have the following simple monotonicity property.

Lemma 9.1
.

[
HB00
, Lemma 5]

There exists an absolute constant
C\geq 1
as follows. Let
A,B_{1},B_{2}\gg 1

and
B_{2}\geq CB_{1}\log(2AB_{1})
. Then,

\mathcal{B}(A,B_{1})\ll\mathcal{B}(A,B_{2}).

We now prove that Heath-Brown’s cubic large sieve is optimal under the Generalized Riemann Hypothesis for
Hecke
L
-functions over
\mathbb{Q}(\omega)
.

Proof of Theorem
1.4
.

The upper bounds in the given ranges follow from the cubic large sieve Theorem
9.1
,
and are unconditional.

We now focus on the conditional lower bounds.
Let
\xi>0
be small and fixed,
A,B\geq 10
,
X:=AB
and
A\in[10,X^{1/2-\xi}]
.
Consider the sequence

(9.3)

\beta_{b}=\overline{\tilde{g}(b)}W\Big(\frac{N(b)}{B}\Big),

where
W
is a smooth compactly supported function in
(1,2)
.
It is supported only on squarefree elements by (
2.2
).
Then

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}A<N(a)\leq 2A\\
a\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}\mu^{2}(a)\Big|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}B<N(b)\leq 2B\\
b\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}\overline{\tilde{g}(b)}W\Big(\frac{N(b)}{B}\Big)\Big(\frac{b}{a}\Big)_{3}\Big|^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}A<N(a)\leq 2A\\
a\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}\mu^{2}(a)\Big|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}\overline{W\Big(\frac{N(b)}{B}\Big)}\tilde{g}(ab)\Big|^{2}\quad(\text{by }\eqref{cuberel}\text{ and }\eqref{twistmult})

(9.4)

\displaystyle\gg A\Big(\frac{B^{5/6}}{A^{1/6}}\Big)^{2}+O\big(X^{o(1)}(A^{17/12}B^{11/12}+A^{13/6}B^{1/6})\big)

(9.5)

\displaystyle\gg_{\xi,W}A^{2/3}B^{5/3},

where display (
9.4
) follows from Voronoi summation (Proposition
8.1
)
and the GRH hypothesis, and (
9.5
) follows from the fact that we are in the range
A\ll(AB)^{1/2-\xi}
.
Thus
\mathcal{B}(A,B)\gg_{\xi}(AB)^{2/3}\gg A+B
for
A\ll(AB)^{1/2-\xi}
and
A\in[X^{1/3},X^{1/2}]
.
Combining this result with (
9.2
) then gives the claim
when
A\in[\sqrt{B},B^{2}]\setminus[B^{1-\xi},B^{1+2\xi}]
.
The result in the range
A\in[B^{1-\xi},B^{1+2\xi}]
then follows from (
9.2
) and Lemma
9.1
,
so
\mathcal{B}(A,B)\gg\mathcal{B}(AX^{-3\xi},B)\gg(AB)^{2/3-3\xi}
.
∎

In light of the proof of Theorem
1.4
, we renormalise the sequences we consider in
the cubic large sieve by setting

c_{b}:=\tilde{g}(b)\beta_{b}
where

\bm{\beta}:=(\beta_{b})_{b\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]}
is a sequence
supported on squarefree
b\equiv 1\pmod{3}
.
Note that
|c_{b}|=|\beta_{b}|
on squarefree
b\equiv 1\pmod{3}
by (
2.2
).
We are able to refine Theorem
9.1
in a special case by:

(1)

Introducing a non-trivial asymptotic main term;

(2)

Assuming additional cancellations/density restrictions for the sequence
\bm{\beta}=(\beta_{b})
.

Proposition 9.1
.

Let
V:(0,\infty)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
be a smooth compactly supported function,

0<\eta\leq 1/4
,
A,B,w\geq 10
and
X:=AB
.
Suppose that
w>(\log X)^{10}
and
\bm{\beta}=(\beta_{b})\in\mathcal{C}_{\eta}(B,w)
. Let
\varepsilon\in(0,\frac{1}{10000})

and
\pi\equiv 1\pmod{3}

be a prime or
1
.
Then there exists
\rho(\varepsilon)\in(0,\frac{1}{10000})
such that uniformly in

1\leq N(\pi)<w
we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
a\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
\pi\mid a\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\mu^{2}(a)V\Big(\frac{N(a)}{A}\Big)\Big|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
(b,a)=1\end{subarray}}\beta_{b}\tilde{g}(b)\overline{\Big(\frac{a}{b}\Big)_{3}}-\frac{(2\pi)^{2/3}}{3\Gamma(\tfrac{2}{3})}\frac{\overline{\tilde{g}(a)}}{N(a)^{1/6}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
(b,a)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\beta_{b}}{N(b)^{1/6}}\Big|^{2}

\displaystyle\ll_{\varepsilon}\frac{A^{2/3}B^{5/3}}{N(\pi)}\Big(\frac{1}{w^{9/10}}+\frac{\delta_{\pi\neq 1}}{N(\pi)}\Big)+\frac{A^{2/3-\rho(\varepsilon)}B^{5/3-\rho(\varepsilon)}}{N(\pi)}+\frac{A^{1/6+\varepsilon}B^{5/3}}{N(\pi)^{1/2+\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle+(N(\pi)X)^{\varepsilon}\Big(N(\pi)^{1/2}B^{29/12}A^{-1/12}+B^{2+2\eta}+\frac{X}{N(\pi)}\Big(1+(B^{2}/A)^{-1000}\Big)\Big).

Proposition
9.1
will follow from a modification of the proof of
Proposition
9.2
using sieve weights, and from Lemma
9.2
below.
At the close of this section we sketch how Proposition
9.1
follows.

Proposition 9.2
.

Let
V:(0,\infty)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
be a smooth compactly supported function,

0<\eta\leq 1/4
,

A,B,w\geq 10
and
X:=AB
.
Suppose that
w>(\log X)^{10}
and
\bm{\beta}=(\beta_{b})\in\mathcal{C}_{\eta}(B,w)
.
Let
\varepsilon\in(0,\frac{1}{10000})
,
\Delta\geq 1
,
\pi\equiv 1\pmod{3}

be a prime or
1
, and
\gamma\equiv 1\pmod{3}
be squarefree such that
(\pi,\gamma)=1
.
Then there exists
\rho(\varepsilon)\in(0,\frac{1}{10000})

such that uniformly in
1\leq N(\gamma)\leq\Delta
and
1\leq N(\pi)<w

we have

(9.6)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
a\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
\pi\gamma^{2}\mid a\end{subarray}}V\Big(\frac{N(a)}{A}\Big)\Big|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
(b,a)=1\end{subarray}}\beta_{b}\tilde{g}(b)\overline{\Big(\frac{b}{a}\Big)_{3}}-\frac{(2\pi)^{2/3}}{3\Gamma(\tfrac{2}{3})}\frac{\overline{\tilde{g}(a)}}{N(a)^{1/6}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
(b,a)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\beta_{b}}{N(b)^{1/6}}\Big|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\Big(\frac{1}{N(\gamma)^{2}}-\frac{\delta_{\gamma=1}}{\zeta_{\mathbb{Q}(\omega)}(2;\mathbf{1}_{\pi})}\Big)\widetilde{V}\Big(\frac{2}{3}\Big)\frac{2\pi}{9\sqrt{3}}\Big(\frac{(2\pi)^{2/3}}{3\Gamma(\tfrac{2}{3})}\Big)^{3}\frac{A^{2/3}}{N(\pi)}\Big|\sum_{b\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]}\frac{\beta_{b}}{N(b)^{1/6}}\Big|^{2}

\displaystyle+O_{\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{1}{N(\pi\gamma^{2})}\Big(\frac{A^{2/3}B^{5/3}}{w^{9/10}}+(\Delta N(\pi)X)^{\varepsilon}\Big(B^{2+2\eta}N(\pi)\Delta^{2}+X\Big(1+(B^{2}/A)^{-1000}\Big)\Big)\Big)\Big)

\displaystyle+\delta_{\gamma=1}\cdot\Big(O_{\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{A^{2/3-\rho(\varepsilon)}B^{5/3-\rho(\varepsilon)}}{N(\pi)}+\frac{A^{2/3}B^{5/3}}{N(\pi)}\Big(\frac{1}{w^{9/10}}+\frac{\delta_{\pi\neq 1}}{N(\pi)}\Big)

\displaystyle+X^{\varepsilon}N(\pi)^{1/2}B^{29/12}A^{-1/12}+\frac{A^{1/6+\varepsilon}B^{5/3}}{N(\pi)^{1/2+\varepsilon}}\Big)\Big).

Proof.

Using (
2.2
) and inclusion-exclusion with the condition
(a,b)=1
, we see that
the expression in (
9.6
)
is equal to

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
a\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
\pi\gamma^{2}\mid a\end{subarray}}V\Big(\frac{N(a)}{A}\Big)\Big|\Big(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
(b,\gamma a)=1\end{subarray}}\beta_{b}\tilde{g}(b)\overline{\Big(\frac{b}{a}\Big)_{3}}-\frac{(2\pi)^{2/3}}{3\Gamma(\tfrac{2}{3})}\frac{\delta_{\gamma=1}\overline{\tilde{g}(a)}}{N(a)^{1/6}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\end{subarray}}\frac{\beta_{b}}{N(b)^{1/6}}\Big)

(9.7)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\Big(\frac{(2\pi)^{2/3}}{3\Gamma(\tfrac{2}{3})}\frac{\delta_{\gamma=1}\overline{\tilde{g}(a)}}{N(a)^{1/6}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
(b,a)\neq 1\end{subarray}}\frac{\beta_{b}}{N(b)^{1/6}}\Big)\Big|^{2}.

Since
V
is non-negative we can apply the parallelogram identity

(9.8)

|X+Y|^{2}\leq 2(|X|^{2}+|Y|^{2})\quad\text{for all}\quad X,Y\in\mathbb{C},

to the display above. This shows that (
9
) is

(9.9)

\displaystyle\leq 2\Big(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
a\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
\pi\gamma^{2}\mid a\end{subarray}}V\Big(\frac{N(a)}{A}\Big)\Big|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
(b,\gamma a)=1\end{subarray}}\beta_{b}\tilde{g}(b)\overline{\Big(\frac{b}{a}\Big)_{3}}-\frac{(2\pi)^{2/3}}{3\Gamma(\tfrac{2}{3})}\frac{\delta_{\gamma=1}\overline{\tilde{g}(a)}}{N(a)^{1/6}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\end{subarray}}\frac{\beta_{b}}{N(b)^{1/6}}\Big|^{2}

(9.10)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\delta_{\gamma=1}\frac{(2\pi)^{4/3}}{9\Gamma(\tfrac{2}{3})^{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
a\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
\pi\mid a\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu^{2}(a)}{N(a)^{1/3}}V\Big(\frac{N(a)}{A}\Big)\Big|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
(b,a)\neq 1\end{subarray}}\frac{\beta_{b}}{N(b)^{1/6}}\Big|^{2}\Big),

where we used (
2.2
) to obtain the last display. The term in (
9.10
) is equal to

(9.11)

\delta_{\gamma=1}\frac{(2\pi)^{4/3}}{9\Gamma(\tfrac{2}{3})^{2}}\sum_{b_{1},b_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]}\frac{\beta_{b_{1}}\overline{\beta_{b_{2}}}}{N(b_{1}b_{2})^{1/6}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
a\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
\pi\mid a\\
(a,b_{1})\neq 1\\
(a,b_{2})\neq 1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu^{2}(a)}{N(a)^{1/3}}V\Big(\frac{N(a)}{A}\Big)=\delta_{\gamma=1}O\Big(\frac{A^{2/3}B^{5/3}}{N(\pi)w^{9/10}}\Big).

The estimate in (
9.11
) follows from the triangle inequality, the fact
(b_{1}b_{2},\pi)=1

(
b_{1}b_{2}
is
w
-rough and
\pi
is
w
-smooth) and

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\varpi\mid b_{1}b_{2}\\
\varpi\text{ prime}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{N(\varpi)}\ll\frac{\log B^{2}}{\log\log B^{2}}\frac{1}{w}\ll\frac{1}{w^{9/10}},\quad\text{for}\quad w>(\log X)^{10}\quad\text{say}.

We repeatedly use this
w
-roughness argument in the course of the proof.

It suffices to compute the term in (
9.9
). We make the change of variable
a\mapsto\pi\gamma^{2}a
. After using cubic reciprocity
and (
2.2
), it suffices to compute

(9.12)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
a\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}V\Big(\frac{N(\pi\gamma^{2})N(a)}{A}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
(b,\pi\gamma a)=1\end{subarray}}\beta_{b}\tilde{g}(b)\overline{\Big(\frac{a}{b}\Big)_{3}}\Big(\frac{\pi^{2}\gamma}{b}\Big)_{3}-\frac{(2\pi)^{2/3}}{3\Gamma(\tfrac{2}{3})}\frac{\delta_{\gamma=1}\overline{\tilde{g}(a\pi)}}{N(a\pi)^{1/6}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\end{subarray}}\frac{\beta_{b}}{N(b)^{1/6}}\Big|^{2}.

Expansion of the square in (
9.12
) shows that we need to evaluate
the diagonal term

(9.13)

\mathscr{D}:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
a\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}V\Big(\frac{N(\pi\gamma^{2})N(a)}{A}\Big)\Big|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
(b,\pi\gamma a)=1\end{subarray}}\beta_{b}\tilde{g}(b)\overline{\Big(\frac{a}{b}\Big)_{3}}\Big(\frac{\pi^{2}\gamma}{b}\Big)_{3}\Big|^{2};

the cross term

\displaystyle\mathscr{C}:=-2\frac{(2\pi)^{2/3}}{3\Gamma(\tfrac{2}{3})}\delta_{\gamma=1}\text{Re}\Big(

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b_{1},b_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\end{subarray}}\frac{\beta_{b_{1}}\tilde{g}(b_{1})\overline{\beta_{b_{2}}}}{N(b_{2})^{1/6}}

(9.14)

\displaystyle\times\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
a\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
(a\pi,b_{1})=1\end{subarray}}V\Big(\frac{N(a)N(\pi)}{A}\Big)\overline{\Big(\frac{a}{b_{1}}\Big)_{3}}\Big(\frac{\pi^{2}}{b_{1}}\Big)_{3}\frac{\tilde{g}(a\pi)}{N(a\pi)^{1/6}}\Big);

and the trivial term,

(9.15)

\mathscr{T}:=\Big(\frac{(2\pi)^{2/3}}{3\Gamma(\tfrac{2}{3})}\Big)^{2}\delta_{\gamma=1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
a\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}V\Big(\frac{N(a)N(\pi)}{A}\Big)\frac{\mu^{2}(a\pi)}{N(a\pi)^{1/3}}\Big|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\end{subarray}}\frac{\beta_{b}}{N(b)^{1/6}}\Big|^{2}.

The appearance of
\mu^{2}(a\pi)
in
\mathscr{T}
is due to
|\widetilde{g}(a\pi)|^{2}=\mu^{2}(a\pi)
(a consequence of (
2.2
)).

In the course of this proof we will establish the three asymptotic estimates:

\displaystyle\mathscr{D}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\pi}{9\sqrt{3}}\Big(\frac{(2\pi)^{2/3}}{3\Gamma(\tfrac{2}{3})}\Big)^{2}\widetilde{V}\Big(\frac{2}{3}\Big)\frac{A^{2/3}}{N(\pi\gamma^{2})}\Big|\sum_{b\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]}\frac{\beta_{b}}{N(b)^{1/6}}\Big|^{2}+O\Big(\frac{A^{2/3}B^{5/3}}{N(\pi\gamma^{2})w^{9/10}}\Big)

(9.16)

\displaystyle+O_{\varepsilon}\Big((\Delta N(\pi)X)^{\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{B^{2+2\eta}N(\pi)\Delta^{2}}{N(\pi\gamma^{2})}+\frac{X}{N(\pi\gamma^{2})}\big(1+(B^{2}/A)^{-1000}\big)\Big)\Big),

\displaystyle\mathscr{C}

\displaystyle=-2\widetilde{V}\Big(\frac{2}{3}\Big)\delta_{\gamma=1}\frac{2\pi}{9\sqrt{3}\zeta_{\mathbb{Q}(\omega)}(2;\mathbf{1}_{\pi})}\Big(\frac{(2\pi)^{2/3}}{3\Gamma(\tfrac{2}{3})}\Big)^{2}\frac{A^{2/3}}{N(\pi)}\Big|\sum_{b\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]}\frac{\beta_{b}}{N(b)^{1/6}}\Big|^{2}

\displaystyle+\delta_{\gamma=1}\Big(O_{\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{A^{2/3-\rho(\varepsilon)}B^{5/3-\rho(\varepsilon)}}{N(\pi)}\Big)+O_{\varepsilon}(X^{\varepsilon}N(\pi)^{1/2}B^{29/12}A^{-1/12})

(9.17)

\displaystyle+O\Big(\frac{A^{2/3}B^{5/3}}{N(\pi)}\Big(\frac{1}{w^{9/10}}+\frac{\delta_{\pi\neq 1}}{N(\pi)}\Big)\Big)\Big),

and

\displaystyle\mathscr{T}

\displaystyle=\delta_{\gamma=1}\widetilde{V}\Big(\frac{2}{3}\Big)\frac{2\pi}{9\sqrt{3}\zeta_{\mathbb{Q}(\omega)}(2;\mathbf{1}_{\pi})}\Big(\frac{(2\pi)^{2/3}}{3\Gamma(\tfrac{2}{3})}\Big)^{2}\frac{A^{2/3}}{N(\pi)}\Big|\sum_{b\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]}\frac{\beta_{b}}{N(b)^{1/6}}\Big|^{2}

(9.18)

\displaystyle+\delta_{\gamma=1}\cdot\delta_{\pi\neq 1}\cdot O\Big(\frac{A^{2/3}B^{5/3}}{N(\pi)^{2}}\Big)+\delta_{\gamma=1}O_{\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{A^{1/6+\varepsilon}B^{5/3}}{N(\pi)^{1/2+\varepsilon}}\Big).

Thus
\mathscr{D}+\mathscr{C}+\mathscr{T}
using the asymptotics (
9
), (
9
) and (
9
) respectively
gives an asymptotic expression for (
9.12
). Substitution of this asymptotic expression into
(
9.9
), and subsititution of (
9.11
) into (
9.10
), will give the result.

We now turn our attention to proving (
9
)–(
9
).

The diagonal term
\mathscr{D}

After expansion of the square in (
9.13
),
we obtain

\mathscr{D}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b_{1},b_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
(b_{1}b_{2},\pi\gamma)=1\end{subarray}}\beta_{b_{1}}\tilde{g}(b_{1})\overline{\beta_{b_{2}}\tilde{g}(b_{2})}\Big(\frac{\pi^{2}\gamma}{b_{1}}\Big)_{3}\overline{\Big(\frac{\pi^{2}\gamma}{b_{2}}\Big)_{3}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
a\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}V\Big(\frac{N(\pi\gamma^{2})N(a)}{A}\Big)\overline{\Big(\frac{a}{b_{1}}\Big)_{3}}\Big(\frac{a}{b_{2}}\Big)_{3}.

If
(b_{1},b_{2})=d
, then recall that (
7.2
) tells us that

\tilde{g}(b_{1})\overline{\tilde{g}(b_{2})}=\tilde{g}(b_{1}/d)\overline{\tilde{g}(b_{2}/d)}\overline{\Big(\frac{d}{b_{1}/d}\Big)_{3}}\Big(\frac{d}{b_{2}/d}\Big)_{3}.

Thus an application of Poisson summation (in the form of Corollary
4.1
) on the
a
sum shows that

\displaystyle\mathscr{D}

\displaystyle=\frac{4\pi A}{9\sqrt{3}N(\pi\gamma^{2})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
d\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
(d,\pi\gamma)=1\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b_{1},b_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
(b_{1},b_{2})=d\end{subarray}}\frac{\beta_{b_{1}}\overline{\beta_{b_{2}}}}{\sqrt{N(b_{1}b_{2})}}

(9.19)

\displaystyle\times\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]}\widetilde{c_{d}}(k)\Big(\frac{d\pi^{2}\gamma k}{b_{1}/d}\Big)_{3}\overline{\Big(\frac{d\pi^{2}\gamma k}{b_{2}/d}\Big)_{3}}\ddot{V}\Big(\frac{kd\sqrt{A}}{b_{1}b_{2}N(\pi\gamma^{2})^{1/2}}\Big).

.
Observe that (
4.8
) and Lemma
5.5
imply that

\widetilde{c_{d}}\big((d\gamma)^{2}\pi H\big)=\check{e}\big({-\frac{(d\gamma)^{2}\pi H}{3\lambda}}\big)\varphi(d)
.
Thus

\displaystyle\mathscr{D}_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{4\pi A}{9\sqrt{3}N(\pi\gamma^{2})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
d\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
(d,\pi\gamma)=1\end{subarray}}\varphi(d)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b_{1},b_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
(b_{1},b_{2})=d\\
(b_{1}b_{2}/d^{2},\pi\gamma)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\beta_{b_{1}}\overline{\beta_{b_{2}}}}{\sqrt{N(b_{1}b_{2})}}

(9.20)

\displaystyle\times\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}H\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
H=\hbox to5.21pt{\vbox to5.21pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}

\lxSVG@closescope {{}}{}{{}}{}{{}}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setdash{0.4pt,1.0pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 1.54 1.54 L 1.54 6.65}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {{}}{}{{}}{}{{}}

{{}}{}{{}}{}{{}}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setdash{0.4pt,1.0pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 1.54 1.54 L 6.65 1.54}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\\
(H,b_{1}b_{2}/d^{2})=1\end{subarray}}\check{e}\Big({-\frac{(d\gamma)^{2}\pi H}{3\lambda}}\Big)\ddot{V}\Big(\frac{d^{3}H\sqrt{A}}{b_{1}b_{2}}\Big).

We further write

\mathscr{D}_{1}=\mathscr{D}^{\star}_{1}+\mathscr{D}^{\diamond}_{1}
,
where
\mathscr{D}^{\star}_{1}
denotes the sum in (
9
)
restricted to
d=1
, and
\mathscr{D}^{\diamond}_{1}
denotes the
sum in (
9
) restricted to
d\neq 1
.
The support of
\bm{\beta}
guarantees that

d\neq 1
implies that
N(d)>w
. Thus by
Lemma
4.4
we have

\displaystyle\mathscr{D}_{1}^{\diamond}

\displaystyle\ll\frac{A}{N(\pi\gamma^{2})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
(d,\pi\gamma)=1\\
d\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
N(d)>w\end{subarray}}\varphi(d)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b_{1},b_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
(b_{1},b_{2})=d\\
(b_{1}b_{2}/d^{2},\pi\gamma)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{|\beta_{b_{1}}\beta_{b_{2}}|}{\sqrt{N(b_{1})N(b_{2})}}\Big(\frac{N(b_{1}b_{2})^{1/3}}{N(d)A^{1/3}}+1\Big)

(9.21)

\displaystyle\ll_{\varepsilon}\frac{A^{2/3}B^{5/3}}{wN(\pi\gamma^{2})}+\frac{ABX^{\varepsilon}}{N(\pi\gamma^{2})}\ll_{\varepsilon}\frac{A^{2/3}B^{5/3}}{wN(\pi\gamma^{2})}+\frac{X^{1+\varepsilon}}{N(\pi\gamma^{2})}.

We now consider
\mathscr{D}_{1}^{\star}
.
We write
H=h^{3}
with
0\neq h\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]

(
h
is necessarily non-zero in this case).
We have

(9.22)

\check{e}\Big({-\frac{\gamma^{2}\pi h^{3}}{3\lambda}}\Big)=\check{e}\Big({-\frac{h^{3}}{3\lambda}}\Big)=1.

This can be seen by writing
h=\zeta\lambda^{i}u
with
u\equiv 1\pmod{3}
,
\zeta\in\{\pm 1,\pm\omega,\pm\omega^{2}\}
,
and
i\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
. Then the last equality in (
9.22
) follows from

\check{e}\Big({-\frac{(\zeta\lambda^{i}u)^{3}}{3\lambda}}\Big)=\check{e}\Big(\pm\frac{\lambda^{3i}}{3\lambda}\Big)=1.

Thus

(9.23)

\mathscr{D}_{1}^{\star}=\frac{A}{N(\pi\gamma^{2})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b_{1},b_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
(b_{1},b_{2})=1\\
(b_{1}b_{2},\pi\gamma)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\beta_{b_{1}}\overline{\beta_{b_{2}}}}{\sqrt{N(b_{1})N(b_{2})}}\cdot\frac{4\pi}{3^{3}\sqrt{3}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
(h,b_{1}b_{2})=1\end{subarray}}\ddot{V}\Big(\frac{h^{3}\sqrt{A}}{b_{1}b_{2}}\Big).

Note that the extra factor of
1/3
in the above display accounts for the fact that
(\omega^{i}h)^{3}=h^{3}
for
i\in\{0,1,2\}

and
0\neq h\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
. We remove the condition
(h,b_{1}b_{2})=1

at negligible cost since
\bm{\beta}
is supported on
w
-rough squarefree integers in
\mathbb{Z}[\omega]

with
w>(\log X)^{10}
.
Thus

\displaystyle\mathscr{D}_{1}^{\star}

\displaystyle=\frac{A}{N(\pi\gamma^{2})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b_{1},b_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
(b_{1},b_{2})=1\\
(b_{1}b_{2},\pi\gamma)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\beta_{b_{1}}\overline{\beta_{b_{2}}}}{\sqrt{N(b_{1})N(b_{2})}}\cdot\frac{4\pi}{3^{3}\sqrt{3}}\sum_{h\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]}\ddot{V}\Big(\frac{h^{3}\sqrt{A}}{b_{1}b_{2}}\Big)

(9.24)

\displaystyle+O\Big(\frac{A^{2/3}B^{5/3}}{N(\pi\gamma^{2})w^{9/10}}\Big)+O\Big(\frac{AB}{N(\pi\gamma^{2})w^{9/10}}\Big).

Observe that
\ddot{V}(u)=\ddot{V}(|u|)
is a Schwarz function by Lemma
4.4
.
Application of Poisson summation (in the form of Lemma
4.1
)
to the sum over
h\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
yields

(9.25)

\displaystyle\frac{4\pi}{3^{3}\sqrt{3}}

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\end{subarray}}\ddot{V}\Big(\frac{h^{3}\sqrt{A}}{b_{1}b_{2}}\Big)=\frac{8\pi}{3^{4}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\end{subarray}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\ddot{V}\Big(\frac{(x+iy)^{3}\sqrt{A}}{b_{1}b_{2}}\Big)\check{e}\Big(\frac{m(x+iy)}{\lambda}\Big)dxdy.

We simplify the right side of (
9.25
).
Recall that
\ddot{V}(u)=\ddot{V}(|u|)
is radial. After changing
x+iy
into polar coordinates
re^{i\vartheta}
, the right side
of (
9.25
) becomes

\displaystyle\frac{8\pi}{3^{4}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}\ddot{V}\Big(\frac{r^{3}\sqrt{A}}{\sqrt{N(b_{1}b_{2})}}\Big)\check{e}\Big(\frac{mre^{i\vartheta}}{\lambda}\Big)rdrd\vartheta

(9.26)

\displaystyle=\frac{8\pi}{3^{4}}\frac{N(b_{1}b_{2})^{1/3}}{A^{1/3}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\end{subarray}}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}\ddot{V}(r^{3})\check{e}\Big(\frac{mre^{i\vartheta}}{\lambda}\frac{N(b_{1}b_{2})^{1/6}}{A^{1/6}}\Big)rdrd\vartheta.

For all
m\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
, Lemma
4.4
implies that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}

\displaystyle\ddot{V}(r^{3})\check{e}\Big(\frac{mre^{i\vartheta}}{\lambda}\frac{N(b_{1}b_{2})^{1/6}}{A^{1/6}}\Big)rdrd\vartheta

(9.27)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}\ddot{V}(r^{3})\delta_{[0,X^{\varepsilon}]}(r)\check{e}\Big(\frac{mre^{i\vartheta}}{\lambda}\frac{N(b_{1}b_{2})^{1/6}}{A^{1/6}}\Big)rdrd\vartheta+O_{\varepsilon}(X^{-2000}).

For
0\neq m\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
,
repeated integration by parts on the right side of (
9
) shows that

(9.28)

\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}\ddot{V}(r^{3})\delta_{[0,X^{\varepsilon}]}(r)\check{e}\Big(\frac{mre^{i\vartheta}}{\lambda}\frac{N(b_{1}b_{2})^{1/6}}{A^{1/6}}\Big)rdrd\vartheta\ll_{\varepsilon}X^{\varepsilon}(B^{2}/A)^{-1000}N(m)^{-1000}.

We substitute (
9
) and (
9.28
) into (
9.26
), and then sum trivially over
0\neq m\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
.
Chasing the result through (
9.25
) gives

(9.29)

\frac{4\pi}{3^{3}\sqrt{3}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\end{subarray}}\ddot{V}\Big(\frac{h^{3}\sqrt{A}}{b_{1}b_{2}}\Big)=\frac{16\pi^{2}}{3^{4}}\frac{N(b_{1}b_{2})^{1/3}}{A^{1/3}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\ddot{V}(r^{3})rdr+O_{\varepsilon}\big(X^{\varepsilon}(B^{2}/A)^{-1000}\big).

We now evaluate the main term on the right side of (
9.29
).
We open
\ddot{V}
using the definition (
4.4
), and find that the main term is

(9.30)

\frac{16\pi^{2}}{3^{4}}\frac{N(b_{1}b_{2})^{1/3}}{A^{1/3}}\int_{0}^{\infty}uV(u^{2})\int_{0}^{\infty}J_{0}\Big(\frac{4\pi r^{3}u}{3\sqrt{3}}\Big)rdrdu.

For each fixed
u\in(0,\infty)
, we make the change of variable

w=4\pi r^{3}u/(3\sqrt{3})
in the
r
-integral.
Thus (
9.30
) becomes

(9.31)

\frac{(4\pi)^{4/3}}{3^{4}}\frac{N(b_{1}b_{2})^{1/3}}{A^{1/3}}\int_{0}^{\infty}u^{1/3}V(u^{2})du\int_{0}^{\infty}J_{0}(w)w^{-1/3}dw.

A change of variable shows that

(9.32)

\int_{0}^{\infty}u^{s-1}V(u^{2})du=\frac{1}{2}\widetilde{V}\Big(\frac{s}{2}\Big),\quad\text{for}\quad s\in\mathbb{C},

and
[
DLMF
, (10.22.43)]
implies that

(9.33)

\int_{0}^{\infty}w^{s-1}J_{0}(w)dw=\frac{2^{s-1}\Gamma(\frac{s}{2})}{\Gamma(1-\frac{s}{2})},\quad\text{for}-1<\operatorname{Re}(s-1)<1/2.

Using (
9.32
), (
9.33
) and Euler’s reflection formula
[
DLMF
, (5.5.3)]
,
we see that (
9.31
)
becomes

(9.34)

\frac{2\pi}{9\sqrt{3}}\Big(\frac{(2\pi)^{2/3}}{3\Gamma(\frac{2}{3})}\Big)^{2}\widetilde{V}\Big(\frac{2}{3}\Big)\frac{N(b_{1}b_{2})^{1/3}}{A^{1/3}}.

After retracing (
9.29
) (
9.30
), (
9.31
) and (
9.34
), we obtain

(9.35)

\frac{4\pi}{3^{3}\sqrt{3}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\end{subarray}}\ddot{V}\Big(\frac{h^{3}\sqrt{A}}{b_{1}b_{2}}\Big)=\frac{2\pi}{9\sqrt{3}}\Big(\frac{(2\pi)^{2/3}}{3\Gamma(\frac{2}{3})}\Big)^{2}\widetilde{V}\Big(\frac{2}{3}\Big)\frac{N(b_{1}b_{2})^{1/3}}{A^{1/3}}+O_{\varepsilon}\big(X^{\varepsilon}(B^{2}/A)^{-1000}\big).

Substitution of (
9.35
) into (
9
) gives

\displaystyle\mathscr{D}_{1}^{\star}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\pi}{9\sqrt{3}}\Big(\frac{(2\pi)^{2/3}}{3\Gamma(\tfrac{2}{3})}\Big)^{2}\widetilde{V}\Big(\frac{2}{3}\Big)\frac{A^{2/3}}{N(\pi\gamma^{2})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b_{1},b_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
(b_{1},b_{2})=1\\
(b_{1}b_{2},\pi\gamma)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\beta_{b_{1}}\overline{\beta_{b_{2}}}}{N(b_{1})^{1/6}N(b_{2})^{1/6}}

(9.36)

\displaystyle+O\Big(\frac{A^{2/3}B^{5/3}}{N(\pi\gamma^{2})w^{9/10}}\Big)+O\Big(\frac{AB}{N(\pi\gamma^{2})w^{9/10}}\Big)+O_{\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{X^{1+\varepsilon}}{N(\pi\gamma^{2})}(B^{2}/A)^{-1000}\Big).

Using
w
-roughness of the support of
\bm{\beta}
,
we drop the conditions
(b_{1},b_{2})=1
and
(b_{1}b_{2},\pi\gamma)=1
at the expense of the error term of the same order
of magnitude of that occurring in (
9
).
After recalling that
\mathscr{D}_{1}=\mathscr{D}_{1}^{\star}+\mathscr{D}_{1}^{\diamond}
, (
9
), and (
9
), we
obtain

\displaystyle\mathscr{D}_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\pi}{9\sqrt{3}}\Big(\frac{(2\pi)^{2/3}}{3\Gamma(\tfrac{2}{3})}\Big)^{2}\widetilde{V}\Big(\frac{2}{3}\Big)\frac{A^{2/3}}{N(\pi\gamma^{2})}\Big|\sum_{b\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]}\frac{\beta_{b}}{N(b)^{1/6}}\Big|^{2}

(9.37)

\displaystyle+O\Big(\frac{A^{2/3}B^{5/3}}{N(\pi\gamma^{2})w^{9/10}}\Big)+O_{\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{X^{1+\varepsilon}}{N(\pi\gamma^{2})}\big(1+(B^{2}/A)^{-1000}\big)\Big).

We now consider
\mathscr{D}_{2}
,

\displaystyle\mathscr{D}_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{4\pi A}{9\sqrt{3}N(\pi\gamma^{2})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
d\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
(d,\pi\gamma)=1\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b_{1},b_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
(b_{1},b_{2})=d\end{subarray}}\frac{\beta_{b_{1}}\overline{\beta_{b_{2}}}}{\sqrt{N(b_{1}b_{2})}}

(9.38)

\displaystyle\times\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
d\pi^{2}\gamma k\neq\hbox to5.21pt{\vbox to5.21pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}

The cross terms
\mathscr{C}

Recall that (
9
) records the cross term.
Observe that (
2.3
) tells us that

(9.41)

\tilde{g}(b_{1})\tilde{g}(a\pi)\overline{\Big(\frac{a}{b_{1}}\Big)_{3}}\Big(\frac{\pi^{2}}{b_{1}}\Big)_{3}=\tilde{g}(a\pi b_{1}).

Substituting (

(9.42)

\displaystyle\mathscr{C}:=-2\frac{(2\pi)^{2/3}}{3\Gamma(\tfrac{2}{3})}\delta_{\gamma=1}\text{Re}\Big(

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b_{1},b_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\end{subarray}}\frac{\beta_{b_{1}}\overline{\beta_{b_{2}}}}{N(b_{2})^{1/6}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
a\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
(a\pi,b_{1})=1\end{subarray}}V\Big(\frac{N(a)N(\pi)}{A}\Big)\frac{\tilde{g}(a\pi b_{1})}{N(a\pi)^{1/6}}\Big).

We now evaluate the sum over
a\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
in (
9.42
) using our asymptotic formula for type-I sums
in Proposition
8.1
(for level
\pi b_{1}
). Thus there exists
\rho(\varepsilon)\in(0,\frac{1}{10000})
such that

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\beta_{b_{1}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
a\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}V\Big(\frac{N(a)N(\pi)}{A}\Big)\frac{\tilde{g}(a\pi b_{1})}{N(a\pi)^{1/6}}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{V}\Big(\frac{2}{3}\Big)\frac{(2\pi)^{5/3}A^{2/3}}{3^{7/2}\Gamma(\tfrac{2}{3})N(\pi)^{5/6}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\end{subarray}}\frac{\beta_{b_{1}}\varphi(\pi b_{1})}{\zeta_{\mathbb{Q}(\omega)}(2;\mathbf{1}_{\pi b_{1}})N(\pi b_{1})^{7/6}}

(9.43)

\displaystyle+O_{\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{A^{2/3-\rho(\varepsilon)}B^{5/6-\rho(\varepsilon)}}{N(\pi)}\Big)+O_{\varepsilon}\big(X^{\varepsilon}N(\pi)^{1/2}B^{19/12}A^{-1/12}\big).

We now use the fact that
\bm{\beta}
is supported on
w
-rough squarefree elements of
\mathbb{Z}[\omega]

that are congruent to

modulo

. We have

\log\Big(\frac{\varphi(\pi b_{1})}{N(\pi b_{1})}\Big)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\varpi\mid\pi b_{1}\\

\varpi\text{ prime}\end{subarray}}\sum_{L=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{LN(\varpi)^{L}}=O\Big(\frac{1}{w^{9/10}}+\frac{\delta_{\pi\neq 1}}{N(\pi)}\Big).

Thus

(9.44)

\frac{\varphi(\pi b_{1})}{N(\pi b_{1})}=1+O\Big(\frac{1}{w^{9/10}}+\frac{\delta_{\pi\neq 1}}{N(\pi)}\Big).

Similarly, we also have

(9.45)

\frac{1}{\zeta_{\mathbb{Q}(\omega)}(2;\mathbf{1}_{\pi b_{1}})}=\frac{1}{\zeta_{\mathbb{Q}(\omega)}(2;\mathbf{1}_{\pi})}+O\Big(\frac{1}{w^{19/10}}\Big).

\displaystyle=\widetilde{V}\Big(\frac{2}{3}\Big)\frac{(2\pi)^{5/3}A^{2/3}}{3^{7/2}\Gamma(\tfrac{2}{3})\zeta_{\mathbb{Q}(\omega)}(2;\mathbf{1}_{\pi})N(\pi)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\end{subarray}}\frac{\beta_{b_{1}}}{N(b_{1})^{1/6}}+O_{\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{A^{2/3-\rho(\varepsilon)}B^{5/6-\rho(\varepsilon)}}{N(\pi)}\Big)

(9.46)

\displaystyle+O_{\varepsilon}(X^{\varepsilon}N(\pi)^{1/2}B^{19/12}A^{-1/12})+O\Big(\frac{A^{2/3}B^{5/6}}{N(\pi)}\Big(\frac{1}{w^{9/10}}+\frac{\delta_{\pi\neq 1}}{N(\pi)}\Big)\Big).

The trivial term

Recall that (

) records

(9.47)

\mathscr{T}=\Big(\frac{(2\pi)^{2/3}}{3\Gamma(\tfrac{2}{3})}\Big)^{2}\delta_{\gamma=1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\

Mellin inversion of the smooth function, the Class number formula

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{(2)}\widetilde{V}(s)\frac{\zeta_{\mathbb{Q}(\omega)}(s+1/3;\mathbf{1}_{\pi})}{\zeta_{\mathbb{Q}(\omega)}(2s+2/3;\mathbf{1}_{\pi})}\frac{A^{s}}{N(\pi)^{s+1/3}}ds

\displaystyle=\widetilde{V}\Big(\frac{2}{3}\Big)(1-\delta_{\pi\neq 1}\cdot N(\pi)^{-1})\frac{2\pi A^{2/3}}{9\sqrt{3}\zeta_{\mathbb{Q}(\omega)}(2;\mathbf{1}_{\pi})N(\pi)}+O_{\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{A^{1/6+\varepsilon}}{N(\pi)^{1/2+\varepsilon}}\Big)

(9.48)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{V}\Big(\frac{2}{3}\Big)\frac{2\pi A^{2/3}}{9\sqrt{3}\zeta_{\mathbb{Q}(\omega)}(2;\textbf{1}_{\pi})N(\pi)}+\delta_{\pi\neq 1}O\Big(\frac{A^{2/3}}{N(\pi)^{2}}\Big)+O_{\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{A^{1/6+\varepsilon}}{N(\pi)^{1/2+\varepsilon}}\Big).

Insertion of (
9
) into (
9.47
) gives (
9
).
∎

Lemma 9.2
.

Given
y\geq 1
, there exists coefficients
(\lambda_{d})_{d\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]}
such that

(1)

\lambda_{1}=1
and
|\lambda_{d}|\ll_{\varepsilon}N(d)^{\varepsilon}
for all
d\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
and all
\varepsilon>0
;

(2)

\lambda_{d}=0
if
N(d)>y^{2}
or
d\not\equiv 1\pmod{3}
;

(3)

For all
n\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
we have

(9.49)

\mu^{2}(n)\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d^{2}|n\end{subarray}}\lambda_{d};

(4)

For any
\varepsilon>0
and
\pi\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
a prime
\pi\equiv 1\pmod{3}
(or 1) we have

(9.50)

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
(d,\pi)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\lambda_{d}}{N(d)^{2}}=\frac{1}{\zeta_{\mathbb{Q}(\omega)}(2;\mathbf{1}_{\pi})}+O_{\varepsilon}(y^{-1/2+\varepsilon}).

Proof.

Given
d\equiv 1\pmod{3}
, let

\lambda_{d}:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}N(e),N(f)\leq y\\
e,f\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
d=[e,f]\end{subarray}}\mu(e)\mu(f)

Properties (
1
) and (
2
) are immediate from the definition. Property (
3
) follows from

\sum_{d^{2}\mid n}\lambda_{d}=\Big(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}N(e)\leq y\\
e^{2}\mid n\end{subarray}}\mu(e)\Big)^{2}.

It remains to check property (
4
). We have

(9.51)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
(d,\pi)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\lambda_{d}}{N(d)^{2}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}N(e),N(f)\leq y\\
e,f\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
(ef,\pi)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(e)\mu(f)}{N([e,f])^{2}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}e,f\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
(ef,\pi)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(e)\mu(f)}{N([e,f])^{2}}+O(y^{-1/2+\varepsilon}).

The main term in (
9.51
) is equal to

\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\varpi\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
\varpi\neq\pi\end{subarray}}\Big(1+\frac{1}{N(\varpi)^{2}}(-2+1)\Big)=\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\varpi\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
\varpi\neq\pi\end{subarray}}\Big(1-\frac{1}{N(\varpi)^{2}}\Big)=\frac{1}{\zeta_{\mathbb{Q}(\omega)}(2;\mathbf{1}_{\pi})},

as required.
∎

Proof of Proposition
9.1
.

In the first display of the statement of Proposition

9.1
we use property (
3
) in Lemma
9.2
with
y=X^{\varepsilon}
with
\varepsilon>0
small and fixed,
and inclusion-exclusion on the condition
(a,b)=1
.
We see that the first display in Proposition

is

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\

\pi\mid a\end{subarray}}V\Big(\frac{N(a)}{A}\Big)\Big|\Big(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
(b,\gamma a)=1\end{subarray}}\beta_{b}\tilde{g}(b)\overline{\Big(\frac{b}{a}\Big)_{3}}-\frac{(2\pi)^{2/3}}{3\Gamma(\tfrac{2}{3})}\frac{\overline{\tilde{g}(a)}}{N(a)^{1/6}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\end{subarray}}\frac{\beta_{b}}{N(b)^{1/6}}\Big)

(9.52)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\Big(\frac{(2\pi)^{2/3}}{3\Gamma(\tfrac{2}{3})}\frac{\overline{\tilde{g}(a)}}{N(a)^{1/6}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
(b,a)\neq 1\end{subarray}}\frac{\beta_{b}}{N(b)^{1/6}}\Big)\Big|^{2}\Big(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma^{2}|a\end{subarray}}\lambda_{\gamma}\Big).

Since

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma^{2}|a\end{subarray}}\lambda_{\gamma}\geq 0
, we can apply the parallelogram inequality (

)
to (

).
We see that the right side of (

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
a\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
\pi\mid a\end{subarray}}V\Big(\frac{N(a)}{A}\Big)\Big(\Big|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
(b,\gamma a)=1\end{subarray}}\beta_{b}\tilde{g}(b)\overline{\Big(\frac{b}{a}\Big)_{3}}-\frac{(2\pi)^{2/3}}{3\Gamma(\tfrac{2}{3})}\frac{\overline{\tilde{g}(a)}}{N(a)^{1/6}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\end{subarray}}\frac{\beta_{b}}{N(b)^{1/6}}\Big|^{2}

(9.53)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{(2\pi)^{4/3}}{9\Gamma(\tfrac{2}{3})^{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
a\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
\pi\mid a\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu^{2}(a)}{N(a)^{1/3}}V\Big(\frac{N(a)}{A}\Big)\Big|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
(b,a)\neq 1\end{subarray}}\frac{\beta_{b}}{N(b)^{1/6}}\Big|^{2}\Big)\Big(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma^{2}|a\end{subarray}}\lambda_{\gamma}\Big).

We interchange the order of summation and use (
2.2
) in (

). We see that the right side of (

) is equal to

\displaystyle 2\Big(\sum_{\gamma\equiv 1\pmod{3}}\lambda_{\gamma}

(9.54)

\displaystyle\times\Big(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
a\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
\pi\mid a,\gamma^{2}\mid a\end{subarray}}V\Big(\frac{N(a)}{A}\Big)\Big|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
(b,\gamma a)=1\end{subarray}}\beta_{b}\tilde{g}(b)\overline{\Big(\frac{b}{a}\Big)_{3}}-\frac{(2\pi)^{2/3}}{3\Gamma(\tfrac{2}{3})}\frac{\delta_{\gamma=1}\overline{\tilde{g}(a)}}{N(a)^{1/6}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\end{subarray}}\frac{\beta_{b}}{N(b)^{1/6}}\Big|^{2}

(9.55)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\delta_{\gamma=1}\frac{(2\pi)^{4/3}}{9\Gamma(\tfrac{2}{3})^{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
a\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
\pi\mid a\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu^{2}(a)}{N(a)^{1/3}}V\Big(\frac{N(a)}{A}\Big)\Big|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
(b,a)\neq 1\end{subarray}}\frac{\beta_{b}}{N(b)^{1/6}}\Big|^{2}\Big)\Big).

Observe that (

), and is estimated by (

).
If

, then the divisibility condition in (

) becomes

.
Thus (

), and is estimated asymptotically by
by
\mathscr{D}+\mathscr{C}+\mathscr{T}
, where
\mathscr{D}
,
\mathscr{C}

and

),

Summing the asymptotic expression for

)

with

and
