∎

10.
Broad Type II estimates

We prove the following type-II estimates for sequences in
\mathcal{C}_{\eta}(\cdot,w)
.

Proposition 10.1
.

Let
W
be a smooth function compactly supported in
[1,2]
,

0<\eta\leq 1/4
,
A,B\geq 10
and set
X:=AB
.
Let
\bm{\alpha}
be a sequence supported in
N(a)\in[A/10,10A]
with
a\equiv 1\pmod{3}
squarefree. Suppose that
w>(\log X)^{10}
,
\bm{\beta}=(\beta_{b})\in\mathcal{C}_{\eta}(B,w)
,
\varepsilon\in(0,\frac{1}{10000})
and
\pi\equiv 1\pmod{3}
a prime or
1
satisfying
1\leq N(\pi)<w
.
Then there exists
\rho(\varepsilon)\in(0,\frac{1}{10000})
such that

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a,b\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
\pi\mid a\end{subarray}}\alpha_{a}\beta_{b}\tilde{g}(ab)W\Big(\frac{N(ab)}{X}\Big)=\frac{(2\pi)^{2/3}}{3\Gamma(\tfrac{2}{3})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a,b\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
\pi\mid a\end{subarray}}\frac{\alpha_{a}\beta_{b}\mu^{2}(ab)}{N(ab)^{1/6}}W\Big(\frac{N(ab)}{X}\Big)

\displaystyle+O_{\varepsilon}\Big(\Big(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
\pi\mid a\end{subarray}}|\alpha_{a}|^{2}\Big)^{1/2}\cdot\Big(\frac{A^{1/3}B^{5/6}}{N(\pi)^{1/2}}\Big(\frac{1}{w^{9/20}}+\frac{\delta_{\pi\neq 1}}{N(\pi)^{1/2}}\Big)+\frac{A^{1/3-\rho(\varepsilon)/2}B^{5/6-\rho(\varepsilon)/2}}{N(\pi)^{1/2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{A^{1/12+\varepsilon/2}B^{5/6}}{N(\pi)^{1/4+\varepsilon/2}}+(XN(\pi))^{\varepsilon/2}\Big(N(\pi)^{1/4}B^{29/24}A^{-1/24}+B^{1+\eta}+\frac{X^{1/2}}{N(\pi)^{1/2}}\Big(1+(B^{2}/A)^{-500}\Big)\Big)\Big)\Big).

Remark 10.1
.

Suppose
\pi=1
say. Then for dense sequences
\bm{\alpha},\bm{\beta}
and given
\xi>0
, the result is non-trivial in the range
X^{1/3+\xi}\leq B\leq X^{1/2-\xi}
,
for some appropriate choice of
\varepsilon,\eta
and
w
.

Proof.

Observe that (
2.3
) gives

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a,b\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
\pi\mid a\end{subarray}}\alpha_{a}\beta_{b}\Big(\tilde{g}(ab)-\frac{(2\pi)^{2/3}}{3\Gamma(\tfrac{2}{3})}\frac{\mu^{2}(ab)}{N(ab)^{1/6}}\Big)W\Big(\frac{N(ab)}{X}\Big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a,b\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
\pi\mid a\\
(a,b)=1\end{subarray}}\alpha_{a}\beta_{b}\Big(\tilde{g}(ab)-\frac{(2\pi)^{2/3}}{3\Gamma(\tfrac{2}{3})}\frac{1}{N(ab)^{1/6}}\Big)W\Big(\frac{N(ab)}{X}\Big)

(10.1)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{-i\infty}^{i\infty}\widetilde{W}(s)X^{s}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
\pi\mid a\end{subarray}}\frac{\alpha_{a}\tilde{g}(a)}{N(a)^{s}}\Big(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
(b,a)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\beta_{b}\tilde{g}(b)}{N(b)^{s}}\overline{\Big(\frac{a}{b}\Big)_{3}}-\frac{(2\pi)^{2/3}}{3\Gamma(\tfrac{2}{3})}\frac{\overline{\tilde{g}(a)}}{N(a)^{1/6}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
(b,a)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\beta_{b}}{N(b)^{1/6+s}}\Big)ds.

Application of triangle inequality and then Cauchy-Schwarz to the
a
-sum shows that

\displaystyle|\eqref{keyexpr}|^{2}\ll

\displaystyle\Big(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
a\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
\pi\mid a\end{subarray}}|\alpha_{a}|^{2}\Big)\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|\widetilde{W}(it)|\Big(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
\pi\mid a\end{subarray}}\mu^{2}(a)V\Big(\frac{N(a)}{A}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\Big|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
(b,a)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\beta_{b}\tilde{g}(b)}{N(b)^{it}}\overline{\Big(\frac{a}{b}\Big)_{3}}-\frac{(2\pi)^{2/3}}{3\Gamma(\tfrac{2}{3})}\frac{\overline{\tilde{g}(a)}}{N(a)^{1/6}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
(b,a)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\beta_{b}}{N(b)^{1/6+it}}\Big|^{2}\Big)dt,

where
V:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
a smooth compactly supported function such that
V\geq\bm{1}_{[1/10,10]}
.
Using Proposition
9.1
gives the result.
∎

11.
Average Type-I estimates

In this section we prove an average Type-I estimate. This average Type-I estimate will be more directly useful to us than the pointwise Type-I
estimate proved in Section
8
. Recall that
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
,
c\equiv 1\pmod{3}
, and

g_{\ell}(c):=\tilde{g}(c)\Big(\frac{c}{|c|}\Big)^{\ell}.

Proposition 11.1
.

Assume the Generalized Riemann Hypothesis for the Dedekind zeta function attached to
\mathbb{Q}(\omega)

twisted by
Größencharaktern.
Let
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
,

\varepsilon\in(0,\frac{1}{1000000})
,
A>1000
be large and fixed, and
(\log X)^{A}\leq w\leq X^{\varepsilon}
.
Let
V,W
be smooth functions, compactly supported in
[\tfrac{1}{2},4]
. Let
0<\eta<100/A

and
\bm{\alpha}=(\alpha_{r})\in\mathcal{C}_{\eta}(X,w)
. Then uniformly for
C>X^{2/3-\varepsilon}
and
|\ell|\leq X^{1/100}
we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
c\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
\varpi|c\implies N(\varpi)>w\end{subarray}}\alpha_{r}\tilde{g}_{\ell}(cr)V\Big(\frac{N(c)}{C}\Big)W\Big(\frac{N(cr)}{X}\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{(2\pi)^{2/3}}{3\Gamma(\tfrac{2}{3})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
c\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
\varpi|c\implies N(\varpi)>w\end{subarray}}\frac{\alpha_{r}\mu^{2}(cr)\big(\frac{cr}{|cr|}\big)^{\ell}}{N(cr)^{1/6}}

(11.1)

\displaystyle+O_{\varepsilon,A}\Big(\frac{X^{5/6}}{(\log X)^{\frac{9}{20}A-10}}\Big).

Proof of Proposition
11.1
.

Möbius inversion asserts that

(11.2)

\delta_{\varpi|c\implies N(\varpi)>w}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}u|c\\
u\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
\varpi|u\implies N(\varpi)\leq w\end{subarray}}\mu(u).

Using (
11.2
), we express the left side of (
11.1
) as

(11.3)

\sum_{k\geq 0}(-1)^{k}\sum_{N\text{ dyadic}}\mathscr{B}_{k,\ell}(N),

where

(11.4)

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}_{k,\ell}

\displaystyle(N):=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r,u,n\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
u,n\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}\alpha_{r}\tilde{g}_{\ell}(nru)\Big(\delta_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega(u)=k\\
\varpi\mid u\implies N(\varpi)\leq w\end{subarray}}\Big)V\Big(\frac{N(n)}{N}\Big)V\Big(\frac{N(nu)}{C}\Big)W\Big(\frac{N(nru)}{X}\Big).

Case 1:
N\geq X^{1/2+1/20}

Consulting Remark
8.4
we see that we in the range
where Corollary
8.1
is non-trivial. Thus Corollary
8.1
and Remark
8.1
guarantee a small fixed
\delta_{0}>0

such that we have (uniformly in
|\ell|\leq X^{1/100}
),

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}_{k,\ell}(N):=\delta_{\ell=0}\cdot\frac{(2\pi)^{2/3}}{3\Gamma(\tfrac{2}{3})}

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r,u,n\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
u,n\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}\frac{\alpha_{r}\mu^{2}(nru)\big(\frac{nru}{|nru|}\big)^{\ell}}{N(nru)^{1/6}}\Big(\delta_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega(u)=k\\
\varpi\mid u\implies N(\varpi)\leq w\end{subarray}}\Big)

(11.5)

\displaystyle\times V\Big(\frac{N(n)}{N}\Big)V\Big(\frac{N(nu)}{C}\Big)W\Big(\frac{N(nru)}{X}\Big)+O_{\varepsilon}(X^{5/6-\delta_{0}}).

We can drop the condition
\delta_{\ell=0}
since if
\ell\neq 0
then the sum over
n
majorised by the error term in the above display.

Case 2:
N<X^{1/2+1/20}

Suppose we are given a squarefree
1\neq u\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
satisfying
u\equiv 1\pmod{3}
,
and such that all prime factors of
u
have norm
\leq w
.
Then, given
a prime
\pi|u
, there is a unique factorisation
u=b\pi d
such that all the prime factors of
d
(resp.
b
)
satisfy
<\pi
(resp. satisfy
>\pi)
. See Remark
6.1
for the ordering
<
on ideals.
Since
N(r)\asymp X/C\ll X^{1/3+\varepsilon}
and
N(ru)\asymp X/N\gg X^{1/2-1/20}
,
we are guaranteed a unique prime
\pi\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
such that
u=b\pi d
with
N(rb)<X^{13/30}
and
N(rb\pi)\geq X^{13/30}
.
Hence

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}_{k,\ell}(N):=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r,n\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
n\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}\alpha_{r}V\Big(\frac{N(n)}{N}\Big)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
u\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}\tilde{g}_{\ell}(nru)V\Big(\frac{N(nu)}{C}\Big)W\Big(\frac{N(nru)}{X}\Big)

(11.6)

\displaystyle\times\Big(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
\pi\text{ prime}\\
N(\pi)\leq w\\
\pi\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}\hskip 5.69046pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}u=b\pi d\\
\omega(u)=k\\
b,d\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
N(rb)<X^{13/30},N(rb\pi)\geq X^{13/30}\\
\varpi\mid b\implies w\geq N(\varpi)\geq N(\pi)\\
\varpi\mid d\implies N(\varpi)<N(\pi)\end{subarray}}1\Big).

We introduce smooth partitions of unity in the
N(r),N(b)
and
N(d)
in (
11
).
Then (
2.3
) and Mellin inversion imply that

(11.7)

\mathscr{B}_{k,\ell}(N)=\sum_{D,R,B\text{ dyadic}}\hskip 5.69046pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(k_{1},k_{2})\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})^{2}\\
k_{1}+k_{2}=k-1\end{subarray}}\mathscr{B}_{(k_{1},k_{2}),\ell}(N,D,R,B),

where

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}_{(k_{1},k_{2}),\ell}(N,D,R,B)=\frac{1}{(2\pi i)^{2}}\int_{-i\infty}^{i\infty}\int_{-i\infty}^{i\infty}C^{s}X^{v}\widetilde{V}(s)\widetilde{W}(v)

(11.8)

\displaystyle\times\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega],\pi\text{ prime}\\
N(\pi)\leq w,\pi\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}\frac{\tilde{g}_{\ell}(\pi)}{N(\pi)^{s+v}}\Big(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j,h\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
j,h\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}\beta_{j}(\pi,k_{1};s,v,\ell)\gamma_{h}(\pi,k_{2};s,v,\ell)\tilde{g}(jh)\Big)dsdv,

and

(11.9)

\displaystyle\beta_{j}(\pi,k_{1};s,v,\ell)

\displaystyle:=\frac{\mu^{2}(j)}{N(j)^{v}}\overline{\Big(\frac{\pi}{j}\Big)_{3}}\Big(\frac{j}{|j|}\Big)^{\ell}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=rb\\
\omega(b)=k_{1}\\
r,b\equiv 1\pmod{3},(j,\pi)=1\\
N(j)<X^{13/30},N(j\pi)\geq X^{13/30}\\
\varpi\mid b\implies w\geq N(\varpi)\geq N(\pi)\end{subarray}}\alpha_{r}V\Big(\frac{N(r)}{R}\Big)V\Big(\frac{N(b)}{B}\Big)N(b)^{-s};

\displaystyle\gamma_{h}(\pi,k_{2};s,v,\ell)

\displaystyle:=\frac{\mu^{2}(h)}{N(h)^{s+v}}\overline{\Big(\frac{\pi}{h}\Big)_{3}}\Big(\frac{h}{|h|}\Big)^{\ell}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}h=nd\\
\omega(d)=k_{2}\\
n,d\equiv 1\pmod{3},(h,\pi)=1\\
\varpi\mid d\implies N(\varpi)<N(\pi)\end{subarray}}V\Big(\frac{N(n)}{N}\Big)V\Big(\frac{N(d)}{D}\Big).

We write

(11.10)

\beta_{j}(B,R,\pi,k_{1};s,v,\ell):=\beta_{j}(\pi,k_{1};s,v,\ell)\quad\text{and}\quad\gamma_{h}(N,D,k_{2},\pi;s,v,\ell):=\gamma_{h}(\pi,k_{2};s,v,\ell),

when we care to emphasise the dyadic ranges
B,R
and
N,D
that are present in the definitions of
\bm{\beta}

and
\bm{\gamma}
respectively.

For each given
\pi\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
prime, the
sum over
j
and
h
in (
11
) is empty unless

(11.11)

X^{13/30}/(1000N(\pi))\leq RB\leq 1000X^{13/30},

and
NDRB\asymp X/N(\pi)
.
Thus
ND\gg X^{17/30}
. Since
N<X^{1/2+1/20}
, we must have
D\gg X^{1/15-1/20}

whenever the sum over
j,h
in (
11
) is non-zero. We now write

(11.12)

\mathscr{B}_{(k_{1},k_{2}),\ell}(N,D,R,B)=\mathscr{B}^{\dagger}_{(k_{1},k_{2}),\ell}(N,D,R,B)+\mathscr{B}^{\star}_{(k_{1},k_{2}),\ell}(N,D,R,B),

where
\mathscr{B}^{\dagger}_{(k_{1},k_{2}),\ell}
corresponds the part of (
11
)
with
N(\pi)\leq(\log D)^{A}
, and
\mathscr{B}^{\star}_{(k_{1},k_{2}),\ell}

corresponds to
(\log D)^{A}<N(\pi)\leq w
.

Treatment of
\mathscr{B}^{\dagger}_{(k_{1},k_{2}),\ell}(N,D,R,B)

Since
(\alpha_{r})
is supported only on
w
-rough elements of
\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
,
the factorisation
j=rb
occurring in the definition of the sequence
\beta_{j}
is unique.
Thus
|\beta_{j}(\pi,k_{1};s,v,\ell)|\leq 1
for
\operatorname{Re}s,\operatorname{Re}v=0
.

On the other hand, the sequence
\gamma_{h}(N,D,\pi,k_{2};s,v,\ell)
is sparse
when
N(\pi)
is on log-power scales.
We pause the proof to illustrate this
in the following Lemma. We also make the crude observations that

\gamma_{h}
is supported on
h\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]

with
N(h)\asymp ND
,
and also satisfies
|\gamma_{h}(\pi,k_{2};s,v,\ell)|\leq 2^{\omega(h)}
for
\operatorname{Re}s,\operatorname{Re}v=0
.

Lemma 11.1
.

Let
N,D,A\geq 10
,
k_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,
and let
\pi\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
be a prime that satisfies
\pi\equiv 1\pmod{3}
and
N(\pi)\leq(\log D)^{A}
. Then

\sum_{h\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]}|\gamma_{h}(N,D,\pi,k_{2};s,v,\ell)|^{2}\ll_{A}(ND)^{o(1)}\cdot ND^{1-K/A},

with
K>0
a small absolute constant.

Proof of Lemma
11.1
.

We first refine our bound for
\|\bm{\gamma}\|_{\infty}
. We have

|\gamma_{h}(\pi,k_{2};s,v,\ell)|\leq 2^{\omega(h)}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{h\in\mathscr{U}_{\pi}}\ll 2^{\frac{2\log N(h)}{\log\log N(h)}}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{h\in\mathscr{U}_{\pi}},

where
\mathscr{U}_{\pi}
is the set of squarefree integers of the form
nd
with
n,d\equiv 1\pmod{3}
,
(nd,\pi)=1
,
N(n)\asymp N
,
N(d)\asymp D
,
and such that all of the prime factors of
d
have norm
<N(\pi)
.
Observe that
d
has necessarily
\geq\frac{\log D}{100\log N(\pi)}
(say) prime factors.
Therefore

(11.13)

\sum_{h\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]}|\gamma_{h}(N,D,\pi,k_{2};s,v,\ell)|^{2}\ll(ND)^{\frac{4}{\log\log(ND)}}\cdot|\mathscr{U}_{\pi}|.

Let
\rho>0
be chosen later. We have

\displaystyle|\mathscr{U}_{\pi}|

\displaystyle\ll(ND)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
\varpi|d\implies N(\varpi)<N(\pi)\\
\omega(d)\geq\frac{\log D}{100\log N(\pi)}\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu^{2}(d)}{N(d)}

\displaystyle\leq(ND)\cdot\exp\Big({-\frac{\rho\log D}{100\log N(\pi)}}\Big)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
\varpi|d\implies N(\varpi)<N(\pi)\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu^{2}(d)e^{\rho\omega(d)}}{N(d)}

\displaystyle\leq(ND)\cdot\exp\Big({-\frac{\rho\log D}{100\log N(\pi)}}\Big)\prod_{N(\varpi)<N(\pi)}\Big(1+\frac{e^{\rho}}{N(\varpi)}\Big)

\displaystyle\ll(ND)\cdot\exp\Big(2e^{\rho}\log\log N(\pi)-\frac{\rho\log D}{100\log N(\pi)}\Big)

\displaystyle\ll(ND)\cdot\exp\Big(2e^{\rho}\log N(\pi)-\frac{\rho\log D}{100\log N(\pi)}\Big)

(11.14)

\displaystyle\ll(ND)\cdot\exp\Big(2Ae^{\rho}\log\log D-\frac{\rho\log D}{100A\log\log D}\Big).

We choose

\rho:=\log\log D-1000\log\log\log D.

Thus (
11
) implies that

(11.15)

|\mathscr{U}_{\pi}|\ll_{A}ND^{1-1/(1000A)}

for all
D
(hence
X
) sufficiently large.
Thus (
11.13
) now implies the Lemma.
∎

We now resume the proof of Proposition
11.1
.
We now use (
2.3
), the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, Heath-Brown’s cubic large sieve (Theorem
9.1
)
Lemma
11.1
, and the conditions (
11.11
),
NDRB\asymp X/N(\pi)
and
D\gg X^{1/15-1/20}
to obtain

\displaystyle|\mathscr{B}^{\dagger}_{(k_{1},k_{2}),\ell}(N,D,R,B)|

\displaystyle\ll_{A}X^{o(1)}\hskip-11.38092pt\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
\pi\text{ prime}\\
N(\pi)\leq(\log D)^{A}\\
\pi\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}\Big((RB)^{1/2}\Big(RB+ND+(RBND)^{2/3}\Big)^{1/2}N^{1/2}D^{1/2-1/(500A)}\Big)

\displaystyle\ll_{A}X^{5/6-1/(1000000A)},

say. We include a redundant main term of size that is absorbed by the error term
i.e. we can write

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}^{\dagger}_{(k_{1},k_{2}),\ell}(N,D,R,B)

\displaystyle=\frac{(2\pi)^{2/3}}{3\Gamma(\tfrac{2}{3})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r,u,n\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
n\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}\frac{\alpha_{r}\mu^{2}(nru)(\frac{nru}{|nru|})^{\ell}}{N(nru)^{1/6}}V\Big(\frac{N(r)}{R}\Big)V\Big(\frac{N(n)}{N}\Big)V\Big(\frac{N(nu)}{C}\Big)W\Big(\frac{N(nru)}{X}\Big)

(11.16)

\displaystyle\times\Big(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
\pi\text{ prime}\\
N(\pi)\leq(\log D)^{A}\\
\pi\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}u=b\pi d\\
\omega(b)=k_{1},\omega(d)=k_{2}\\
b,d\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
N(rb)<X^{13/30}\\
N(rb\pi)\geq X^{13/30}\\
\varpi\mid b\implies w\geq N(\varpi)\geq N(\pi)\\
\varpi\mid d\implies N(\varpi)<N(\pi)\end{subarray}}V\Big(\frac{N(b)}{B}\Big)V\Big(\frac{N(d)}{D}\Big)\Big)+O_{A}(X^{5/6-1/(1000000A)}).

Treatment of
\mathscr{B}^{\star}_{k,\ell}(\cdots)

Recall that
(\log X)^{A}\leq w\leq X^{\varepsilon}
, and that

(\log X)^{A}<N(\pi)\leq w
. We reassemble the integral in the
v
-variable
in (
11
), and recover the smooth weight
W(N(jh\pi)/X)
.
By Lemma
6.1
and Lemma
6.3
we have

\bm{\beta}(R,B;\pi;s,\ell)\in\mathcal{C}_{\eta}(RB,N(\pi))
(from (
11.9
)) (after re-scaling by an appropriate absolute non-zero constant)
for all
\eta>100/A
.
We then apply Proposition
10.1
and see that there is a
\rho(\varepsilon)\in(0,\frac{1}{10000})
such that

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}^{\star}_{(k_{1},k_{2}),\ell}(N,D,R,B)

\displaystyle=\frac{(2\pi)^{2/3}}{3\Gamma(\tfrac{2}{3})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r,u,n\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
n\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}\frac{\alpha_{r}\mu^{2}(nru)(\frac{nru}{|nru|})^{\ell}}{N(nru)^{1/6}}V\Big(\frac{N(r)}{R}\Big)V\Big(\frac{N(n)}{N}\Big)V\Big(\frac{N(nu)}{C}\Big)W\Big(\frac{N(nru)}{X}\Big)

(11.17)

\displaystyle\times\Big(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]\\
\pi\text{ prime}\\
(\log D)^{A}<N(\pi)\leq w\\
\pi\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}u=b\pi d\\
\omega(b)=k_{1},\omega(d)=k_{2}\\
b,d\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
N(rb)<X^{13/30}\\
N(rb\pi)\geq X^{13/30}\\
\varpi\mid b\implies w\geq N(\varpi)\geq N(\pi)\\
\varpi\mid d\implies N(\varpi)<N(\pi)\end{subarray}}V\Big(\frac{N(b)}{B}\Big)V\Big(\frac{N(d)}{D}\Big)\Big)+E,

where

\displaystyle E

\displaystyle=O_{A,\varepsilon}\Big(\log\log w\Big(\frac{X^{5/6}}{w^{9/20}}+X^{5/6-\rho(\varepsilon)}\Big)+\frac{X^{5/6}}{(\log X)^{A/2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+X^{83/120+o(1)}w^{1/4}+X^{47/60+o(1)}w^{3/4}+X^{17/60+(13/30)(1+100/A)+o(1)}w^{1/2}\Big).

Note that both the error terms in (
11
) and (
11
) are uniform with respect to
\ell,k_{1}
and
k_{2}
.

Conclusion

After combining (
11
) and (
11
) in (
11.12
),
we obtain an asymptotic expression for
\mathscr{B}_{(k_{1},k_{2}),\ell}(N,D,R,B)

for each dyadic value of
N
satisfying
N<X^{1/2+1/20}
.
We reassemble the sum over
(k_{1},k_{2})\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})^{2}

(satisfying
k_{1}+k_{2}=k-1
),
as well as the
partitions of unity in
N(b)
,

N(d)
and
N(r)
in (
11.7
).
We then collapse the weights in the main term back to
\big(\delta_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega(u)=k\\
\varpi\mid u\implies N(\varpi)\leq w\end{subarray}}\big)
,
and obtain an asymptotic expression for
\mathscr{B}_{k,\ell}(N)

for each dyadic value
N
satisfying
N<X^{1/2+1/20}
.
Recall that (
11
) gives an
asymptotic expression
for
\mathscr{B}_{k,\ell}(N)

for each dyadic value
N
satisfying
N\geq X^{1/2+1/20}
.
We combine these two results
in (
11.3
),
and reassemble the partition of unity over
N(n)
.
Note that the reassembly of partitions of unity and the sums over
k_{i}
do not overwhelm
the error terms (one only has losses of
O((\log X)^{10})
say.
Inserting this asymptotic expression
into (
11.3
), and noting that

\sum_{k\geq 0}(-1)^{k}\Big(\mathbf{\delta}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\omega(u)=k\\
\varpi|u\implies N(\varpi)\leq w\end{subarray}}\Big)\mu^{2}(nru)=\mu^{2}(nru)\Big(\mathbf{\delta}_{\varpi|u\implies N(\varpi)\leq w}\Big)\mu(u),

as well as (
11.2
),
we obtain the result.
∎

12.
Combinatorial decompositions

We will use the following combinatorial decomposition.

Lemma 12.1
.

Let
W:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be a smooth function compactly supported in
(0,C)
.
Let
(s(n))_{n\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]}
be a sequence satisfying
|s(n)|\leq 1
and
have support on squarefree
n
satisfying
n\equiv 1\pmod{3}
.
Then
for
2\leq w\leq CX^{1/3}\leq z
we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\varpi\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
N(\varpi)>z\end{subarray}}s(\varpi)W\Big(\frac{N(\varpi)}{X}\Big)=-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\varpi_{1},\varpi_{2}\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
N(\varpi_{1}),N(\varpi_{2})>z\end{subarray}}s(\varpi_{1}\varpi_{2})W\Big(\frac{N(\varpi_{1}\varpi_{2})}{X}\Big)

\displaystyle+\sum_{k\geq 0}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{k!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w<N(\varpi_{1}),\ldots,N(\varpi_{k})\leq z\\
\forall i:\varpi_{i}\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
c\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
k=0\implies c\neq 1\\
\varpi|c\implies N(\varpi)>w\end{subarray}}s(c\varpi_{1}\ldots\varpi_{k})W\Big(\frac{N(c\varpi_{1}\ldots\varpi_{k})}{X}\Big)+O(\sqrt{X}).

Proof.

We assume that
\operatorname{Re}s>1
throughout this proof.
We have

\zeta_{>z}(s):=\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}N(\varpi)>z\\
\varpi\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}\Big(1-\frac{1}{N(\varpi)^{s}}\Big)^{-1},

and

\displaystyle\sum_{L\geq 1}\frac{1}{L}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}N(\varpi)>z\\
\varpi\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{N(\varpi)^{Ls}}=\log\zeta_{>z}(s)=\log(1+(\zeta_{>z}(s)-1))

(12.1)

\displaystyle=(\zeta_{>z}(s)-1)-\frac{1}{2}\cdot(\zeta_{>z}(s)-1)^{2}+\sum_{j\geq 3}\frac{(-1)^{j+1}}{j}\cdot(\zeta_{>z}(s)-1)^{j}.

Furthermore,

\zeta_{>z}(s)=\zeta_{>w}(s)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}w<N(\varpi)\leq z\\
\varpi\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}\Big(1-\frac{1}{N(\varpi)^{s}}\Big),

where

(12.2)

\zeta_{>w}(s):=\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}N(\varpi)>w\\
\varpi\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}\Big(1-\frac{1}{N(\varpi)^{s}}\Big)^{-1}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}c\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
\varpi|c\implies N(\varpi)>w\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{N(c)^{s}}.

The equation (
12.2
) is valid since every
c\equiv 1\pmod{3}
has a unique factorisation

c=\varpi_{1}\ldots\varpi_{k}
with
\varpi_{i}\equiv 1\pmod{3}
for
i=1,\ldots,k
. Expand the product

\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}w<N(\varpi)\leq z\\
\varpi\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}\Big(1-\frac{1}{N(\varpi)^{s}}\Big)=1+\sum_{k\geq 1}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{k!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w<N(\varpi_{1}),\ldots,N(\varpi_{k})\leq z\\
\forall i:\varpi_{i}\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
\varpi_{i}\text{ all distinct}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{N(\varpi_{1}\ldots\varpi_{k})^{s}}.

Therefore

(12.3)

\zeta_{>z}(s)-1=\sum_{k\geq 0}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{k!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w<N(\varpi_{1}),\ldots,N(\varpi_{k})\leq z\\
\forall\varpi_{i}\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
\varpi_{i}\text{ all distinct}\\
c\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
k=0\implies c\neq 1\\
\varpi|c\implies N(\varpi)>w\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{N(c\varpi_{1}\ldots\varpi_{k})^{s}}.

Substitution of (
12.3
) into (
12
) gives

(12.4)

\displaystyle\sum_{L\geq 1}\frac{1}{L}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}N(\varpi)>z\\
\varpi\equiv 1\pmod{3}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{N(\varpi)^{Ls}}=\sum_{k\geq 0}

\displaystyle\frac{(-1)^{k}}{k!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w<N(\varpi_{1}),\ldots,N(\varpi_{k})\leq z\\
\forall\varpi_{i}\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
\varpi_{i}\text{ all distinct}\\
c\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
k=0\implies c\neq 1\\
\varpi|c\implies N(\varpi)>w\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{N(c\varpi_{1}\ldots\varpi_{k})^{s}}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\cdot(\zeta_{>z}(s)-1)^{2}+\sum_{j\geq 3}\frac{(-1)^{j+1}}{j}\cdot(\zeta_{>z}(s)-1)^{j}.

The result follows from a comparison of coefficients. Observe that that the total contribution from terms
N(\varpi)^{k}\leq X
with
k\geq 2
on the left side
of (
12.4
) is
O(\sqrt{X})
.
Since
z>CX^{1/3}
and
W
is compactly supported in
(0,C)
, we see that the contribution from all terms
(\zeta_{>z}(s)-1)^{j}
with
j\geq 3
is zero.
Notice that
s(c\varpi_{1}\ldots\varpi_{k})
is zero if
c\varpi_{1}\ldots\varpi_{k}
is not squarefree by hypothesis,
so we can drop the requirement that the
\varpi_{i}
are all distinct.
∎

13.
Proof of Theorems
1.1
and
1.3

We first record a useful Lemma due to Polymath that classifies
the Type-I, Type-II and Type-III information that occurs in the proof of
our main theorems.

Lemma 13.1
.

[
Pol14
, Lemma 3.1]

Given an integer
n\geq 1
and
\tfrac{1}{10}\leq\sigma<\tfrac{1}{2}
,
let
t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}
be non-negative real numbers such that
t_{1}+\ldots+t_{n}=1
. Then at least one of the following three statement holds:

(Type-I)

There is an
i\in[1,n]
such that
t_{i}\geq\tfrac{1}{2}+\sigma
;

(Type-II)

There is a partition
\{1,\ldots,n\}=S\cup T
such that

\frac{1}{2}-\sigma<\sum_{i\in S}t_{i}\leq\sum_{i\in T}t_{i}<\frac{1}{2}+\sigma;

(Type-III)

There exists distinct
i,j,v\in[1,n]
such that
2\sigma\leq t_{i}\leq t_{j}\leq t_{v}\leq\frac{1}{2}-\sigma
and

t_{i}+t_{j},t_{j}+t_{v},t_{v}+t_{i}\geq\frac{1}{2}+\sigma.

Furthermore, if
\sigma>1/6
, then the Type-III alternative can’t occur.

Proof of Theorems
1.1
and
1.3
.

We first explain some initial manipulations.

Initial reduction

For any rational prime
p\equiv 1\pmod{3}
we have

\frac{S_{p}}{2\sqrt{p}}=\operatorname{Re}\tilde{g}(\varpi),

where
\varpi\in\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
is a prime such that

\varpi\equiv 1\pmod{3}
and

p=\varpi\overline{\varpi}
.
The number of primes
\varpi\equiv 1\pmod{3}
for
which
N(\varpi)
is not prime is
O(\sqrt{X})
. Such primes
are those that lie over rational primes
p\equiv 2\pmod{3}
.
To prove Theorem
1.1
it suffices to estimate
the quantity

\sum_{\varpi\equiv 1\pmod{3}}\tilde{g}(\varpi)W\Big(\frac{N(\varpi)}{X}\Big).

Observe that (
2.2
) implies that

\tilde{g}(\varpi)^{3}=-\frac{\varpi}{|\varpi|}.

Thus

\tilde{g}(\varpi)^{k}=(-1)^{\ell}\Big(\frac{\varpi}{|\varpi|}\Big)^{\ell}\times\begin{cases}\tilde{g}(\varpi)&\text{ if }k\equiv 1\pmod{3}\text{ with }\ell=\frac{k-1}{3}\\
\overline{\tilde{g}(\varpi)}&\text{ if }k\equiv 2\pmod{3}\text{ with }\ell=\frac{k+1}{3}\\
1&\text{ if }k\equiv 0\pmod{3}\text{ with }\ell=\frac{k}{3}\end{cases}.

In particular, Theorem
1.3
with
k\equiv 0\pmod{3}
follows directly from the assumption of the Generalized Riemann Hypothesis.

To establish Theorem
1.3
, it suffices to show that

\sum_{\varpi\equiv 1\pmod{3}}\tilde{g}(\varpi)\Big(\frac{\varpi}{|\varpi|}\Big)^{\ell}W\Big(\frac{N(\varpi)}{X}\Big)=o\Big(\frac{X^{5/6}}{\log X}\Big),

as
X\rightarrow\infty
and uniformly in
0<|\ell|\leq X^{1/100}
.
To prove both Theorem
1.1
and Theorem
1.3
simultaneously it is enough to estimate

\sum_{\varpi\equiv 1\pmod{3}}\tilde{g}(\varpi)\Big(\frac{\varpi}{|\varpi|}\Big)^{\ell}W\Big(\frac{N(\varpi)}{X}\Big),

to a precision better than
o(X^{5/6}/\log X)
. For
c\equiv 1\pmod{3}
define

\tilde{g}_{\ell}(c):=\tilde{g}(c)\Big(\frac{c}{|c|}\Big)^{\ell}.

Let
\varepsilon\in(0,10^{-6})
be fixed.
Let

w:=X^{\varepsilon}\quad\text{and}\quad z:=X^{1/3+\varepsilon}.

By Lemma
12.1
we have

(13.1)

\displaystyle\sum_{\varpi\equiv 1\pmod{3}}\tilde{g}_{\ell}(\varpi)W\Big(\frac{N(\varpi)}{X}\Big)=-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\varpi_{1},\varpi_{2}\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
N(\varpi_{1}),N(\varpi_{2})>z\end{subarray}}\tilde{g}_{\ell}(\varpi_{1}\varpi_{2})W\Big(\frac{N(\varpi_{1}\varpi_{2})}{X}\Big)

(13.2)

\displaystyle+\sum_{k\geq 0}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{k!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\varpi_{1},\ldots,\varpi_{k},c)\in\mathcal{S}(w,z)\end{subarray}}\tilde{g}_{\ell}(\varpi_{1}\ldots\varpi_{k}c)W\Big(\frac{N(\varpi_{1}\ldots\varpi_{k}c)}{X}\Big)+O(\sqrt{X}),

where
\mathcal{S}(w,z)
denotes the set of tuples
(\varpi_{1},\ldots,\varpi_{k},c)
with
k\geq 0
such that

•

\varpi_{1},\ldots,\varpi_{k}
are primes congruent to
1\pmod{3}
(when
k\geq 1
);

•

For all
1\leq i\leq k
we have
w<N(\varpi_{i})\leq z
(when
k\geq 1
);

•

c
is
w
-rough,
c\equiv 1\pmod{3}
, and
k=0\implies c\neq 1
.

When
k=0
, the sum is understood just to be over the variable
c
.

Let
\xi\in(0,10^{-6})
be a small fixed quantity to be decided at a later point in the proof
(it will ultimately depend on
\varepsilon
).

Remark 13.1
.

Uniformity of error terms in
\ell
is not an issue when deploying Type II/III estimates
(i.e. Proposition
7.1
and Proposition
10.1
). This is because (
2.3
) is applied to
\tilde{g}_{\ell}(ab)
,
and the dependence on
\ell
is absorbed into the
coefficients
\bm{\alpha}
and
\bm{\beta}
that satisfy
\|\bm{\alpha}\|_{\infty},\|\bm{\beta}\|_{\infty}\leq 1
.
The dependence on
\ell
issue emanates from the
application of the average Type-I estimate in Proposition
11.1
.

Sum on the right side side of (
13.1
)

We introduce a smooth partition of unity on each of the
N(\varpi_{i})
to evaluate the (Type-II) sum over
N(\varpi_{1}),N(\varpi_{2})>z
.
Thus it is sufficient to estimate

(13.3)

\mathscr{F}_{\ell}(X,P_{1},P_{2};z):=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\varpi_{1},\varpi_{2}\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
N(\varpi_{1}),N(\varpi_{2})>z\end{subarray}}\tilde{g}_{\ell}(\varpi_{1}\varpi_{2})W\Big(\frac{N(\varpi_{1}\varpi_{2})}{X}\Big)V\Big(\frac{N(\varpi_{1})}{P_{1}}\Big)V\Big(\frac{N(\varpi_{2})}{P_{2}}\Big),

for all dyadic partitions
(P_{1},P_{2})
that satisfy
z/2\leq P_{1},P_{2}\leq 2X
and
P_{1}P_{2}\asymp X
. When
z/2\leq\min\{P_{1},P_{2}\}\leq X^{1/2-\xi}

we can apply Proposition
10.1
with
\pi=1
, and
\eta>0
arbitrarily small and fixed by Lemma
6.2

(the only requirement is that
\eta>100\log\log X/\log X
).
Thus there exists
\delta_{0}(\xi,\varepsilon)>0

such that

\displaystyle\mathscr{T}_{\ell}(X,P_{1},P_{2},z)

\displaystyle=\frac{(2\pi)^{2/3}}{3\Gamma(\tfrac{2}{3})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}N(\varpi_{1}),N(\varpi_{2})>z\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu^{2}(\varpi_{1}\varpi_{2})\big(\frac{\varpi_{1}\varpi_{2}}{|\varpi_{1}\varpi_{2}|}\big)^{\ell}}{N(\varpi_{1}\varpi_{2})^{1/6}}V\Big(\frac{N(\varpi_{1})}{P_{1}}\Big)V\Big(\frac{N(\varpi_{2})}{P_{2}}\Big)

(13.4)

\displaystyle\times W\Big(\frac{N(\varpi_{1}\varpi_{2})}{X}\Big)+O_{\xi,\varepsilon}(X^{5/6-\delta_{0}(\xi,\varepsilon)}),\quad\text{when}\quad z/2\leq\min\{P_{1},P_{2}\}\leq X^{1/2-\xi}.

When
X^{1/2-\xi}\leq P_{1},P_{2}\leq X^{1/2+\xi}
, we appeal
to Proposition
7.1
. In particular, the smooth coefficients here are supported on
z=X^{1/3+\varepsilon}>X^{\varepsilon}

-rough integers.
We obtain

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}_{\ell}(X,P_{1},P_{2};z)\ll\frac{X}{(\varepsilon\log X)^{3/2}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\min(P_{1},P_{2})}}

\displaystyle+\frac{X^{5/6}}{(\varepsilon\log X)^{2}},

\displaystyle\text{when}\quad X^{1/2-\xi}\leq P_{1},P_{2}\leq X^{1/2+\xi},

where the implied constant is absolute. We can include a redundant main term that is majorised by the error term i.e.

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}_{\ell}(X,P_{1},P_{2};z)

\displaystyle=\frac{(2\pi)^{2/3}}{3\Gamma(\tfrac{2}{3})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}N(\varpi_{1}),N(\varpi_{2})>z\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu^{2}(\varpi_{1}\varpi_{2})\big(\frac{\varpi_{1}\varpi_{2}}{|\varpi_{1}\varpi_{2}|}\big)^{\ell}}{N(\varpi_{1}\varpi_{2})^{1/6}}V\Big(\frac{N(\varpi_{1})}{P_{1}}\Big)V\Big(\frac{N(\varpi_{2})}{P_{2}}\Big)W\Big(\frac{N(\varpi_{1}\varpi_{2})}{X}\Big)

\displaystyle+O\Big(\frac{X}{(\varepsilon\log X)^{3/2}}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{\min(P_{1},P_{2})}}\Big)+O\Big(\frac{X^{5/6}}{(\varepsilon\log X)^{2}}\Big),

(13.5)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\text{when}\quad X^{1/2-\xi}\leq P_{1},P_{2}\leq X^{1/2+\xi}.

Since
P_{1}P_{2}\asymp X
there are
O(\xi\log X)
choices of
P_{1},P_{2}

in the narrow range
X^{1/2-\xi}\leq P_{1},P_{2}\leq X^{1/2+\xi}
.
Summing (
13
) and (
13
) over all possible dyadic tuples
(P_{1},P_{2})
gives

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\varpi_{1},\varpi_{2}\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
N(\varpi_{1}),N(\varpi_{2})>z\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\tilde{g}_{\ell}(\varpi_{1}\varpi_{2})W\Big(\frac{N(\varpi_{1}\varpi_{2})}{X}\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{(2\pi)^{2/3}}{3\Gamma(\tfrac{2}{3})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\varpi_{1},\varpi_{2}\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
N(\varpi_{1}),N(\varpi_{2})>z\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu^{2}(\varpi_{1}\varpi_{2})\big(\frac{\varpi_{1}\varpi_{2}}{|\varpi_{1}\varpi_{2}|}\big)^{\ell}}{N(\varpi_{1}\varpi_{2})^{1/6}}W\Big(\frac{N(\varpi_{1}\varpi_{2})}{X}\Big)

(13.6)

\displaystyle+O\Big(\frac{X^{3/4+\xi/2}}{(\varepsilon\log X)^{3/2}}\Big)+O\Big(\frac{\xi X^{5/6}}{\varepsilon^{2}\log X}\Big)+O_{\xi,\varepsilon}(X^{5/6-\delta_{1}(\xi,\varepsilon)}),

for any fixed
0<\delta_{1}(\xi,\varepsilon)<\delta_{0}(\xi,\varepsilon)
.

Sum in (
13.2
)

For each
0\leq k\leq 1/\varepsilon
, we analyse the sum

(13.7)

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\varpi_{1},\ldots,\varpi_{k},c)\in\mathcal{S}(w,z)\end{subarray}}\tilde{g}_{\ell}(\varpi_{1}\ldots\varpi_{k}c)W\Big(\frac{N(\varpi_{1}\ldots\varpi_{k}c)}{X}\Big).

We insert a smooth partition of unity in
N(c)
and each
N(\varpi_{i})
for
i=1,\ldots,k
in (
13.7
).
Thus its suffices to
estimate

(13.8)

\displaystyle\mathscr{S}_{\ell}(P_{1},\ldots,P_{k+1}):=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\varpi_{1},\ldots,\varpi_{k},c)\in\mathcal{S}(w,z)\end{subarray}}\tilde{g}_{\ell}(\varpi_{1}\ldots\varpi_{k}c)W\Big(\frac{N(\varpi_{1}\ldots\varpi_{k}c)}{X}\Big)V\Big(\frac{N(c)}{P_{k+1}}\Big)\prod_{i=1}^{k}V\Big(\frac{N(\pi_{i})}{P_{i}}\Big),

for all dyadic partitions
H=(P_{1},\ldots,P_{k+1})
satisfying
P_{1}\ldots P_{k+1}\asymp X
,
w/2\leq P_{i}\leq 2z
for all
i=1,\ldots,k
,
and
P_{k+1}\geq 1/2
. Our goal will be to show that
\mathscr{S}(P_{1},\ldots,P_{k+1})
is asymptotically equal to
(either for individual tuples
(P_{1},\ldots,P_{k+1})
or on average)

\displaystyle\mathscr{M}_{\ell}(P_{1},\ldots,P_{k+1})

\displaystyle=\frac{(2\pi)^{2/3}}{3\Gamma(\tfrac{2}{3})}\sum_{(\varpi_{1},\ldots,\varpi_{k},c)\in\mathcal{S}(w,z)}\frac{\mu^{2}(\varpi_{1}\ldots\varpi_{k}c)\big(\frac{\varpi_{1}\ldots\varpi_{k}c}{|\varpi_{1}\ldots\varpi_{k}c|}\big)^{\ell}}{N(\varpi_{1}\ldots\varpi_{k}c)^{1/6}}W\Big(\frac{N(\varpi_{1}\ldots\varpi_{k}c)}{X}\Big)V\Big(\frac{N(c)}{P_{k+1}}\Big)\prod_{i=1}^{k}V\Big(\frac{N(\varpi_{i})}{P_{i}}\Big).

For a given
(P_{1},\ldots,P_{k+1})
,
let

(13.9)

t_{i}:=\frac{\log P_{i}}{\log(P_{1}\ldots P_{k+1})}\geq 0\quad\text{for}\quad i=1,\ldots,k+1.

We necessarily have

(13.10)

\displaystyle t_{1}+\ldots

\displaystyle+t_{k+1}=1;

(13.11)

\displaystyle\frac{\log w}{\log X}\leq t_{i}\leq\frac{\log z}{\log X}

\displaystyle\quad\text{for}\quad i=1,\ldots,k.

We now apply Lemma
13.1
with choice
\sigma:=1/6-\xi
to decompose the proof into cases.

Narrow Type-III sums

In this case we necessarily have
k\geq 2
, and

(13.12)

\exists\text{ three distinct indices }i,j,\ell\in\{1,\ldots,k+1\}\text{ such that }t_{i},t_{j},t_{\ell}\in(\tfrac{1}{3}-2\xi,\tfrac{1}{3}+\xi).

In particular, either

(1)

\exists
an index
i
such that
t_{i}\in[\tfrac{1}{3},\tfrac{1}{3}+\xi)
, or

(2)

we have
t_{i},t_{j},t_{\ell}\in(\tfrac{1}{3}-2\xi,\tfrac{1}{3})
.

The sum over all dyadic partitions
(P_{1},\ldots,P_{k+1})
for which there exists an index
i
such that
t_{i}\in[\tfrac{1}{3},\tfrac{1}{3}+\xi)
(and two additional indices
j,\ell
such that
t_{j},t_{\ell}\in(\tfrac{1}{3}-2\xi,\tfrac{1}{3}+\xi)
) is

(13.13)

\leq(k+1)!\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X^{1/3}\leq P\leq X^{1/3+\xi}\\
P\text{ dyadic}\end{subarray}}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\|\bm{\alpha}\|_{\infty},\|\bm{\beta}\|_{\infty}\leq 1\\
\bm{\beta}\in\mathcal{C}_{\eta}(P,w)\end{subarray}}\Big|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a,b\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
\pi|a\implies N(\pi)>w\\
\pi\mid b\implies N(\pi)>w\\
N(a)\asymp X/P,N(b)\asymp P\end{subarray}}\alpha_{a}\beta_{b}\tilde{g}_{\ell}(ab)W\Big(\frac{N(ab)}{X}\Big)\Big|,

where
\eta>0
is arbitrarily small and fixed by Lemma
6.2
(the only requirement is that
\eta>100\log\log X/(\varepsilon\log X)
).
Notice that the factor
(k+1)!=k!\cdot(k+1)
arises from the fact that there are
k+1
ways of choosing the first index
i
for which
P_{i}\in[\tfrac{1}{3},\tfrac{1}{3}+\xi)
(and this index becomes our
P
) and there are
k!
ways of representing
a
as a product of the remaining
k
variables.
Application of Proposition
7.1
shows that (
13.13
) is

\displaystyle\ll(k+1)!\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X^{1/3}\leq P\leq X^{1/3+\xi}\\
P\text{ dyadic}\end{subarray}}\Big(\frac{1}{(\varepsilon\log X)^{3/2}}\cdot\frac{X}{\sqrt{P}}+\frac{X^{5/6}}{(\varepsilon\log X)^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\ll(k+1)!\Big(\frac{X^{5/6}}{(\varepsilon\log X)^{3/2}}+\frac{\xi X^{5/6}}{\varepsilon^{2}\log X}\Big),

where the implied constants are absolute.

We now handle the remaining case in which
t_{i},t_{j},t_{\ell}\in(\tfrac{1}{3}-2\xi,\tfrac{1}{3})
. We group together two variables coming from
the indices
i
and
j
say.
We sum over all dyadic partitions
(P_{1},\ldots,P_{k+1})
for which
t_{i},t_{j},t_{\ell}\in(\tfrac{1}{3}-2\xi,\tfrac{1}{3})
.
This sum is

(13.14)

\leq(k+1)!\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X^{2/3-4\xi}\leq U\leq X^{2/3}\\
U\text{ dyadic}\end{subarray}}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\|\bm{\alpha}\|_{\infty},\|\bm{\beta}\|_{\infty}\leq 1\\
\bm{\beta}\in\mathcal{C}_{\eta}(X/U,w)\end{subarray}}\Big|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a,b\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
\pi|a\implies N(\pi)>w\\
\pi\mid b\implies N(\pi)>w\\
N(a)\asymp U,N(b)\asymp X/U\end{subarray}}\alpha_{a}\beta_{b}\tilde{g}_{\ell}(ab)W\Big(\frac{N(ab)}{X}\Big)\Big|,

where
\eta>0
is arbitrarily small and fixed by Lemma
6.2
(the only requirement is that
\eta>100\log\log X/(\varepsilon\log X)
).
The factor
(k+1)!=(k-1)!\cdot 2\binom{k+1}{2}
arises from the fact that there are
2\binom{k+1}{2}
ordered choices
of
i
and
j
such that
t_{i},t_{j}\in(\tfrac{1}{3}-2\xi,\tfrac{1}{3})
, and

(k-1)!
ways of representing
a
as the product of the remaining
k-1
variables.
Applying Proposition
7.1
and arguing in a similar way to the above shows that (
13.14
) is

\ll(k+1)!\Big(\frac{X^{5/6}}{(\varepsilon\log X)^{3/2}}+\frac{\xi X^{5/6}}{\varepsilon^{2}\log X}\Big),

where the implied constant is absolute.

Combining the two cases we conclude that

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(P_{1},\ldots,P_{k+1})\\
\eqref{eq:condi1}\text{ holds}\end{subarray}}\mathscr{S}(P_{1},\ldots,P_{k+1})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(P_{1},\ldots,P_{k+1})\\
\eqref{eq:condi1}\text{ holds}\end{subarray}}\mathscr{M}(P_{1},\ldots,P_{k+1})+O\Big(\frac{(k+1)!X^{5/6}}{(\varepsilon\log X)^{3/2}}\Big)

\displaystyle+O\Big(\frac{(k+1)!\xi X^{5/6}}{\varepsilon^{2}\log X}\Big).

Notice that the main term is absorbed by the error term in this case.

Narrow Type-II sums

In this case we necessarily have
k\geq 1
,
and

(13.15)

\exists\text{ a partition $S\cup T=\{1,\ldots,k+1\}$ such that }\tfrac{1}{2}-\xi<\sum_{i\in S}t_{i}\leq\sum_{j\in T}t_{j}<\tfrac{1}{2}+\xi.

The contribution of all such
(P_{1},\ldots,P_{k+1})
is

(13.16)

\leq(k+1)!\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X^{1/2-\xi}\leq U\leq X^{1/2}\\
U\text{ dyadic}\end{subarray}}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\|\bm{\alpha}\|_{\infty},\|\bm{\beta}\|_{\infty}\leq 1\\
\bm{\beta}\in\mathcal{C}_{\eta}(U,w)\end{subarray}}\Big|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a,b\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
\pi|a\implies N(\pi)>w\\
\pi\mid b\implies N(\pi)>w\\
N(a)\asymp X/U,N(b)\asymp U\end{subarray}}\alpha_{a}\beta_{b}\tilde{g}_{\ell}(ab)W\Big(\frac{N(ab)}{X}\Big)\Big|,

where
\eta>0
is arbitrarily small and fixed by Lemma
6.2
(the only requirement is that
\eta>100\log\log X/(\varepsilon\log X)
).
The term
(k+1)!
arises from the fact that for each
1\leq i\leq k
, there are
i!\binom{k+1}{i}
ordered choices for the set
S
containing
i
elements,
and there are
(k+1-i)!
ways of representing
b
as a product of the remaining
k+1-i
variables indicated by the set
T
. Applying Proposition
7.1
, we see that (
13.16
) is

\displaystyle\ll(k+1)!\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X^{1/2-\xi}\leq U\leq X^{1/2}\\
U\text{ dyadic}\end{subarray}}\Big(\frac{1}{(\varepsilon\log X)^{3/2}}\cdot\frac{X}{\sqrt{U}}+\frac{X^{5/6}}{(\varepsilon\log X)^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\ll(k+1)!\Big(\frac{X^{3/4+\xi/2}}{(\varepsilon\log X)^{3/2}}+\frac{\xi X^{5/6}}{\varepsilon^{2}\log X}\Big),

where the implied constants are absolute.

In particular,

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(P_{1},\ldots,P_{k+1})\\
\eqref{eq:condi2}\text{ holds}\end{subarray}}\mathscr{S}(P_{1},\ldots,P_{k+1})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(P_{1},\ldots,P_{k+1})\\
\eqref{eq:condi2}\text{ holds}\end{subarray}}\mathscr{M}(P_{1},\ldots,P_{k+1})+O\Big(\frac{(k+1)!X^{3/4+\xi/2}}{(\varepsilon\log X)^{3/2}}\Big)

\displaystyle+O\Big(\frac{(k+1)!\xi X^{5/6}}{\varepsilon^{2}\log X}\Big),

where the main term is absorbed by the error term.

Remaining ranges

We now consider all of the remaining dyadic partitions
(P_{1},\ldots,P_{k+1})
one by one. For each remaining tuple
(P_{1},\ldots,P_{k+1})
we will show that

(13.17)

\mathscr{S}_{\ell}(P_{1},\ldots,P_{k+1})=\mathscr{M}_{\ell}(P_{1},\ldots,P_{k+1})+O_{A,\xi,\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{X^{5/6}}{\log^{A}X}\Big),

for any given
A>10
(depending on
\varepsilon>0
). Recall that
k\leq 1/\varepsilon
. Since there are at most
(\log X)^{k}
dyadic partitions
(P_{1},\ldots,P_{k+1})

satisfying
P_{1}\cdots P_{k+1}\asymp X
, we can sum over
the error term in (
13.17
) without overwhelming the main term.
Notice that each of the remaining configurations of
(P_{1},\ldots,P_{k+1})
now fall into either of two cases:

(1)

\exists i\in\{1,\ldots,k+1\}
such that
t_{i}\geq\tfrac{2}{3}-\xi
;

(2)

Or
\exists
a partition
S\cup T=\{1,\ldots,k+1\}
such that

(13.18)

\frac{1}{3}+\xi\leq\sum_{i\in S}t_{i}\leq\frac{1}{2}-\xi\leq\frac{1}{2}+\xi\leq\sum_{j\in T}t_{j}\leq\frac{2}{3}-\xi.

If
\exists i\in\{1,\ldots,k+1\}
such that
t_{i}\geq\tfrac{2}{3}-\xi
, then
i=k+1
by (
13.11
). This corresponds to the
c
variable
appearing in
\mathscr{S}_{\ell}(P_{1},\ldots,P_{k+1})
in (
13.8
).
After applying Proposition
11.1
(average Type-I estimate) we obtain (
13.17
) uniformly in
|\ell|\leq X^{1/100}
.
If the second alternative holds,
then (
13.17
) follows from Proposition
10.1
(broad Type-II estimate).

Assembly

Summing over all dyadic partitions
(P_{1},\ldots,P_{k+1})
we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{0\leq k\leq 1/\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\frac{(-1)^{k}}{k!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\varpi_{1},\ldots,\varpi_{k},c)\in\mathcal{S}(w,z)\end{subarray}}\tilde{g}_{\ell}(\varpi_{1}\ldots\varpi_{k}c)W\Big(\frac{N(\varpi_{1}\ldots\varpi_{k}c)}{X}\Big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{0\leq k\leq 1/\varepsilon}\frac{(-1)^{k}}{k!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\varpi_{1},\ldots,\varpi_{k},c)\in\mathcal{S}(w,z)\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu^{2}(\varpi_{1}\ldots\varpi_{k}c)\big(\frac{\varpi_{1}\ldots\varpi_{k}c}{|\varpi_{1}\ldots\varpi_{k}c|}\big)^{\ell}}{N(\varpi_{1}\ldots\varpi_{k}c)^{1/6}}W\Big(\frac{N(\varpi_{1}\ldots\varpi_{k}c)}{X}\Big)

(13.19)

\displaystyle+O\Big(\frac{\xi X^{5/6}}{\varepsilon^{4}\log X}\Big)+O\Big(\frac{X^{5/6}}{\varepsilon^{7/2}(\log X)^{3/2}}\Big)+O\Big(\frac{X^{3/4+\xi/2}}{\varepsilon^{7/2}(\log X)^{3/2}}\Big)+O_{A,\xi,\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{X^{5/6}}{\log^{A-\varepsilon^{-1}}X}\Big),

uniformly in
|\ell|\leq X^{1/100}
. We now drop the third error term in (
13
) because it is majorised by the second one.
Combining (
13
) and (
13
) in (
13.1
)–(
13.2
), and then applying Lemma
12.1
(in the reverse direction, and to the symbol
\mu^{2}(\cdot)(\cdot\cdot)^{\ell}
) gives

\displaystyle\sum_{\varpi\equiv 1\pmod{3}}

\displaystyle\tilde{g}_{\ell}(\varpi)W\Big(\frac{N(\varpi)}{X}\Big)=\frac{(2\pi)^{2/3}}{3\Gamma(\tfrac{2}{3})}\sum_{\varpi\equiv 1\pmod{3}}\frac{\big(\frac{\varpi}{|\varpi|}\big)^{\ell}}{N(\varpi)^{1/6}}W\Big(\frac{N(\varpi)}{X}\Big)

(13.20)

\displaystyle+O\Big(\frac{\xi X^{5/6}}{\varepsilon^{4}\log X}\Big)+O\Big(\frac{X^{5/6}}{\varepsilon^{7/2}(\log X)^{3/2}}\Big)+O_{A,\xi,\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{X^{5/6}}{\log^{A-\varepsilon^{-1}}X}\Big).+O_{\xi,\varepsilon}(X^{5/6-\delta_{1}(\xi,\varepsilon)}),

uniformly in
|\ell|\leq X^{1/100}
.
After choosing
\xi=\varepsilon^{1000}
and
A=\varepsilon^{-1000}
(say),
the error terms in (
13
) are
O\big(\frac{\varepsilon X^{5/6}}{\log X}\big)
as
X\rightarrow\infty
.
We conclude by noticing that

\sum_{\varpi\equiv 1\pmod{3}}\frac{1}{N(\varpi)^{1/6}}W\Big(\frac{N(\varpi)}{X}\Big)\sim\int_{0}^{\infty}W(x)x^{-1/6}dx\cdot\frac{X^{5/6}}{\log X}\quad\text{as}\quad X\rightarrow\infty,

and for
\ell\neq 0
,

\sum_{\varpi\equiv 1\pmod{3}}\frac{\big(\frac{\varpi}{|\varpi|}\big)^{\ell}}{N(\varpi)^{1/6}}W\Big(\frac{N(\varpi)}{X}\Big)=o\Big(\frac{X^{5/6}}{\log X}\Big)\quad\text{as}\quad X\rightarrow\infty,

uniformly in
|\ell|\leq X^{1/100}
. This proves Theorem
1.1
and Theorem
1.3
.
∎

14.
Proof of Theorem
1.2

Proof of Theorem
1.2
.

We expand
f
in a Fourier series

f(x)=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\widehat{f}(k)e(kx).

For
p\equiv 1\pmod{3}
,

f(\theta_{p})=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\widehat{f}(k)e(k\theta_{p})=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\widehat{f}(k)\tilde{g}(\varpi)^{k},

where
\varpi
is a prime in
\mathbb{Z}[\omega]
such that

p=\varpi\overline{\varpi}
. Therefore

\sum_{p\equiv 1\pmod{3}}f(\theta_{p})W\Big(\frac{p}{X}\Big)

is equal to

\widehat{f}(0)\sum_{p\equiv 1\pmod{3}}W\Big(\frac{p}{X}\Big)+\sum_{0<|k|\leq X^{1/100}}\widehat{f}(k)\Big(\sum_{\varpi\equiv 1\pmod{3}}\tilde{g}(\varpi)^{k}W\Big(\frac{N(\varpi)}{X}\Big)+O(\sqrt{X})\Big)+O_{A}(X^{-A})

for any given
A>10
. We now appeal to Theorem
1.1
and Theorem
1.3
to see that the sum over
k\neq 0
is equal to

(\widehat{f}(1)+\widehat{f}(-1))\cdot\frac{(2\pi)^{2/3}}{3\Gamma(\tfrac{2}{3})}\cdot\int_{0}^{\infty}W(x)x^{-1/6}dx\cdot\frac{X^{5/6}}{\log X}+o\Big(\frac{X^{5/6}}{\log X}\Big),

as claimed.
∎

We note that,

\displaystyle\langle\{(j,k_{j}(E)):1\leq j\leq 27\}\rangle

\displaystyle\simeq C_{2}^{12}

\displaystyle\langle\{(j,k_{j}(P)):1\leq j\leq 27\}\rangle

\displaystyle\simeq C_{3}^{9}

\displaystyle\langle\{(j,k_{j}(T)):1\leq j\leq 27\}\rangle

\displaystyle\simeq C_{3}^{8}.

These isomorphisms are easily seen from the table by following the cycle structure.
The exponents
12
in
C_{2}^{12}
be explained by noticing that the forms
j=1,17,26
are invariant under
E
and
all the other elements are of order two, giving us
\frac{27-3}{2}=12
generators.
Likewise the exponent
8
in
C_{3}^{8}
can be explained by noticing that the forms with
j=1,2,3
are invariant
and there are
\frac{27-3}{3}=8
remaining generators all of order
3
. Finally the exponent
9
appears in the
case of
k_{j}(P)
because no forms is left invariant by
P
and
P
is of order three.
